# Comsky Books: batch, serial/IMEI, label printing and scanning (architecture and spec)

> **Status:** design only. Nothing here is built and no source file was changed. **Second pass 2026-10-23:** an adversarial review (an inventory, accounting and sync lens and a hardware, UX and India-compliance lens) raised 42 findings (1 blocker, 22 major, 19 minor); each was re-checked against the code and the primary pages, and **all 42 reproduced in substance and were applied (seven with a fix adapted from the reviewer's wording); three carried a sub-claim that did not reproduce or needed correcting (23, 26, 32); none was rejected outright** (section 20 is the log). **Four things in this document change what the owner may have read in the first draft:** the within-day order of vouchers is decided per unit and a count is the close of its date (4.2, 4.3); `Specific` costing is no longer the Mobile preset's default (4.5); CF-9a has been written in the worktree, so the shared stock key widens it rather than asking CF to change it first (Appendix R); and the label half of the headline is conditional on a one-day printed spike (13.6). Written 2026-10-23 against `main` at `7074445` (wave 3 committed as `2cee555` and deployed; the MCP server committed, not deployed) and, read-only, the worktree `/Users/Akshay/tallyreplica/.wt-cf` (branch `cf-back-nav`, `f446674`, "WIP checkpoint 2 ... unreviewed": custom fields, reporting by field, stock by field and the Back control).
> This is the build contract for the owner's request of 2026-10-22: *"batch tracking of Tally is weak. Busy's customers appreciate its batch management. Batches of items are tracked in businesses like mobile (IMEI is tracked), for example. Also label printing and scanning is required, in which Tally is again weak. Build these features well."*
> **Method.** The four research documents of this workstream were read in full (`docs/research/batch-serial-tracking.md`, `batch-serial-code-audit.md`, `tracking-verticals-india.md`, `labels-and-scanning.md`, cited below as **[R-bst]**, **[R-aud]**, **[R-vert]**, **[R-lab]** with their section numbers), with the custom-fields spec of the worktree (its decisions table, the addendum AD.3 "stock by field", the slice table and the release order, sections 9.3, 16 and 17), the Back spec's header and rules B5 and B12, and `docs/research/wave-a2-plan.md` (the report grid plan). The code they cite was re-read where a decision here depends on it: `src/core/inventory.ts`, `openingStock.ts`, `valuation.ts` (header), `src/keys.ts`, `src/types.ts` (the stock, line and voucher types), `src/ui/VoucherScreen.tsx` (the item walk, `ItemRow`, `validInvLines`, the Alt+B opener, `BatchPrompt`, the negative-stock warning, the rail), `src/core/shortcutRegistry.ts`, `src/data/db.ts`, `src/sync/sync.ts` (`isKnownTable`), `backend/src/{numbering,conflict}.js`, `electron/{exportBridge,preload}.cjs`, `src/core/qr.ts`, the `scripts/` layout (assert runner, mutation runner, shots harness, perf). The worktree's `.wt-cf` engine was checked twice on 2026-10-23. **At the first check CF-9a was not written; by the review pass it was**, in the worktree's **uncommitted, unreviewed working tree** on top of `f446674` (`src/core/variantCore.ts`, `src/core/stockByField.ts` and the `inventory.ts` edits, file times 06:33 to 06:40): `StockPosition.byVariant: Map<VariantKey, { qty, byGodown }>`, `ValuationOpts.variantOf`, `StockMove.variants` and `.parts`, the `countPart` merge in `stockMovements`, and `countDeltas` in `variantCore.ts` (`VARIANT_SEP` is U+0001). So **the window in which the shared stock key of section 2 could be agreed before CF-9a was written has closed; Appendix R's fallback is now the plan of record** (T-0b widens the shipped shape; it does not ask CF to change it), and T-0b stays gated on CF-9a being committed, reviewed and green on a rebased `main` (15.3).
> **Web research (the first pass made three small fetches to settle facts the design leans on; the review pass added the five primary reads named below; everything else is carried from the four research documents with their own tags).** Electron 31 `webContents.print` options [W]: the units of the `pageSize` object are **not stated**, `margins` are in pixels, `silent` plus `deviceName` select the printer, and the page does not say whether a CSS `@page` size is honoured by `print()` (only `printToPDF` has `preferCSSPageSize`) (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/electron/electron/v31.4.0/docs/api/web-contents.md). GS1 fixed-length application identifiers [Ws]: the old General Specifications table lists AI 00, 01, 02, 11 to 17, 20 and 31 to 36 as fixed length (no separator after them), AI 10 and 21 as variable with a separator unless last, and GS1 has since renamed "predefined length" to "fixed length" (search summary of a vendor copy and GS1 change notices; the current General Specifications themselves were not read). Legal Metrology Rule 7 [Ws, first pass]: a commentary gave a 1 mm floor and said the numeral heights were in separate tables; the bands could not be confirmed then and the first draft shipped an empty table. **The review pass read the primary text [W]** (the Department of Consumer Affairs' consolidated book of the Rules with all amendments, https://consumeraffairs.gov.in/public/upload/admin/cmsfiles/whatsnews/Book_on_Legal_Metrology_Packaged_Commodities_Rules,2011_with_all_amendments_whatsnews.pdf): **Rule 7(2) as substituted by GSR 629(E) of 23 June 2017 (in force 1 January 2018) puts the height of any numeral AND letter into Table-I by the area of the principal display panel**, and the "1 mm for letters" sentence is the superseded old 7(3); section 13.8 now ships the five bands. **Other primary reads of the review pass [W]:** the Drugs Rules 1945 as consolidated by CDSCO (Rules 65(3) and 94(2); https://cdsco.gov.in/opencms/resources/UploadCDSCOWeb/2022/drug_rules/Drugs%20Rules,%201945%20(1).pdf), the FSSAI Labelling and Display Regulations compendium (regulations 5(10) and 8; Version-II of 29 December 2021, https://fssai.gov.in/upload/uploadfiles/files/Compendium_Labelling_Display_04_01_2022.pdf), GS1 Sweden's size guide for the EAN-13 minimums (https://gs1.se/en/guides/how-to-guides/size-guide/) and ICAI's Expert Advisory Committee opinion that quotes AS 2 paragraphs 14 and 15 (https://eacopinion.icai.org/html/20/27.htm).
> **Evidence tags.** **[C file:symbol]** read in this repository (symbols are stable; line numbers are of `7074445` and drift). **[R-xxx §n]** stated in a research document of this workstream, which tags its own source. **[W]** a page fetched this pass. **[Ws]** a search summary only. **[J]** my judgement. **[unverified]** could not be checked. **[P]** a probe by the audit [R-aud Appendix A]. External facts are paraphrased.
> **What this document does not do:** run any gate (`npm run assert`, `shots`, `build`, `perf` belong to the trees that other work is gating), edit any file but itself, touch the CF worktree, or settle what needs a human (section 17 lists those, each with the default taken so work can proceed). **What I could not verify, plainly (section 18 is the full list):** there was no scanner, label printer or phone; no live Busy, Marg or TallyPrime; no Postgres; Electron 31 was not run; whether Dexie 4 lets an older tab open a newer database is read from its source (`VersionError` is retried with no version, `node_modules/dexie/dist/dexie.js`) and was not exercised in a browser.

## 0. The decisions, in one table

Each can be vetoed; the default if the owner is silent is the one written here. "Vetoable" marks the ones where I expect the owner to have a view. Section numbers say where the decision is worked out.

| # | Decision | Why | Vetoable |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | **ONE stock key.** A stock **cell** is `(godown, batch, variant)`; each part is optional; **a serial is a unit identity under a cell, never a fourth key part.** `byGodown`, the legacy `byBatch` and CF-9's `byVariant` stay as **projections** of one map, `StockPosition.cells`, which an engine computes **only when the caller passes a resolver** (as CF-9 designed for `variantOf`), so the 23 existing `stockPositions` call sites stay deep-equal. **CF-9a has since been written (variants only, uncommitted in `.wt-cf`); T-0b widens it as Appendix R's fallback describes and leaves `byVariant` exactly as CF shipped it.** A cell key is **length-prefixed**, never joined by a separator (CF's own variant key already contains U+0001) | a batch and a colour can both be tracked on one item (a shade and its lot); three parallel maps give no `(godown, batch, variant)`; the count-group repair CF-9 already found for variants is exactly the repair batch counts need (2, 4.2) | yes (the CF lane's call) |
| T2 | **Tracking is a per-item mode** `StockItem.track.mode` = `none` / `batch` / `serial` / `batchSerial`, behind a company switch `CompanyFeatures.tracking`. Both are absent for every existing company and item. A legacy `batches: true` item keeps today's `Alt+B` box and every existing figure until its owner upgrades it | "nothing chosen is the app as it was", asserted by identity (CLAUDE.md Phase 27) (2.2, 3.1) | no |
| T3 | **Serials live on the voucher line**, `InventoryLine.serials?: SerialRow[]`; the unit state is a **derived, lazy, rebuildable index**; **no serials table, no Dexie bump, no multi-entry index** | a field on the line rides undo, cancel, sync, backup, restore, the Mismatch Report and REST for free; a table redoes every one of about twelve mechanisms and costs 0.7 GB per million rows [R-aud 3.9, risk 1] (3.2, 4.3) | no |
| T4 | **Batch identity is `(item, batch number, printed MRP when the item keeps MRP per batch)`.** The batch's attributes are **snapshots on the inward lines**; the batch master is derived from the lines. The only stored batch data is an **overlay on the stock item document** (`StockItem.batchRecs`): status (active, hold, recalled, quarantine), a corrected expiry or MRP, a code, notes. The server keeps the overlay against a stale write (**hold wins**). **`mrpAt` and `serialKind` are locked once the item has a line** (they define identity; a change would re-key all history) | a batch is created by receiving it, so its facts already live on documents that never conflict; a recall must not be erasable by a stale item save (3.4, 10.4) | yes (MRP in the identity) |
| T5 | **A unit's status is derived, never typed:** from the voucher that last moved it and the **kind of godown** it sits in (`Godown.kind`: normal, transit, service, quarantine, jobwork). In stock, in transit, with service centre, held (returned, awaiting disposition), sold, returned to supplier ("rejected"), scrapped, consumed, pending identity. No event log, no status voucher | the app's engines are pure functions over the voucher log; every state the owner listed is a place or a leaving voucher (4.3) | yes |
| T6 | **Costing stays per item for batch and variant** (CF24's pooled stance; a batch's own cost is a report). **A serial item may use a new method `Specific`** (each unit at its own cost, resolved by the unit chain, no lot array, O(units moved)); FIFO and LIFO are **not offered** for serial items and are **coerced to Avg. Cost** if a writer sets them. **`Specific` is an opt-in with an AS 2 caution line and an explicit confirmation, and it is NOT the Mobile preset's default**: AS 2 para 15 calls specific identification inappropriate for large numbers of interchangeable items, and one handset model is exactly that. **Per-unit cost is kept as data in every serial item** (`SerialRow.cost`; the Unit Profit report says "books use Avg. Cost: difference X"), which is what a margin-scheme record needs. `Specific` is offered as a sensible default only where units are genuinely unique (jewellery pieces, segregated project stock), and the accountant decides | 125,000 one-unit FIFO lots took 16 to 24 s in `stockPositions` [P]; AS 2 paras 14 and 15 [W]; a margin scheme and a jeweller need per-unit cost [R-vert 5.3] (4.5) | yes (the default) |
| T7 | **Allocation:** FEFO is the default for an item that asks an expiry, FIFO (oldest received) otherwise, `manual` is today's behaviour; a scan can also name the unit or batch. **A proposal is never silent**: the chosen batch, expiry and MRP stay visible on the line; the proposal is pre-selected and Enter accepts it; a short batch rolls into the next with a visible split (4.4, 6) | the leaders pick by hand; Odoo proposes by removal date [R-bst 2.5, 6.4] | no |
| T8 | **The rules are ONE pure function** over the voucher log (`core/trackRules.ts`: the rules R1 to R31 of section 5) called from four places: the entry screen at Accept, the **write funnel** (so Bulk Entry, imports, Mark Optional and every writer is covered), the Data Check, and the post-pull scan. Severities: block / block with a reasoned override by right / warn / inform. An override is stored **on the voucher** with a reason (`Voucher.trackOverrides`). **The funnel's guarantee is per open window** (the app has no cross-window data coordination; Accept and every bulk writer first check the outbox for another window's writes and reload, 10.1) | the leaders are permissive by default; being strict with a named override is the cheap way to be better [R-bst 7] (5) | yes (the defaults per rule) |
| T9 | **Entry:** the allocation panel opens **after Quantity, only for a tracked item**; Rate follows it exactly as Rate follows Quantity today. The frozen walk of every other row is asserted by identity (a recorded key table). A tracked row shows a **chip** (batch, expiry, MRP; or "12 units") in its name cell, always visible; one grid row stays one `InventoryLine` (a split across batches inserts rows directly beneath) (6) | the owner's rule: never change what a key means, only what a field accepts; a carried value must always be visible [CLAUDE.md Phase 20 to 21] | no |
| T10 | **For a serial item the quantity IS the number of units** (an outward line cannot be saved otherwise). A **purchase or opening** line may save with fewer identities than its quantity ("pending identities", listed and unsaleable, dated slots that follow a godown transfer and are adopted only by a sale on or after their date, 4.3); an outward line may not (5, 6.5). **A serial line's quantity is capped at 5,000 whatever any setting says** (a 15-digit IMEI typed into Quantity is refused, not booked as 356 trillion pending units) | a carton reaches the counter before it is scanned; Odoo's refusal to validate a receipt is wrong for a shop that must book the bill today [R-bst 6.3] | no |
| T11 | **IMEI:** 15 digits, Luhn check as a **warning** never a block, a second identifier (IMEI 2) held in the same unique space, a TAC "different model?" prompt, a range tool that **steps the 14-digit body and recomputes the check digit**. **An IMEI is unique across the company; any other serial is unique per item.** The key space comes from **the line's own `serialKind` snapshot**, never from the item (so the server, which holds no item rows, derives the same key). **A dual-SIM unit is one unit with two identifiers**: an outward row always stores the **primary** id (a scanned IMEI 2 is rewritten to IMEI 1 on entry) and the server groups by a canonical `uid`, so IMEI 1 sold on one device and IMEI 2 on another is still one phone sold twice (3.3, 10.2) | consecutive units are not consecutive numbers [R-bst 3.1.1]; a unit moved between two items (a storage-size conversion) must stay one unit (3.3, 4.3) | no |
| T12 | **Counts by cell reuse CF-9's `countPart` flag and the merge in `stockMovements`;** `countDeltas` is generalised to `countSpread` over an opaque cell key. A batch count made as one line per batch (today's 12 becomes 7) stops being possible for a tracked item; **old counts are history, read as they always were, and listed by the Data Check**. **A count of a tracked item is the close of its date**: its lines carry `InventoryLine.eod`, and `stockMovements` applies them after every other movement of that date (4.2, 4.7, 7.6) | one mechanism, one flag, one older-reader caveat | no |
| T13 | **Transfers mirror the cell and its units** (the receiving line starts from the source line, CF's rule for a variant, widened); **a return starts from the sale** (a credit note names units sold to that party; a batch return defaults to the original batch); **no new batch on a sale** (7) | the fraud and error patterns of [R-bst 3.1.5, 7] | no |
| T14 | **A minimal Delivery Note / Receipt Note link:** `InventoryLine.billsNote` marks an invoice line that bills units the note already moved; `stockMovements` skips such a line (billing only). Built in T-3, optional, off until used | without it a unit delivered and then invoiced is issued twice [R-aud 3.2.3, R-bst 7.3 R15] | yes (build or defer) |
| T15 | **Manufacturing:** component lines are allocated (FEFO proposal), the output gets an **auto-named batch** (mfg date = voucher date, expiry = mfg + shelf life) or generated serials; **genealogy is the voucher** (every output of a manufacturing voucher traces to every component cell of it; certain for a single output, "possible" for several). **Job work** is a godown of kind `jobwork` plus the same voucher; no chain is built (7.8) | the job-work chain does not exist [R-aud 3.2.4]; the voucher is the honest unit of lineage | yes |
| T16 | **GST and e-invoice:** one invoice line per item and cell with the unit list **on a printed annexure** (more than 6 units); `BchDtls` only for a 3 to 20 character name and an `ExpDt` only for 2010 to 2029, **with a visible note when left out**; **`WrDt` is not sent** (its meaning is not confirmed); `PrdSlNo` only on the optional one-line-per-unit expansion below the 1,000-line cap, IMEI 2 as an attribute; `Barcde` from the item's primary code (8) | [R-vert 3.2] | no |
| T17 | **Reports:** 14 Reports Center rows over 7 report screens (and a drill screen and two entry screens besides), each a **pure engine plus a thin screen**, each tied to the figure it stands beside to the paisa (9) | the grid is not built; a report written now is bespoke and re-ported later [R-aud 3.16] | no |
| T18 | **Two devices selling one IMEI:** the honest promise is *one window cannot, an offline device is caught at sync and never silently renumbered or refused, and **(only with slice T-9b built)** two online devices are stopped by a server check at Accept*. **Detection (mandatory, slice T-9a) = a server table `unit_events` written inside `/sync/push`'s tenant lock from the STORED payload, a new mismatch kind `duplicate-serial` with its own row cap, a screen that resolves it.** The reserve at Accept (T-9b) is the second, optional slice, and the near-hard promise belongs to it alone. The state machine is **one shared file, `backend/src/unitChain.js`**, imported by the app (the `passwordPolicy` precedent) so the client and the server cannot disagree (10) | [R-aud 3.10, 5 risk 2]; Odoo's and SAP's controls assume one database | yes (hard block vs flag offline) |
| T19 | **An older client must not erase serial or batch data:** capability marker `trk1` beside CF's `udf2`, the **one carry function with two bags** (CF 9.3), `keep` extended with `serials`, `serialKind`, `eod`, `mrp`, `salePrice`, `billsNote`, `missing`; `CAP_KEPT` for the item keys; the header array `trackOverrides` is **unioned, never spread**; **backup schema 4**; the editor refuses to switch tracking on in a synced company until the pull answer states `trk1` **and while any device seen in the last 30 days lacks it** (`sync_device.caps`, 10.5); the push **and the pull page** are size-capped, not only count-capped (10.6). **An old build cannot be forced to comply** (it saves a tracked line with no unit identity): the layer detects what it can (a new build's post-pull scan, a Data Check kind) and says so plainly | a dropped serial list frees IMEIs silently [R-aud risk 18] | no |
| T20 | **No Dexie bump in T-0 to T-6.** The only candidate is T-7's synced `labelTemplates` table for custom label designs (an additive `version(9)`, owner veto, fallback: device-local templates with file export and import). A bump is tolerated by an older tab (Dexie 4 retries `VersionError` without a version) but costs about twelve plumbing sites [R-aud 3.9] (3.7) | the library of fixed templates is code, and printer profiles are device facts | yes |
| T21 | **Labels are data plus one layout engine with three renderers** (SVG for preview and browser print, a 1-bit bitmap at the printer's own dots, native TSPL / ZPL). Bars are drawn in **whole printer dots**, computed from the printer's **real dpi** (203, 300 or 600: `dots = round(mm x dpi / 25.4)`, not the manuals' rounded 8, 12 and 24 per mm); the printer **profile** (language, dpi, width, offset) is separate from the **template** (millimetres). A library of **14 fixed templates (12, and 2 offered only to a packer)** ships first; a constrained designer second (13) | [R-lab 4.3, 1.7] | no |
| T22 | **Barcodes are written in pure TypeScript** under `src/core/barcode/` (EAN-13/8, UPC-A, Code 128, Code 39, ITF-14, GS1-128 strings, a Data Matrix in the optional T-6d; QR already exists). **No runtime dependency.** Tested against an **independent decoder written for the test** and **module strings recorded once from bwip-js by an offline script** (the `viz-tokens.record.mjs` precedent), including all 107 Code 128 values | [R-lab 1.8 and 1.9]: the first experiment exercised only 29 of 107 rows | yes (Data Matrix: write or lazy-load) |
| T23 | **Print paths, in order:** the browser dialog (A4 sheet grid; roll through an injected `@page`), Electron driver print to a **named, listed printer**, Electron raw **TSPL / ZPL** through a validated IPC bridge in the style of `exportBridge.cjs`, then WebUSB / Web Serial after TLS. A network printer is added only through a **main-process dialog** and then referred to by id; the renderer never names a host, a path or a command (13.6) | the renderer is untrusted (Phase 29 rule) | no |
| T24 | **Compliance is warnings, never a guarantee.** The MRP wording is locked ("MRP", "inclusive of all taxes"), the numeral and letter height of Rule 7 Table-I is checked, a statutory pack label is offered only to a company that says it packs or imports, and the app **never prints a number a regulator issues** (a HUID is typed or imported after hallmarking). **The numeral and letter height check is Legal Metrology Rule 7(2) Table-I by principal display panel area** (five bands, read from the consolidated Rules [W]), measured on cap height, not font size. GS1 trade barcodes are held to GS1's own minimums (13.8) | [R-lab 3]; Rules 2011 Rule 7 [W] | no |
| T25 | **The scan layer is opt-in per device and changes nothing when off** (no listener is installed; the dispatcher keeps handing every handler the same event object). On: a timing detector at the keydown capture phase **clocked by `KeyboardEvent.timeStamp`**; **scan targets** that take a scan as one change; a **hold-the-first-key** rule only for truly bare contexts (body or a non-interactive container: never a button, a rail row or a Settings control, which React handles); **resolve at the terminator** inside inputs; AltGr, dead keys, IME composition and Alt-plus-keypad are never read as a scan; **Caps Lock is corrected from `shiftKey`**; a quantity that is 8 or more bare digits is refused as "a barcode, not a quantity". **The layer reduces wrong-field hazards; it does not guarantee them away**: two timing-independent backstops protect Enter and Y on the Accept bar and Narration's Enter. A passive burst observer (hint only, no hold, no swallow) runs for a tracking company with no scanner configured (14) | [R-lab 5, R-aud 3.14] | no |
| T26 | **No new global chord.** `Alt+B` becomes the allocation panel for any tracked item (label text only); Print labels is reached from the page `...` menu, Go To, the left menu, the voucher-type flag "Print labels after Accept" and a bare **`L`** in the report selection bar (the sibling of `I`); inside the Label screen the existing Print chord prints (13.7, 14.6) | the chord table is full (all 26 Alt letters are taken somewhere) [C `shortcutRegistry.ts` `RAIL_FIXED`] | no |
| T27 | **Build order (15):** the pure new-file slices (barcode encoders, label layout, the scan detector and GS1 parser, the unit chain and rules, the Electron bridge's pure half) start **now** in a worktree cut from `main`; **every slice that edits `inventory.ts`, `valuation.ts`, `openingStock.ts`, `stockSummary.ts`, `types.ts`, `VoucherScreen.tsx`, `store.ts`, `keys.ts` or `backend/src/sync.js`** is built in a **new worktree cut from `cf-back-nav` after CF-9a is committed, reviewed and green on a rebased `main`** and rebased in the end. **On `VoucherScreen.tsx` the order is Back's overlay edits, CF-2, CF-3, then this work's thin T-2a (which creates `useStockDims` and the allocation stop), then CF-9c adopting that hook, then T-2b**; the owner may instead jump T-2a ahead of CF-2 (question 22, with the cost written). The label half of the headline is **conditional on a one-day printed spike** (T-6s, 13.6) | one writer per file at a time (wave-a2 rule A2-5); about 50 builder-days must not be written against an unreviewed tip | yes (question 22) |
| T28 | **A second sample company** ("Mobile and pharma sample") is built by a new `core/demoTracking.ts` and offered on the welcome screen; **`demo.ts` is not edited** (its goldens are pinned). Suites build their book from `scripts/fixtures/trackingBook.ts` (16.5) | [R-aud 3.17] | no |
| T29 | **The MCP server** gets the new keys classified in `mcp/src/data/fields.ts`, its write planner refuses a tracked item in the same release (a serial item must not be drafted with no serial), and `mcp/engine-lock.json` is re-recorded after the reconciliation suites pass (15.5) | [R-aud 4.5] | no |
| T30 | **The parity board is corrected when work starts:** `batches` and `expiry-reports` are re-stated **partial**, and the new capabilities are `beyond` rows, never folded into the parity percentage (Appendix P) | the Phase 24 rule: a status and its note must agree | no |
| T31 | **Order inside one date is decided per unit, never by cross-type voucher numbers** (rule W, 4.3): a voucher number is a counter per voucher TYPE, so Sales 14 can follow Credit Note 1 and Purchase 20 follow Sales 3. A unit's same-day `in`, `out` and `move` events are arranged so they alternate (and a return follows an `out` to the same party), the number only breaking ties; a count is the close of its date (`eod`). **Cell and value rules are judged at date boundaries**, so no same-day order is ever read by them. One function (`orderUnitEvents`) serves the entry check, the save, the Data Check, the post-pull scan, the server index and the Specific cost ledger | the blocker of the second review: after save the log sorted a return before its sale and R3 blocked the resale; a same-day purchase and sale reported notInStock | no |
| T32 | **An Upgrade keeps yesterday's looseness for real:** it stamps `ItemTracking.strict: false`, which makes R10 (a cell below zero), R25's required fields and R27 (a typed new batch on a sale) **Warn**, never Block, until the owner chooses Tighten; the Data Check lists negative and phantom cells with a suggested re-balancing Stock Journal (11.4) | the superset rule: accepting less is a regression; today's engine lets a batch go to -3 | no |
| T33 | **Device capabilities are recorded** (`sync_device.caps`, from the push body): the Company features screen lists devices that cannot record units, the editor refuses to switch tracking on while one was seen in the last 30 days, and a new build's post-pull scan flags a tracked outward line that arrived with no units (10.5) | an old build sells tracked items with no unit identity and no layer notices | no |
| T34 | **Pharma items carry the maker and the schedule** (`StockItem.manufacturer`, `StockItem.schedule` none, C, H, H1, X; tail fields): Drugs Rules 65(3)(1)(f) wants the manufacturer, batch and expiry in the sale record of a Schedule C, H or H1 supply, **and** the prescriber and patient and a pharmacist signature, which Comsky does not capture, so **an invoice is not the Rule 65(3) record in v1** and the screens say so (8.2, 13.8, 18) | CDSCO's consolidated Rules [W] | yes |
| T35 | **Scanner facts are a device profile:** `reads2D` (set by a setup scan that includes a QR and a Data Matrix), a pair mode for dual-SIM boxes (two scans per unit; units, not scans, are counted), Caps Lock handled, gap statistics kept per connection (USB and Bluetooth apart) (6.3, 14.7) | handset boxes carry two IMEI barcodes; a 1-D scanner cannot read the QR sticker | no |
| T36 | **A raw label job is one job of many labels** (`labels: [{ bitmap, copies }]`, one spooler write or one TCP stream, progress, a status poll before and after), the Windows RAW helper is a **bundled, hash-checked PowerShell script** run by main only, macOS raw is out of scope for v1, and **the browser-dialog path is proven by a one-day printed spike before anything is promised** (13.6) | one bitmap per call made 1,240 stickers 1,240 spooler jobs | no |

## 1. What this has to stand on, and what it must not collide with

**What exists today** (the facts the design rests on; the full inventory with line numbers is [R-aud 2 and 3]):

- A batch is a free-text name and an expiry on an `InventoryLine` (`batchName`, `mfgDate`, `expiryDate`), entered in a two-field box that opens **only** on `Alt+B` or the rail button and never by itself; the engine keeps one `byBatch` map per item with **no godown**, keyed by the raw name [C `VoucherScreen.tsx` `BatchPrompt`, the Alt+B opener; `inventory.ts` `byBatch`]. Batch arithmetic is wrong in ordinary flows (opening stock is in no batch; a count by batch turns 12 into 7; a transfer or return with no batch on the receiving leg drops the quantity out of every batch; `B-17` and `b-17` are two batches; a batch goes negative without a word) [P, R-aud 3.2.2].
- No serial, IMEI, barcode, label, scan or camera code exists; the Batch and Expiry Summary menu row opens a four-line stub [C `BatchSummary.tsx`]; the parity board nevertheless says `batches` and `expiry-reports` are covered [R-aud 1.2].
- There is a pure QR encoder with an independent decoder test (`core/qr.ts`), a millimetre-exact custom page (the cheque leaf), a print host, a PDF bridge for an untrusted renderer (`electron/exportBridge.cjs`) and a bulk-print selection flow; there is **no** 1-D encoder, no label page, no silent print or printer list [R-aud 3.6].
- Costing is per item; FIFO and LIFO recompute the whole open-lot sum on every movement, so one-unit lots freeze `stockPositions` [P, R-aud 3.2.5].

**What is arriving beside this work** (so the design does not collide):

- **Custom fields, CF-9 "stock by field" and the Back control** in `.wt-cf`: a tracking line field makes `(item, variant)` a stock position with **pooled per-item costing**, a Physical Stock count made by variant is a **count group** (`countPart` lines merged by `stockMovements`), the voucher gets one `cf:` focus per line field, `Box` takes `onClose`, every dialog that holds typed or scanned work registers a dirty probe (rules B5 and B12). CF-9a (the engine) was not written at the first check and **is written in the worktree's uncommitted tree at the review pass** (`variantCore.ts`, `stockByField.ts`, `inventory.ts`; unreviewed, ungated) [C `.wt-cf`, 2026-10-23]; its public shape is the one T-0b widens.
- **The MCP server** pins SHA-256 hashes of 52 `src/` files (`inventory.ts`, `valuation.ts`, `openingStock.ts`, `types.ts`, `integrity.ts`, `splitCompany.ts`, `syncGuard.ts`, `ewb.ts`, `vchNumbering.ts`, `voucherCancel.ts` among them) and classifies every key of the loaded interfaces [R-aud 4.5].
- **Parity wave 3** is committed and deployed (BETA): cancel (`Alt+X`) keeps a shell with no lines, server numbering v2 is live (`backend/src/numbering.js`, `008_voucher_series.sql`, `/sync/next-number`), the Electron PDF and share bridge exists and **has never run on a real Electron binary** [R-aud 4.1].

**Rules this spec obeys** (from CLAUDE.md; each is where a decision below comes from): accepting more input is never a regression, accepting less is; **nothing chosen is the app as it was, asserted by identity**; the default shortcuts and field order are frozen and a new master field goes **last**, in a `tailGroup`; a carried value must always be visible; optional vouchers never count; snapshot tax and price facts on the line; a source-scanning assertion needs a guard that it scanned something; cap length before any pattern test and scan linearly; an Electron IPC takes a name and options, never a path or a free command string; never invent a number a government system issues; "sent to the printer", never "printed".

---

## 2. The tracking model

### 2.1 One stock key, and what a serial is

**The cell.** For an item the engine already keeps quantity per godown (`byGodown`), per batch (`byBatch`, no godown) and, from CF-9, per variant (`byVariant`). All three are *marginals* of one table the engine does not yet keep: quantity per **(godown, batch, variant)**. That table is the cell map.

```ts
/** A cell key is LENGTH-PREFIXED, never joined by a separator: `<len>:<godownId><len>:<batch><variant>`, the variant being the remainder of the string.
 *  CF's VariantKey is itself option ids joined by U+0001 (a two-field variant, Colour then Size, holds a separator), so a separator-joined key would
 *  split a variant in two (a naive split on U+0001 silently drops the second segment) and a batch name containing the separator would shift every part.
 *  `cellKey(parts)` writes it and `cellParts(key)` reads the two lengths and returns the rest as the variant: linear, unambiguous, asserted on a
 *  two-field variant and on hostile batch names. Each part is '' when the item is not tracked by it ('' batch = "no batch": opening stock, unbatched
 *  lines). Never stored. */
export type CellKey = string;
export interface CellParts { godownId: string; batch: string; variant: string }
```

- `batch` is the **normalised batch key** (section 3.4): format characters and C0 and C1 controls removed, trimmed, whitespace collapsed, case-folded, NFKC, and, when the item keeps MRP per batch (`track.mrpAt === 'batch'`), suffixed with `\u0002` and the printed MRP in paise. A display name (the most frequent spelling) is kept beside it.
- `variant` is CF-9's `VariantKey` (the option ids of the item's tracking fields, joined by U+0001), read through CF's resolver, never stored on the line [CF25].
- `godown` is the line's `godownId`.

**Projections, not three maps.** `StockPosition` gains one optional member, `cells?: Map<CellKey, number>`; `byGodown` stays as it is (maintained exactly as today for every item), `byBatch` stays as it is (raw-name keys, no godown) **for every item, so every existing reader and the MCP's locked copy read the same numbers** (a merged count movement aside, 4.2), and `byVariant` is CF-9's API **as CF-9a wrote it** (`Map<VariantKey, { qty, byGodown }>`, updated beside `byGodown` after the valuation call; T-0b does not change it, and for an item that is also batch-tracked derives it from the cell map by `byVariantOf`). For a tracked item the new readers use **projection functions** (`core/stockCell.ts`): `byBatchOf(cells)`, `byVariantOf(cells)`, `byGodownOf(cells)`. The invariant, asserted after every movement at 2,000 and 50,000 vouchers: **for a tracked item the cells add up to the item's quantity, and for every godown they add up to `byGodown[g]`**, to 1e-9 (CF-9's own invariant, widened to one more part).

**The resolver pattern (what keeps the identity claim).** CF-9 passes `variantOf` in `ValuationOpts` and does nothing when it is absent. This spec composes it: `ValuationOpts.cellOf?: (item, line) => CellParts | null`, built by `core/stockCell.ts` `cellResolver(items, trackingDefs)` which answers **`undefined`** when no item is tracked by batch (new mode) or by a variant, and `null` for an untracked item. With it absent, `stockPositions` runs exactly as it always has. **No item-level figure (quantity, value, average cost, `byGodown`) ever depends on whether a resolver was passed**: the cell effect is added **beside** the valuation call, after it, the way CF-9a adds `byVariant`. (The one place how a line was entered moves an item-level figure is a count group, section 4.2; it is read from the line's own `countPart` flag by the one generator every caller shares, so every reader sees the same number.)

**A serial is a unit identity under a cell.** A serial item's line is one cell (`godown`, `batch`, `variant`) plus a list of `qty` identities; the identities do **not** multiply the cell map (500,000 phones are not 500,000 cells). A unit's current cell is derived from its history (section 4.3). So the three dimensions of the stock key are godown, batch and variant, and a serial is the *grain* below them.

**What T-0b does to CF-9's shipped shape** (Appendix R, now the plan of record because CF-9a has been written): add `cells` beside `byVariant` in `stockPositions` and tell `observe` a new `StockMove.cells`; widen CF's per-movement variant effect to `(godown, batch, variant)` as `movementCellEffects`; define `countDeltas` as the variant-only wrapper of `countSpread` (the five CF vectors stay the oracle); keep every public name CF's other slices already read (`byVariant`, `StockMove.variants`, `allocateValue`, `stockDeltaByItem`) unchanged; do **not** refuse a batch-tracked item that also has a tracking variant (the first research draft suggested that [R-vert 8.1 decision 10]; with one shared key there is nothing to refuse). The cost of widening after CF-9a rather than before is a re-point of the variant update in `stockPositions` (CF-9a as written books an ordinary line inline and a count through `countVariantMoves`, then applies both with `applyVariantMoves`; T-0b folds those into `movementCellEffects`) and of the readers named in Appendix R, not a rewrite, because CF kept the key an opaque string and the count spread in one pure function (`countDeltas`).

### 2.2 The four modes and the switches

| Mode | The cell has | A line carries | Quantity | Typical use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `none` (default; also every legacy item) | godown only (and a variant if CF tracks one) | nothing new | any | everything today |
| `batch` | godown + batch | `batchName`, `mfgDate`, `expiryDate`, `mrp`, optional `salePrice` | any (decimals by unit) | pharma, FMCG, food, paint |
| `serial` | godown (+ variant) | `serials[]` | **whole number, equals the unit count** | handsets, appliances, tags |
| `batchSerial` | godown + batch (+ variant) | the batch fields **and** `serials[]` | whole number, equals the unit count | batteries, tyres, serialised drugs, appliances with a lot |

- **Where the mode lives:** `StockItem.track` (section 3.1), plus the existing `batches?: boolean` kept as the **engine's legacy switch** and written `true` whenever the mode includes a batch, so an older reader that knows only `batches` still sees a batch item.
- **The company switch** `CompanyFeatures.tracking?: boolean` (F11, "Maintain batch and serial tracking"), absent for every old company. Off: the stock item form, every voucher screen and every menu are exactly today's. On: the form's existing "Maintain in batches" slot becomes a four-way selector (the **same slot in the Enter walk**, so the walk keeps its length), and the new rules sit in a tail group (6.1, 3.1). A preset (section 11.5) switches it on.
- **Legacy items** (`batches: true`, no `track`): behave as today (the `Alt+B` box, `byBatch` by raw name, no popup, no rules) until upgraded; the cell engine still reads their lines (a line with no batch lands in the "no batch" cell) so every new report works on them; an **Upgrade** action (item, or all items in bulk, from the item list and the Data Check) writes `track: { v: 1, mode: 'batch', ...defaults }` and changes no voucher (11.4).
- **Combination rules:** `serial` and `batchSerial` need a unit with 0 decimals and **refuse a compound or alternate unit** (a "box" has no one IMEI) with a sentence; the costing method of a serial item is `AvgCost` (the default), `Specific` (opt-in, with the caution and confirmation of 4.5), `MonthlyAvg`, `LastPurchase`, `Std`, `Zero` or the legacy `Avg`, and FIFO and LIFO are not offered (T6) and are **coerced to Avg. Cost** by `resolveMethod` when any writer sets them (an importer, REST, an older client; 4.5); a service item cannot be tracked; a tracking **variant** may be combined with any mode (the cell is then `(godown, batch, variant)`); **`second` (a second identifier) exists only when `serialKind` is `imei`**; **`serialKind` and `mrpAt` are locked once the item has any line or opening unit** (the editor refuses with a sentence; the mode may still move between `batch`, `serial` and `batchSerial` for new lines, because the chain is derived from the lines that carry `serials`, not from the item's mode).

### 2.3 What "track" holds, and the rules each key buys

`ItemTracking` is one object so `master-resave`, the MCP classifier and the server carry each handle **one key** (full type in 3.1). In words: the mode; for batch modes whether the manufacturing and expiry dates are asked or required (`off` / `ask` / `req`), a shelf life in days (the expiry is proposed from the manufacturing date), the policy for an **expired** batch at sale (`block` default, `warn`, `allow`), the warning days before expiry (default 30; a pharmacy sets 90, 60, 30), a **minimum remaining life at issue** in days (the "do not issue within N days of expiry" rule), the pick strategy (`fefo` / `fifo` / `manual`), where MRP lives (`none` / `item` / `batch` / `unit`), whether the sale price follows the batch (`batchPrice`), the **MRP ceiling** at sale (`off` / `warn` default / `block`), an auto-naming pattern for batches a manufacturing voucher creates; for serial modes the kind (`imei`, `serial`, `tag`), whether a second identifier is kept, a warranty (months and whether it starts at the invoice or at activation), an auto-number pattern for non-IMEI serials, and whether a purchase may save with pending identities (default yes).

Defaults for a new item come from the **preset** the company chose (11.5); with no preset, a new tracked item gets the strict defaults above (`expiredSale: 'block'`, `mrpCeiling: 'warn'`, `pick: 'fefo'` when the expiry is asked). A legacy upgrade keeps today's looseness (`expiredSale: 'allow'` and **`strict: false`** until the person says otherwise), because the first Upgrade must not start refusing yesterday's habits (the superset rule): the form says so in one line. **`strict: false` is what makes the looseness real**: with it R10 (a cell below zero), R25's required fields and R27 (a typed batch name that does not exist) are **Warn** (visible, Continue armed No) and not Block, so a pharmacy whose history already holds negative or phantom batch cells (today's engine lets a batch go to -3 with no message and drops transfer and return quantity out of every batch [P, R-aud 3.2.2]) can still sell on the first day; a new tracked item is created `strict: true`; **Tighten** (item form, or in bulk from the Data Check) sets `strict: true` and offers `expiredSale: block` and `pick: fefo` together (11.4).

---

## 3. Data model and storage

### 3.1 Types (all additive, all optional; one new types-only file)

The new types live in **`src/trackTypes.ts`** (types only, no runtime), re-exported from `src/types.ts`, which gains only the optional member lines below. That keeps the one-writer edit of `types.ts` to about twenty lines and lets the pure slices start before the CF lane has finished with `types.ts` (section 15).

```ts
// ── on the stock item (tail group of the form; a writer that rebuilds a StockItem MUST spread ...editing, master-resave classifies each) ──
StockItem.track?: ItemTracking;          // absent = legacy: `batches` decides ('batch' when true, else 'none')
StockItem.barcodes?: ItemBarcode[];      // up to 24 per item
StockItem.mrp?: number;                  // the item's MRP when track.mrpAt === 'item' (one undated figure in v1; a dated history is a later feature)
StockItem.batchRecs?: Record<string, BatchRec>;   // keyed by the normalised batch key; up to 500 per item
StockItem.manufacturer?: string;         // the maker's name (<= 80): printed on the batch line of a scheduled drug (8.2); Drugs Rules 65(3)(1)(f) [W]
StockItem.schedule?: 'C' | 'H' | 'H1' | 'X';   // absent = none; the Pharma presets switch these two tail fields on (T34)

export type TrackMode = 'none' | 'batch' | 'serial' | 'batchSerial';
export interface ItemTracking {
  v: 1;
  mode: TrackMode;
  // batch modes
  mfg?: 'off' | 'ask' | 'req';  exp?: 'off' | 'ask' | 'req';   // default ask, ask
  shelfLifeDays?: number;                                       // 1..3650: proposes expiry = manufacturing date + this
  expiredSale?: 'block' | 'warn' | 'allow';                     // new item: block; legacy upgrade: allow
  warnDays?: number[];                                          // ascending, at most 4 values of 1..730; default [30]
  minLifeDays?: number;                                         // 0..730: not issued inside this many days of expiry
  pick?: 'fefo' | 'fifo' | 'manual';                            // default fefo when exp is asked, else fifo
  mrpAt?: 'none' | 'item' | 'batch' | 'unit';                   // LOCKED once the item has a line or opening unit (it is part of a batch's identity)
  strict?: boolean;                                             // false = an Upgrade's looseness: R10, R25 req and R27 only Warn; absent counts as false; a new item is true (2.3, T32)
  batchPrice?: boolean;                                         // the sale price follows the batch (Busy, Zoho)
  mrpCeiling?: 'off' | 'warn' | 'block';                        // sale above MRP; default warn
  autoBatch?: { prefix: string; style: 'date' | 'seq' };        // names a batch a manufacturing voucher creates
  // serial modes
  serialKind?: 'imei' | 'serial' | 'tag';                       // LOCKED once the item has any unit; written onto each line as a snapshot (below)
  second?: boolean;                                             // a second identifier per unit (IMEI 2); only when serialKind is 'imei'
  warranty?: { months: number; from: 'invoice' | 'activation' };
  serialPattern?: { prefix: string; pad: number };              // generator for a non-IMEI serial
  pendingOk?: boolean;                                          // default true: a purchase may save with fewer identities than its quantity
}

export interface ItemBarcode {
  code: string;                       // exact text after normalisation, 3..48 characters
  kind: 'item' | 'pack' | 'variant' | 'scale' | 'alias';
  packQty?: number;                   // pack: pieces in the pack (a scan adds this many)
  packLabel?: string;                 // "Carton of 12"
  variant?: string;                   // variant: the VariantKey (option ids), so a renamed colour keeps its code
  source?: 'maker' | 'shop';
  primary?: true;                     // at most one: the e-invoice Barcde and the label's default code
  off?: true;                         // retired: a scan says so, never resolves silently
}

export interface BatchRec {
  rev: number;                        // bumped by every writer; the server keeps the higher against a stale write
  status?: 'hold' | 'recalled' | 'quarantine';   // absent = active
  reason?: string; since?: string; by?: string;
  name?: string;                      // a corrected display name
  mfgDate?: string; expiryDate?: string; mrp?: number; salePrice?: number;   // corrected values win over the lines'
  mfrBatchNo?: string;                // the manufacturer's number where the shop numbers its own
  attrs?: Record<string, string>;     // up to 5 pairs a trade needs and the core does not (a tile's shade and caliber, a commodity's grade); edited in the Batch master, not typed at purchase in v1
  code?: string;                      // the shop's minted batch barcode (first label print)
  note?: string;
}

// ── on the line (a Voucher literal is rebuilt by VoucherScreen.save: each is carried by itemRowFromLine / validInvLines; voucher-resave classifies each) ──
InventoryLine.mrp?: number;           // snapshot of the batch's / unit's / item's printed MRP at posting (2 dp)
InventoryLine.salePrice?: number;     // inward: the selling price recorded for this batch when it was received (batch-wise price)
InventoryLine.serials?: SerialRow[];  // at most 5,000 per line and per voucher, and at most 400,000 bytes of serial JSON per voucher (the caps below); quantity = length on an outward line
InventoryLine.serialKind?: 'imei' | 'serial' | 'tag';   // snapshot of the item's kind, written with `serials`: the unit key space (3.3) comes from the line, so the server (which holds no item rows) and every later reader derive the same keys even if the item changes
InventoryLine.eod?: true;             // Physical Stock lines of a tracked item: "counted at the close of the voucher's date"; stockMovements applies them after the other movements of that date (4.2). Written only by the voucher screen
InventoryLine.billsNote?: string;     // the id of the Delivery or Receipt Note whose units this line only BILLS (T14)
InventoryLine.missing?: { id: string; why: string }[];   // Physical Stock lines only: units counted missing, each with its reason
// existing and unchanged: batchName, mfgDate, expiryDate (a month-only expiry is stored as the LAST DAY of the month)

export interface SerialRow {
  id: string;                         // the primary identifier, normalised (3.3)
  id2?: string;                       // the second IMEI
  cost?: number;                      // inward lines: this unit's own cost (absent: the line's book value less the explicit costs, shared out, 4.5 rule 1; a returning unit's cost is its issued cost and an edit needs a reason, R30)
  mrp?: number;                       // when track.mrpAt === 'unit'
  attrs?: Record<string, string>;     // up to 10; name <= 24 and value <= 100 characters: colour, storage, HUID, gross / net weight
  lot?: string;                       // the maker's lot or date code, <= 20: an attribute for recall and warranty, NOT a stock dimension
  chk?: { on: string; ok: boolean; by?: string; note?: string };   // used-device IMEI database check stamp (a record, not a lookup)
  replaces?: string;                  // inward: the unit this one replaces (a DOA replacement; the old warranty end is copied)
  w?: { type: 'sale' | 'purchase' | 'install'; from: string; months: number; to: string; why?: string }[];  // EXCEPTIONS ONLY: a sale warranty is derived from the item's rule and the voucher date (3.1 caps); an entry exists only for a start set at activation, a supplier warranty, an installation, a replacement's copied end or an edit (which needs a reason)
  note?: string;                      // <= 200
}

// ── on the voucher ──
Voucher.trackOverrides?: { rule: string; line?: number; unit?: string; reason: string; by: string; at: number }[];   // at most 50: every reasoned override of section 5

// ── on the godown ──
Godown.kind?: 'normal' | 'transit' | 'service' | 'quarantine' | 'jobwork';   // absent = normal

// ── opening stock by cell (supersedes the two independent lists for a tracked item; trusted under the rule of 3.6) ──
OpeningStockAlloc.cells?: { godownId: string; batch?: string; variant?: string; qty: number; mfgDate?: string; expiryDate?: string; mrp?: number; rate?: number; serials?: SerialRow[] }[];

// ── company ──
CompanyFeatures.tracking?: boolean;             // F11: "Maintain batch and serial tracking" (absent = off)
CompanyFeatures.trackingOffline?: 'allow' | 'block';   // a SYNCED company only: may a device sell serial units while it cannot reach the cloud? default 'allow'
VoucherType.labelsAfterSave?: boolean;          // like printAfterSave: opens the Print Labels screen filled from this voucher (off by default)
```

**Why one nested `track` object and not seven item keys:** `master-resave.assert` classifies every `StockItem` key, the MCP classifier and the server's carry rule name keys, and an older item form that spreads `...editing` keeps unknown keys; one key is one classification, one carry entry, one backup check.

**Caps (every one enforced at the write funnel and the importers, each says what it protects):** serial rows **5,000 per line and 5,000 per voucher, and at most 400,000 bytes of serial JSON per voucher** (the byte cap is the real one: a stored version past `LIMITS.versionChars` = 512 KB is recorded in the Mismatch Report **without its content**, so the admin could not restore either side [C `conflict.js`]). Measured row sizes, [P] computed for this pass: a bare `{"id":"356938035643809"}` is **25 bytes** (5,000 rows = 125 KB; the first draft's "about 90 KB" was bare strings, not rows); id, id2 and cost is **62 bytes** (5,000 rows = 310 KB, 8,456 rows would fill 512 KB); a sale row that also carried a warranty entry `w` is **134 bytes** (5,000 rows = 670 KB, over the version size), which is why `w` holds **exceptions only** and a sale warranty is derived from the item's rule and the voucher date; a larger dispatch is split across vouchers and the panel says so; a unit id 32 characters; an attribute name 24, a value 100, ten per unit; `batchRecs` 500 per item; `barcodes` 24 per item, a code 48 characters; `trackOverrides` 50 per voucher; a paste 20,000 rows; **a serial line's quantity 5,000** (a pending-identities purchase of 5,000 is the largest the funnel accepts, so no quantity is ever a pasted 15-digit number). Everything past a cap is **refused whole with the reason**, never cut.

### 3.2 Why the serials ride on the line

The audit's ranked risk 1 is storing serials as a synced table: about twelve mechanisms (outbox, push batching, pull and full reload, backup, restore, undo, cancel release, Mismatch Report, REST scans, `TABLE_AREA`, access and the drift suites) would each need a bespoke path, and at 1,000,000 serials the table plus outbox rows are about 0.7 GB with 3,334 push requests [R-aud 3.9, 5 risk 1]. On the line, a serial rides undo (one step, correct), cancel (the shell has no lines, so the units are released with no code), delete, sync, the Mismatch Report diff, restore and REST for free [R-aud 3.12]. The cost is one older-client exposure (an older `VoucherScreen` rebuilds the line and drops the list), which section 10.5 closes with the carry rule CF-6a is already building, and the rule that **one serial array on one line is unambiguous under the weak line identity** (the reason the audit prefers an array to one line per unit).

**One line, one cell.** For a tracked item every line is exactly one cell (`godown`, `batchName` + MRP, variant) plus its units. A person never has to think in cells: the entry grid shows one row per line and the panel splits a quantity across batches by inserting rows (6.3).

### 3.3 Identifier normalisation (pure, `core/unitId.ts`)

`normaliseUnitId(raw, kind): { id: string; notes: Note[] }`, applied at every door (typing, paste, scan, import, REST). Steps, in this order, the first being the cap:

1. cut to 64 characters **before any pattern test** (the Phase 24 rule; a hostile 1 MB cell costs nothing);
2. Unicode NFKC; remove zero-width, bidirectional and other format characters, C0 and C1 controls and **all** whitespace (the `cleanLabel` family of Phase 27);
3. kind `imei`: map any Unicode decimal digit (category Nd: Arabic-Indic, Devanagari, fullwidth) to its ASCII digit **and say so** (a note "converted from non-ASCII digits": a paste from a Hindi sheet is a real case), then drop the separators `- / . :`; the result is an IMEI only if it is exactly 15 ASCII digits, otherwise it is kept as typed and flagged **not 15 digits**;
4. every other kind: upper-case, **never convert digits**, drop nothing else; at most 32 characters (longer is refused: the e-invoice field is 20 and a 33-character "serial" is a pasted line);
5. the result must be non-empty, contain at least one letter or digit, and not be `0`, a run of one repeated character of length 8 or more, or the literal text `N/A`, `NA`, `NULL`, `NONE`, `-` (a placeholder is flagged, not stored silently).

`unitKey(itemId, id, kind) = kind === 'imei' ? 'I\u0001' + id : 'S\u0001' + itemId + '\u0001' + id` (T11), **where `kind` is the line's own `InventoryLine.serialKind` snapshot** (3.1), never read from the item: the server holds no item rows, and an owner who later changes an item's kind must not make local keys and the server's disagree (the editor also locks `serialKind` once units exist, 2.2).

**One phone, two identifiers (the alias rule).** A unit has a **primary id** (`SerialRow.id`) and, for `second: true`, an `id2`. Both are keys of the same unit: `unitIndex` and `buildChains` keep an alias map `ukey(id2) -> uid` where **`uid` is the canonical key, the primary id's `ukey`**, and every chain, status, clash and report is grouped by `uid`. **An outward row always stores the unit's primary id**: scanning IMEI 2 at a sale finds the unit through the alias map and the row is **rewritten to IMEI 1 on entry** (the chip says "IMEI 2 scanned: unit 3569...809"). Where the purchase is not in the device's own index (an offline device that never synced it) the scanned id is stored as scanned and the server resolves it (10.2: `unit_events.uid`), so a phone sold on device A by IMEI 1 and on device B by IMEI 2 is still one phone sold twice; R9's "all identifiers resolve to the same unit" is therefore **local-index-only on a device and global on the server**, said in the rule. Maps over keys are **`Map`s, never objects** (an id of `__proto__` or `constructor` is just a string). `isImei(id)` = 15 ASCII digits and `luhnOk(id)`. `luhnOk` is the standard Luhn over all 15 digits; `luhnCheckDigit(body14)` is its generator; both asserted on the vectors of Appendix V (`490154203237518` valid, `490154203237519` not, `356938035643809` valid; the whole-number-plus-one trap).

**The range tool.** `rangeUnits(first, count, kind, pattern?)`: for an IMEI it steps the **14-digit body** by one and recomputes the check digit (`490154203237518`, `...526`, `...534`, `...542`, `...559`: the last digits are 8, 6, 4, 2, 9), never the whole number; for another serial it takes the trailing digit run, keeps the prefix and the zero padding and increments (`SN-0009` x 4 gives `SN-0009` to `SN-0012`); at most 5,000, previewed (first and last shown) before anything is added; a run that crosses a pad width widens and says so. Whether a carton's phones really are sequential differs by maker [R-bst 3.1.1], so the tool is a convenience, never an assumption: every generated id is judged by the same rules as a scanned one.

**The TAC prompt.** `tacOf(id)` is the first 8 digits of a 15-digit id. For an item with at least 5 identified units whose most common TAC covers at least 80 % of them, a new unit with a different TAC raises the information **"different model? (type code 49015420 against 35693803)"**, never a refusal (a colour or storage variant of one item may differ) [R-bst 3.1.1].

### 3.4 Batch identity, attributes and the overlay

- **Key.** `batchKey(item, line)`: NFKC, **strip zero-width, bidirectional and other format characters and C0 and C1 controls (the `cleanLabel` family, as 3.3 does for unit ids; a pasted U+0001 cannot reach a cell key)**, trim, collapse inner whitespace to one space, case-fold; if `track.mrpAt === 'batch'` append `\u0002` and the printed MRP in paise (so one batch number at two MRPs is two cells, each shown with its MRP; the pharmacy research asked for uniqueness per `(item, batch number)` with a dated MRP revision, and the vertical research reconciled the two by keeping the **printed** MRP in the identity [R-vert 6.2]; this spec adopts that reconciliation and flags it vetoable). A line with no batch name has the "no batch" key `''`. **A bare batch number on an item that keeps MRP per batch** (a line from an importer, REST, the MCP, a Tally file or history written before MRP was kept carries no `mrp`) is **resolved, not split**: `cellResolver` makes one pass per item over its inward lines and maps a line whose number matches **exactly one** MRP-keyed cell to that cell; with several MRPs it stays a separate "no MRP" cell, the importer preview and REST (`batch_ambiguous` warning) say so, and the Data Check lists it. `mrpAt` and `serialKind` are **locked once the item has a line** (2.2); changing either means a deliberate re-key action with a preview, which is not built in v1 (section 18) because it would restate history.
- **Display name.** The most frequent raw spelling of the key across the item's lines (a `B-17` and a `b-17` merge, and the Batch Summary lists "names merged: B-17, b-17" so nothing is hidden).
- **Attributes come from the lines.** For a key, `mfgDate`, `expiryDate`, `mrp`, `salePrice` are folded from the item's inward lines in date order (opening cell first): the **earliest** non-empty value is the batch's, a later inward line that **differs** raises information at entry ("same batch number, different expiry: probably another batch; Enter keeps the first, Esc renames this one") and a row in the Data Check; the **overlay wins** when present. Nothing is written to the item at Accept.
- **Dates.** Accepted forms: `d-m-yyyy`, `ddmmyy`, `08/27`, `0827`, `Aug-27`, `Aug 2027`, `31-8-2027`, ISO, and the existing mnemonics. **A month-only value is stored as the last day of that month** and prints as MM/YYYY; the batch is saleable *through* that day and expired from the next [R-bst 3.2, R20; the rule is stated in Drugs Rule 96's explanation and the 2025 cosmetics amendment, R-vert 3.1]. "Use before" wording (cosmetics: the day before the first of the month) is not modelled in v1 (listed in section 18).
- **The overlay** `StockItem.batchRecs[key]` exists only for a batch someone edited, put on hold or labelled. It carries a `rev`; each writer sets `rev = max(stored, loaded) + 1`. Because an item write is a whole-document write, a stale save from another device could erase a recall: **the server keeps `batchRecs` against any write that does not state `trk1`, and keeps the higher-`rev` entry per key against a stale one** (hold wins, 10.4); the client mirrors the same merge in `planRemote` (an unsent local edit of the item never silently overwrites a pulled recall: the existing pull guard already holds the pulled version, the Mismatch Report records it).
- **The status sets visibility, not history.** `hold`, `recalled` and `quarantine` make a batch **not issuable** (section 5, R13): it is excluded from every allocation proposal and refused at sale, **never** refused at return, count, transfer-to-quarantine or label print. Releasing is an overlay edit with a reason; every change writes `by`, `since`, `reason`.

### 3.5 What a "godown kind" does

`Godown.kind` is a tail field of the Godown master (the form is the simple-master block in `ui/inventory/masters.tsx`). It changes **nothing for an untracked item**. For a tracked item it decides saleability and the unit's status (4.3): only a `normal` godown's stock is offered at sale; a `transit` godown holds units "in transit"; `service` holds "with service centre" (a repair, a warranty claim); `quarantine` holds returned or recalled stock awaiting disposition ("not for sale"); `jobwork` holds stock at a job worker, still ours. The presets create the godowns a trade needs ("In Transit", "Service Centre", "Returns (not for sale)"); a company with one godown never sees the field.

### 3.6 Opening stock by cell

`OpeningStockAlloc.cells` is the opening of a tracked item: a grid of `(godown, batch, variant)` with quantity, dates, MRP, rate and, for a serial item, the unit rows. It is edited in an **"Opening by cell" panel** reached from the item form's tracking tail group (a `Box` with `onClose` and a dirty probe, an Alt-key panel like the GST history, not a walk field). `openingCellMap(item)` (a fresh `Map` per call) reads, **in this order**:

1. **`openingAlloc.cells`**, trusted only while their quantities add up to the item's `openingQty` within `ALLOC_EPS` (CF-9's two tests collapsed into one, because a cell carries its own godown);
2. else **the lists Split Company Data has written since Phase 24** (`openingAlloc.godowns` and `.batches`, two independent marginals [C `splitCompany.ts`, `openingStock.ts`]): when only one of them exists the cells are that list's rows; when both exist they are paired by the **north-west-corner rule** (godowns and batches in list order, each batch poured into the first godown with room), which reproduces **both** marginals exactly; every cell derived this way is flagged **assumed pairing** (shown so in the panel and listed in the Data Check; saving the panel writes `cells` and ends the assumption). The first draft ignored these lists and put an upgraded split company's whole opening in one no-batch cell in Main Location, so `byGodown` (from the godown list) and the cells disagreed and the asserted invariant failed;
3. else one cell in Main Location with no batch.

For a **tracked item** `openingGodownMap(item)` is **derived from the same cells** (so for every godown the cells add up to `byGodown[g]` by construction, including every upgraded split company); for every other item it is unchanged. **The form never produces a mismatch:** while a tracked item has cells, the item form's **Opening Quantity is read-only and shows the cells' total** ("from Opening by cell"); changing the quantity means editing the cells, and a save whose cells do not add up is refused with the gap. The engine still ignores a list that does not add up (an import, an older client's write) and then the Data Check names the item and the gap ("opening cells ignored: 300 units in 3 cells against Opening Quantity 301"), because silently losing 300 opening IMEIs to a typo is the one failure this rule must not allow. A serial row's `cost` and a cell's `rate` are informational for the batch cost report and **are the unit's own cost for `Specific` (4.5 rule 5: `openingValue` stays the book figure and is distributed over the rows, a difference going to the pending pool)**.

### 3.7 Dexie, indexes, backup, sync payload, performance

- **No Dexie bump and no new table in T-0 to T-6.** Everything new is an optional field on `vouchers`, `stockItems`, `godowns` and `companies` documents; the app loads every table into memory and the engines run over arrays, so **no IndexedDB index is needed or built** (a unit is found through the in-memory index of 4.3, 0.4 microseconds a lookup at a million units [P]); a multi-entry index would need a derived top-level array on each voucher and a hook, and `applyRemote` writes whatever another device sent [R-aud 3.9].
- **The one possible bump is T-7's `labelTemplates`** (custom label designs shared across counters): an additive `version(9)` with a table `'id, cid'`, listed in `ALL_TABLES`, the Dexie class, `loadCompanyData`, `COLLECTIONS`, `checkDelete`, `TABLE_AREA`, `NOUNS`, `BOOK_TABLES`, the server's `DATA_TABLES` and `backupMigrate` (about twelve places, [R-aud 3.9]). It is **optional and vetoable** (T20): the fallback is device-local templates with a file export and import (the Settings precedent). A newer database is tolerated by an older tab (Dexie 4.4.4 retries `VersionError` with no version [C `node_modules/dexie/dist/dexie.js`], not exercised in a browser) and an older client simply never sees the table (`isKnownTable` filters a pull [C `sync.ts`]).
- **Backup.** Every new field is plain JSON on an existing row, so export, restore, Split Company and the auto backup carry it. `BACKUP_SCHEMA_VERSION` is **4 when any tracking data exists** (a `track` object, a `serials` array, a cell opening, a `batchRecs` entry, a godown kind); CF takes 3 when custom data exists, and a file written as 4 is refused by an app that knows only 3 (an older app must refuse rather than eat the units on its next save), exactly CF5's reasoning; `core/backupMigrate.ts` is one writer, shared with CF-0.
- **Sync payload.** A voucher holds at most 400,000 bytes of serial JSON (3.1); `sync.ts` pushes 300 documents per request and the server accepts 20 MB a request [R-aud 3.9], so a batch of large vouchers could exceed it. T-9 adds a **byte cap to the push batching** (about 4 MB a request; a single document above the cap goes alone) **and to the pull page**: `/sync/pull` pages by **500 documents** (`PULL_PAGE`, no byte bound [C `sync.js`]), so a new device's first pull of 500 vouchers of 400 KB each would be 200 MB in one response; the page stops at 500 documents **or about 4 MB of payload, whichever comes first** (the cursor already lets the client continue; the cap is one extra loop condition in the route). The server's `current` read for an older writer (CF 9.3) is bounded by the same per-voucher caps.
- **Performance budgets [J, to be replaced by the first measurement; reference rows are added to `scripts/perf`, not gated until measured]:** `trk.cells` (the cell map for a 250,000-voucher book where 1 item in 20 is batch-tracked) at most 1.15 x the plain `stockPositions` call; `trk.units` (the unit index, 1,000,000 serial events, 500,000 distinct units) at most 400 ms and 130 MB heap, a point lookup under 2 microseconds [P: 240 ms, 94 MB, 0.4 microseconds]; `trk.accept` (one Accept of a tracked voucher against 250,000 vouchers, index rebuilt) at most 700 ms; `trk.specific` (`stockPositions` with 125,000 one-unit Specific lots) at most 600 ms against the 16 to 24 s of FIFO [P]; a 10,000-row paste parsed and judged in at most 150 ms and rendered windowed (only the visible rows mount); a 13-character scan at 3 ms spacing processed in the entry screen in at most 1.5 s **at 120,000 vouchers and a 30,000-item master** with no character lost or reordered (each of the 13 characters costs a render today, and the item popup refilters every item per render [C `VoucherScreen.tsx` `itemPopup`], so the budget is only reachable if a scan is at most two state changes and an exact code is resolved before the substring list is built, 14.3). The index is **lazy**: nothing builds it for a company with no tracked item, and it is dropped (weak memo) when the screens that need it close.

---

## 4. The engines (pure, `src/core/`; no React, no store, no Dexie)

### 4.1 The module map

| File | Owns | Imports |
|---|---|---|
| `src/trackTypes.ts` | the types of 3.1 | nothing |
| `src/core/unitId.ts` | `normaliseUnitId`, `unitKey`, `isImei`, `luhnOk`, `luhnCheckDigit`, `rangeUnits`, `tacOf`, `parsePaste` (separators, header row, pairs) | types only |
| `backend/src/unitChain.js` (+ `.d.ts`, re-exported by `src/core/unitChain.ts`) | the **unit state machine** (4.3): `eventsOf`, `orderUnitEvents` (rule W), `buildChains` (grouped by `uid`, alias map), `statusOf`, `canIssue`, `violations`, `dependants`, `findClashes`, `patchVoucher`, `pendingSlots` (dated slots, adoption), and the **cost ledger** `unitCostLedger` (4.5) | `unitId` logic is **inlined or imported from a sibling backend file** so the server needs nothing from `src/`; one implementation, two importers (the `passwordPolicy` precedent) |
| `src/core/stockCell.ts` | `CellKey`, `cellKey`, `cellParts`, `cellResolver`, `movementCellEffects`, `countSpread`, `byBatchOf`, `byVariantOf`, `byGodownOf`, `heldCells` | `inventory.ts` types, CF's `VariantOf` |
| `src/core/batches.ts` | `batchKey`, `foldBatches` (the derived batch master), `parseExpiry`, `isExpired`, `daysLeft`, `nearTier`, `proposeAllocation`, `splitToLines`, `mrpCeiling` | `stockCell` |
| `src/core/trackRules.ts` | the rules R1 to R31 as one function `checkTracking(ctx, voucherDraft) -> Finding[]` (section 5), `applyOverrides`, `needsReason` | `unitChain`, `batches`, `stockCell` |
| `src/core/unitIndex.ts` | the lazy, memoised index over `vouchers` (`unitIndexOf(vouchers, items, openingCells)`), `lookupUnit`, `unitsInStock`, `warrantyOf`, `traceBatch`, `traceUnit`, `profitByUnit` | `unitChain`, `stockCell` |
| `src/core/valuation.ts` (lane I) | the `Specific` method (4.5) | `unitId` |
| `src/core/inventory.ts`, `openingStock.ts`, `stockSummary.ts` (lane I) | the integration of 4.2 | `stockCell` |

Everything above `valuation.ts` in the table is a **new file**; the three lane-I files are edited by one builder at a time, after CF-9a (section 15).

### 4.2 The cell engine and the count spread

`stockPositions(items, vouchers, upto, observe, opts)` gains the optional `opts.cellOf`. With it, for a tracked item the engine, **after** the valuation call and beside `byGodown`, applies `movementCellEffects(m, ctx)` to `p.cells` and tells `observe` two additive things: `StockMove.cells?: ReadonlyArray<{ key: CellKey; dQty: number }>` and (CF's) `StockMove.variants` derived from the same effects. Nothing before the valuation call changes, so `qty`, `value`, `avgCost`, `byGodown` and the legacy `byBatch` of every item are what they were (the one skip, for a merged count movement, is below).

```ts
export interface CellEffect { key: CellKey; dQty: number }
/** The cell effects of ONE movement, as stockPositions booked it (Physical Stock already resolved to its ITEM-level delta). Pure.
 *  Ordinary line: one effect, +qty or -qty on cellOf(item, line).
 *  Physical Stock: countSpread over the item's cells held at that godown (4.2) — the effects sum to itemDelta exactly. */
export function movementCellEffects(m: MovementLike, ctx: { item: StockItem; cellOf: CellOf; itemDelta?: number; held?: ReadonlyMap<CellKey, number> }): CellEffect[];

/** CF-9's countDeltas over an opaque cell key. parts = the merged movement's parts (null for a whole-item line); wholeKey = the cell a whole-item line names. Pure.
 *  With parts: every listed key (summed if repeated) goes to its counted quantity, delta counted - held; every other key held at the godown with a non-zero balance
 *  goes to 0, delta -held; the difference between the sum of these and itemDelta (at most the rounding of the engine's own round2) goes to the listed key of the largest
 *  absolute delta (the first in list order on a tie), so the sum is itemDelta to the last bit. Without parts: the whole itemDelta goes to wholeKey. */
export function countSpread(held: ReadonlyMap<CellKey, number>, itemDelta: number, parts: ReadonlyArray<{ key: CellKey; qty: number }> | null, wholeKey: CellKey): Map<CellKey, number>;
```

**The five vectors of CF AD.3.3 are kept as they are** (Red 10 and Blue 10 held at Main; parts Red 8 and Blue 10 give item 18; parts Red 8 alone give item 8; a blank whole-item count of 20 moves nothing; a blank whole-item count of 8 gives item 8 with Not set -12) and **repeated with a batch as the key** (B1 5 and B2 7 held; parts B1 5 and B2 7 give item 12, not 7; parts B1 5 alone give item 5, B2 0), and with `(batch, variant)` together; `countDeltas` is `countSpread` with variant-only keys, asserted equal on every CF vector. **The count group** is CF's: `InventoryLine.countPart: true` written by the voucher screen only, `stockMovements` merges the part lines of one `(item, godown)` into one movement of their sum with `parts: InventoryLine[]`, so every reader sees one whole-item count with or without a resolver. **What is new:** the part lines of a tracked item are one per held **cell** (a batch, a variant, or both), and the merged movement's `line` keeps the first part's `batchName`; booking the whole delta to that one batch would be the old 12-to-7 error again, so **the legacy `byBatch` update is skipped for a merged count movement, for every caller alike** (the skip depends only on the movement's `parts`, which the shared generator provides to all callers, so it cannot make an item-level figure depend on a resolver); the cell map is updated from `parts`.

**Old counts are history.** A Physical Stock voucher written before this existed (one line per batch, no `countPart`) is read exactly as it always was at item level (the 12 that becomes 7); at cell level it is a whole-item line per batch (`parts = null`), whose delta goes to the cell the line names, which reproduces the legacy `byBatch` result (`B1 -2, B2 9`) and may leave a cell negative. Nothing is restated: the Data Check lists "Physical Stock vouchers with more than one line for an item at a godown: N" and the owner decides (default: leave) [R-aud 4.2 item 2].

**Inside one date nothing is ordered by voucher number, and a count is the close of its date.** A voucher number is a counter **per voucher type** (`nextVchNo` numbers by `typeId` [C `store.ts`]) and the store sorts `(date, number)` across types, so within a date the order of a Sales voucher, a Credit Note and a Physical Stock is arbitrary, and a Voucher has no time of day. Therefore: (1) **cell quantities (R10, the forward check) and `Specific` values are judged at the end of each date** (the cell after every voucher dated on or before D), never at a voucher's position among the other types of its day; (2) **the unit chain decides validity per unit (rule W, 4.3)**, so the same-day order of two vouchers matters only to the units both name; (3) **a Physical Stock line of a tracked item carries `InventoryLine.eod`** (written by the voucher screen: the Stock Take and the manual count both say "counted as at the end of <date>"), and `stockMovements` yields an `eod` movement after every other movement of its date, stable among themselves (a count group is `eod` when its first part is), so a count's delta is the counted quantity less the balance **after** that day's sales and receipts in every reader. Without it a count numbered below that day's sales is applied first and double-decrements: item 10, three sold, closing count 7: counted-first gives delta -3 then the sales -3, item 4 (Appendix V). The flag is read by the one shared generator exactly as `countPart` is (never from a resolver); a company with no `eod` line gets **byte-identical** `stockMovements` output (asserted); a count written before this existed has no flag and is read as it always was (history is not restated, section 11.4); an older build ignores the flag (the number order as before, a release-note line beside CF's count-group line). The item-level engine otherwise **keeps sorting by (date, number)**: its running same-day figure can dip below zero in between (its entry-time negative-stock warning is unchanged and still item-level), and the two views **agree at every date boundary**: asserted at 2,000 and 50,000 vouchers for item quantity, `Σ cells` and `Specific` value, with each date's vouchers run in every order.

**What the engine asserts.** After every movement on the 2,000- and 50,000-voucher books (and at every date boundary for the order-sensitive figures above): `Σ cells = item qty`; for every godown `Σ cells in godown = byGodown`; `byBatchOf(cells)` summed over godowns equals the legacy `byBatch` for an item whose lines all name a batch and no two raw names fold together; every field of every existing position is identical with and without `cellOf` (`qty`, `value`, `avgCost`, `byGodown`, `byBatch`).

### 4.3 The unit chain: states, status, rules over time

The unit engine is **`backend/src/unitChain.js`**, a single pure JavaScript file with a `.d.ts`, imported by `src/core/unitChain.ts` and by the server (T18). It knows nothing of Dexie or Postgres.
