**Events.** For every non-optional, non-cancelled voucher, every line with `serials` yields one event per unit row: `{ vid, li, date, no, dir, base, itemId, godownId, partyId?, cell, uid, eod? }`, where `no` is the voucher's number (a per-type counter: **only a final tie-break, see rule W**) and `uid` is the unit's canonical key (3.3). Directions come from `lineDir`: Purchase, Receipt Note, Credit Note and a Stock Journal receipt are `in`; Sales, Delivery Note, Debit Note and a Stock Journal issue are `out`; a Stock Journal that issues and receives **the same unit id** in one voucher is a single `move` (godown to godown); a Physical Stock line's `serials` are `present` events and its `missing` entries `absent` events (4.7), both `eod`. The opening cells' serials are `in` events dated `booksFrom`, before every other event of that date. A line carrying `billsNote` yields no event (it bills; the note moved the unit, T14). **The chain of a unit is its events in the order of rule W, not in the store's `(date, number)` order.**

**Order inside one date (rule W; the second review's blocker).** The store sorts vouchers by `(date, number)` [C `store.ts` `sortVouchers`] and a voucher number is a counter **per voucher type** [C `store.ts` `nextVchNo`], so across types the order inside a date is arbitrary. Reading it as time was wrong in three ways found by review: **Sales 14 and Credit Note 1** on one day (a customer buys a phone and returns it within the hour) sort the return first, so the chain reads `in(PV), in(CN), out(SV)`, `receivedTwice`, status "sold", and R3 blocks the resale, although it passed at Accept (the entry position was "after the last voucher of its date") and fails only after save; **Purchase 20 and Sales 3** on one day sort the sale first (`notInStock`, and under `Specific` the unit leaves the pending pool at the average and then sits in the units map for good); a **closing count numbered below that day's sales** is applied before them. Rule W removes the question instead of guessing time:

1. Dates ascend. Within a date, **`present` and `absent` events come last** (a count is the close of its date, 4.2).
2. The remaining events of **one unit** on that date (`in`, `out`, `move`) are arranged by `orderUnitEvents(events, stateBefore, lastOutParty)`: a depth-first search that at each step takes the unplaced event with the **lowest `(no, li)`** among the events that are valid next, backtracking if none completes. **Valid next** means (a) alternation: an `in` only when the unit is not held, an `out` or `move` only when it is held (a unit with no earlier event counts as not held); (b) a **return** `in` (a Credit Note: `base` says so; a Receipt Note is an ordinary receipt, because nothing on the voucher marks it as a customer return and v1 does not model that) only when the last `out` before it in the chain, on this or an earlier date, was **to the same party** (R5's own pairing). The first complete arrangement found is the lexicographically smallest by number among the valid ones (an opening event has `no = -1`, so it sorts first): **the number breaks ties and never decides validity.**
3. No complete arrangement (a real violation: two `out`s with no `in` between) or more than 8 events of one unit on one date: fall back to number order and let `violations` report it as it would.

**The same function everywhere (T31):** the entry check on the draft (its events carry the tentative number of its type; there is no separate "position at entry"), the check after save, the Data Check, the post-pull scan, the **server** (`unit_events` carries `vdate`, `vord` (the number), `line_no`, `party_id`, `base`, `dir`, `eod`, which is exactly what rule W reads, 10.2) and the **cost ledger** of 4.5. Nothing else in the chain reads the order of two vouchers of one date. Worked cases are in Appendix V: PV 9, SV 14, CN 1 become PV, SV, CN; PV 20 and SV 3 become PV, SV; a sale to Raju, a return from Raju and a sale to Meena become SV 7, CN 2, SV 9, and with the return coming from Meena they become SV 9, CN 2, SV 7; and a return with no sale to its party anywhere stays a violation.

**Status of a unit as at a date** (`statusOf(chain, asOn)`): the last event not after `asOn` decides, with the godown's kind for an inward:

| Last event | Status | Shown as |
|---|---|---|
| `in` / `move` / `present` at a `normal` godown | `inStock` | In stock |
| the same at a `transit` godown | `inTransit` | In transit |
| the same at a `service` godown | `service` | With service centre |
| the same at a `quarantine` godown | `held` | Held (or **Returned** when the event is a Credit Note or Receipt Note: it came back from a customer and awaits disposition) |
| the same at a `jobwork` godown | `atJobWorker` | At job worker |
| `out` by Sales or Delivery Note | `sold` | Sold to the party |
| `out` by Debit Note | `rejected` | Returned to supplier (also a DOA) |
| `out` by a Stock Journal that receives no unit and no other item | `scrapped` | Scrapped (a write-off) |
| `out` by a Stock Journal that receives a different item (manufacture, conversion) | `consumed` | Consumed |
| `absent` (counted missing) | `scrapped` | Missing (counted, not found), with the reason |
| an inward line's quantity above its identities | `pending` | not a unit: counted as a number per `(item, godown)` |

The owner's six states map as: in stock, sold, **returned** (the chip on a held unit that came back from a customer, or a plain in-stock unit once restocked), in transit, scrapped, **rejected** (returned to supplier). Everything is a function of vouchers and godown kinds: **no event log, no status voucher, nothing to sync** beyond the vouchers (T5).

**Pending identities, adoption and transfer (rewritten after review: the first text counted a unit twice and ignored dates).**

- **Slots.** An inward line (opening cells included) with `serials.length < qty` contributes a **dated slot** `{ itemId, godownId, date, n: qty - serials.length }`. The **effective pending** of `(item, godown)` as at a date is `Σ n of the slots dated on or before it` less the **adoptions** (below) that still lack an explicit `in`.
- **Adoption.** An outward line naming an identifier the chain does not know may **adopt**: it consumes the earliest slot of that `(item, godown)` **dated on or before the voucher's date**; the unit is created by that sale as a derived, provisional `in`. With no such slot the id is `notInStock` (R3, whose override "purchase bill not yet entered" is exactly the case of a sale dated before the purchase: a sale on 5-Oct can no longer adopt a slot of a purchase dated 9-Oct, which the first text allowed, bypassing R3). This is how a company that starts tracking late sells the phones it already holds (Busy lets tracking start late and apply to later transactions [R-bst 2.1]).
- **An explicit `in` supersedes the adoption**, whatever its date: when the clerk later scans the unit onto the purchase (the normal way to finish a purchase), the provisional `in` is dropped from the chain and the slot it consumed is released, so the chain reads `in(PV), out(SV)` and the count is not taken twice. (The first text kept both: `receivedTwice`, so R7 refused the alteration, or `pending` fell by `serials.length + 1` and the Pending Identities report showed 4 where 5 units were unidentified.) An explicit `in` dated **after** the sale then reads as any unit does under R3, "sold before received". Example: 20 booked with 14 IMEIs, 6 slots; IMEI X is sold (adopted): effective pending 5; the clerk scans the remaining 6 boxes onto the purchase, X among them: 20 serials, pending 0, the adoption superseded, the chain valid; had only 3 been scanned (17 serials): slot 3 less the one still-adopted unit = 2 truly unidentified (Appendix V).
- **Transfer.** R4's "an outward line needs `serials.length = qty`" is relaxed **for a Stock Journal source line**: it may carry fewer rows than its quantity, the shortfall being **pending slots moved** (the source godown must hold effective pending of at least the shortfall); the destination line mirrors the same shortfall as slots **carrying their original dates**, so a carton booked with pending identities can be sent to the shop godown and still be adopted by a sale there, but never by one that predates its purchase.
- **Identity the report asserts:** identified units in stock at a godown plus effective pending equal the cell quantity, at every date boundary.

**Violations over a chain** (the engine behind R1, R3, R5, R7, R9; `violations(chains, vouchers) -> V[]`): an `out` with no `in` before it (and no pending slot to adopt) is `notInStock`; two `out`s in a row by different vouchers is `soldTwice`; two `in`s in a row is `receivedTwice`; the same id twice in one voucher is `repeatedInVoucher`; an `out` at a godown other than the one the unit last sat in is `wrongGodown`; an `out` of a unit in a non-saleable godown kind is `notSaleableHere`; a `Credit Note` in for a unit whose last event is not an `out` to **that party** by Sales or Delivery Note is `notSoldToParty`, **except** a unit the books hold **no earlier event for at all** (sold before a Split Company Data cut, or never recorded): that is `unknownUnit`, a **Warn** that adds the unit as returned stock with a note "history is in the earlier company" and never a Block (the earlier company's file holds its sale, 7.3, 11 Split). All of it runs over chains in the order of rule W. **A hypothetical alteration** is checked with `patchVoucher(chains, oldVoucher, newVoucher)`, which replaces that voucher's events in the affected units' chains only and returns the violations **that are new**; an alteration, cancel or delete is refused when it adds one and **lists the dependants** (the later vouchers whose events rest on this one), while a change that keeps the unit set (a narration, a price) is never refused for a chain it did not touch [R-bst 7.1 R7]. **The same patch mechanism checks batch cells over time (R7 and R10, section 5):** `patchCells` replaces a voucher's cell effects in the engine's per-date cell quantities and returns the cells that **newly** go below zero **at any later date boundary** (the minimum of the running cell quantity over every date from the voucher's date onward), so cancelling or reducing a purchase of 18 strips after 30 were sold from it is refused and lists the dependants, and a back-dated sale from a cell that is positive on its own date but needed by a later sale is refused, which a check at the voucher's own date alone cannot do.

**Clash detection** (`findClashes`) is the same function without a hypothetical: every `soldTwice` and `receivedTwice` in the log, with both vouchers, for the Data Check, the post-pull scan and the server (10.1). **A clash is the true-order clash**: it is evaluated after rule W has arranged each date, so a legitimate same-day sale, return and resale (whatever their numbers) is never reported, and the server therefore never writes a false `received-twice` row for such a pair (the first text's number order did, for every same-day sale-and-return pair, crowding real records out of the mismatch report's open-row cap).

### 4.4 The batch engine: the derived master, expiry, proposals

- **`foldBatches(items, vouchers, cellsEngine, recs) -> BatchRow[]`:** one row per `(item, batchKey)` with display name, the folded attributes of 3.4, the overlay merged, the **cells** (per godown quantity, from the engine's cell map), the first received date and supplier, the purchase rate (the quantity-weighted rate of its inward lines), the status, days left and the near-expiry tier. It is the one source of every batch report and picker.
- **Expiry maths.** `parseExpiry(text, base)` returns an ISO last-day date and a `monthOnly` flag; `isExpired(expiry, asOn) = asOn > expiry` (a batch is saleable **on** its expiry day); `daysLeft(expiry, asOn)` is signed; `nearTier(daysLeft, warnDays)` is the first `warnDays` value not below it. The check date is the **voucher's date**, not today, so a back-dated entry is judged as of its own day.
- **`proposeAllocation({ cells, need, asOn, policy }) -> { picks, short, skipped }`** (pure): eligible cells are those with quantity above 0 in a `normal` godown, status active, and not expired unless `expiredSale` is `warn` or `allow` (then they come **last**, flagged); a cell whose remaining life is under `minLifeDays` is **short-dated**: listed after the valid ones and proposed only when the valid ones cannot cover `need`, and then flagged. Order for `fefo`: effective expiry ascending (no expiry last), then first received date, then batch key; for `fifo`: first received date, then batch key; for `manual`: no proposal, FIFO order listed. `picks` take greedily, a short cell rolls into the next, `short` is what no eligible cell covers (it feeds the negative-cell rule). **Odoo's removal date** (expiry less a removal period) is what `minLifeDays` does by exclusion rather than by ranking; a ranking variant is not built [R-bst 2.5].
- **`splitToLines(picks, template)`** turns picks into the line list (one per cell, quantity, `batchName`, `mfgDate`, `expiryDate`, `mrp`, the rate of each from `salePrice` or the template), the function the panel and the importers share.
- **MRP.** `mrpCeiling(line, item)` compares the **tax-inclusive** unit price `rate x (1 - discount) x (1 + (gst + cess) / 100)` with the line's `mrp` (a retail MRP includes all taxes; the stored rate is tax-exclusive, so the comparison must add the tax back, [R-lab 3.1 rule 2(m), R-vert 3]); more than the MRP plus 0.005 is a finding of severity `warn` (or `block` where the item says so) naming the amounts. No MRP, no check.

### 4.5 Costing: what moves, what never does

- **No figure outside the new screens moves for any item that does not choose `Specific`** (the one exception is a serial item whose stored method is FIFO or LIFO, which is coerced to Avg. Cost, below; no such item exists before this work). The item's costing method values it, batches and variants share the item's value (CF24), and `allocateValue` (CF AD.3.5: value shared by quantity, residual to the largest, "Unallocated" when a share is negative) is **reused** for the "book value share" column of the batch report, which therefore ties to the Stock Summary to the paisa.
- **Batch cost is a report.** `foldBatches` carries `purchaseRate` and `valueAtCost = quantity x purchaseRate`; the Batch Summary shows both columns and a line "costing method difference" (the item's book value less the sum of batch values at cost), so a pharmacist sees what each lot cost **and** the books still tie.
- **`Specific` (new method, serial items only; opt-in, see the AS 2 bullet).** `CostingMethod` gains `'Specific'` with a `METHODS` entry carrying a **`caution` line like LIFO's** (below) and an **explicit confirmation** in the item form ("Use Specific cost for <item>? Esc keeps Avg. Cost"). `valuation.ts` gets a branch that holds **no lot array**: `ValState.units?: Map<string, number>` (unit key to its cost), `pendingQty` and `pendingValue` (identity-less stock), and a running `value`. **The cost of every unit event is resolved by the unit chain, not by the order in which movements are processed**: `unitChain.js` `unitCostLedger(chains, lines)` gives every event a cost, `unitIndex` builds it once, and `stockPositions` reads it through `ValuationOpts.unitCosts` (a `Map<eventKey, number>`, `eventKey = voucherId, line, ukey`); with it absent a `Specific` item is valued as Avg. Cost for that call, which is also exactly what an older build and the MCP's locked engine do (`resolveMethod` answers `'Avg'` for a method it does not know [C `valuation.ts`]: **closing stock of a `Specific` item differs between builds until each is updated or re-locked**, said in the release note, in the item form's one line, and by a Data Check line while any device seen in the last 30 days lacks `trk1`). The rules, each pinned by a vector in Appendix V:
  1. **A new unit** (no earlier event in its chain) costs its own `SerialRow.cost`, else the line's **book value** (`line.amount`, or quantity x rate: what the engine books today) less the explicit costs, shared over the rows without a cost and the pending identities. The line's book value is **distributed, never replaced**: when every row states a cost and the sum differs from it, the difference sits in the pending pool (`pendingValue`), so the invariant below holds by construction.
  2. **A re-entering unit** (the chain shows an earlier `out`: a Credit Note, a transfer back, a re-listing under another item) comes back **at the cost it left at**, whatever the return's line amount. A Credit Note's line amount is the **sale price** (the engine books `inValue = line.amount || qty x rate` and the other methods enter a return "at the note's own value" [C `valuation.ts` header]); `Specific` does not use it, because the first text would have re-entered a phone bought at 10,450 and sold at 14,000 **at 14,000**, overstating stock by 3,550 and showing the resale at zero margin. An edit of that cost needs a reason (a reasoned override of **R30**, Inventory right).
  3. **A unit with no history** that comes in (a return of a unit sold before a company split, a count that **found** a unit, an adopted unit) takes the **pool average**: the item's value over its quantity at the start of that date, or the item's last purchase rate when stock is nil; the Data Check lists "units at an assumed cost".
  4. **A cross-item re-itemisation of an IMEI** (the unit key is company-wide) moves the unit's cost from the issuing item to the receiving item: A falls and B rises by the same amount, value-neutral. A non-IMEI serial is per item, so its receipt is a new unit under rule 1, and a Stock Journal receipt with no cost and no rate takes **what the same voucher issued** (the existing transfer rule [C `inventory.ts` `sjIssued`]); the first text left such a receipt at zero.
  5. **Opening** is the first inward line: the opening rows' own costs, else `openingValue` less the explicit costs shared over the rows; a difference (the rows' costs summing to something else) goes to the pool and the Data Check says so. `openingValue` stays the book figure, so the invariant holds on day 0.
  6. **An outward unit** leaves at the cost it holds; an unknown unit adopted from pending is taken at the pool average and becomes a known unit; a count's `absent` unit leaves at its cost.
  **Invariant, asserted at 2,000 and 50,000 vouchers under every voucher type, a return, an opening, a conversion and a count: at the end of every date the item's value equals the sum of the costs of the units in stock plus the pending pool and equals what the Stock Summary prints, and it does not depend on the order in which two vouchers of one date were processed (the suite runs each date's vouchers in every order, 4.2).** The work per movement is the units moved, so the 125,000-lot book of the audit is linear [P]. An item whose lines carry no serials behaves as Avg. Cost. `valuationCompare` skips a serial item with a note (it would be asked to rebuild one-unit lots under FIFO).
- **Why not FIFO/LIFO for serial items:** both call `lotsValue` (a sum over **every open lot**) on every inward and outward [C `valuation.ts` `lotsValue`; R-aud 3.2.5 corrected the first audit's blame on `Array.shift`]. The item form therefore does not offer them for a serial item; an existing FIFO item cannot be switched to a serial mode until its method is changed (a one-line prompt). **Hiding them in the form is not enough** (an import: `parseTallyCosting` maps FIFO and LIFO [C `valuation.ts`]; the Tally batch-of-one conversion; a REST write; an older client): `resolveMethod` is widened to read `track.mode` and **returns `'AvgCost'` for a serial or batchSerial item whose stored method is FIFO or LIFO** (an item with no `track`, which is every item today, is untouched, so no existing figure moves), the Data Check lists the item ("method FIFO ignored: serial items are valued at Avg. Cost"), and the converter sets `AvgCost` and says so in its report. Without this a converted Tally FIFO phone item would freeze `stockPositions` at 100,000 one-unit lots. Making `lotsValue` incremental would also help a batch item with thousands of open lots; it is a separate, identity-gated optimisation (an optional item of T-0b), not a precondition.
- **AS 2, and why `Specific` is not the Mobile preset's default (reversed by the second review).** AS 2 paragraph 14 assigns cost by specific identification to items that are **not ordinarily interchangeable** and to goods produced and **segregated for specific projects**; paragraph 15 says it is **inappropriate where there are large numbers of interchangeable items, because profit could then be steered by choosing which units remain in inventory** [W: ICAI's Expert Advisory Committee opinion quotes both, https://eacopinion.icai.org/html/20/27.htm; the standard's own PDF at kb.icai.org failed its certificate check]. Handsets of one model, colour and storage are interchangeable, and with `Specific` the clerk's choice of which IMEI to scan decides cost of sales, which is what an auditor challenges. So: **the Mobile preset defaults to Avg. Cost and keeps the per-unit cost as data** (`SerialRow.cost`, the Unit Profit report with its "books use Avg. Cost: difference X" line), which is all a margin-scheme record (CGST Rule 32(5)) needs to read; **`Specific` is an opt-in** behind the caution and confirmation above, and is a sensible default only where units are genuinely unique (jewellery pieces, segregated project stock), where the Jewellery preset may default it. The owner's accountant decides, and the used-device margin invoice is **not built here** (it needs a CA's confirmation first, [R-bst 3.1.4]). The `METHODS` caution reads: "AS 2 reserves specific identification for items that are not ordinarily interchangeable or are set aside for specific projects; for many units of one model it lets profit be steered by which unit is sold. Use Avg. Cost and keep the per-unit cost as data; confirm with your accountant." Tally export names it "Avg. Cost" with the "not carried" notice.

### 4.6 Opening stock

`openingCellMap(item)` (a fresh `Map` per call) reads, in the order of 3.6: `openingAlloc.cells` while they add up, else **the godown and batch lists Split Company Data wrote (north-west-corner pairing, flagged assumed)**, else one cell in Main Location. `applyOpeningAlloc` seeds `p.cells` when the caller passed `cellOf`; the unit chain seeds `in` events from the cells' `serials`. For a **legacy** item (no `track`) the legacy `openingAlloc.godowns` and `.batches` lists are read exactly as before; for a **tracked** item they are read **through `openingCellMap`**, never independently, so `byGodown` and the cells cannot disagree. Split Company writes `cells` for a tracked item (lane I, T-0b) so a split company opens with its batches per godown.

**What a split company does not carry, and what the later company does about it (said plainly; owner question 24).** Unit chains, the sale party and date and the warranty end of a unit **sold before the cut**, batch genealogy and recall lists live in vouchers that stay in the earlier company, so the later company starts each unit still **in stock** with an opening `in` dated `booksFrom` (its `cost`, `id2`, attributes and purchase warranty travel in the opening cells' rows), and knows **nothing** of units already sold. The consequences are handled, not hidden: (a) R5 judges a return of a unit the later company has **no event for** as `unknownUnit`, a **Warn**, not a Block (4.3), so a customer's phone sold last year can still be taken back; (b) Unit History and Warranty answer an unknown identifier with "no record in this company: history before <booksFrom> is in the earlier company's file", never "no such unit"; (c) Batch Trace and a recall list show the quantity received before the cut as one line "received before <booksFrom>: see the earlier company"; (d) **the Split Company screen, for a company that tracks, shows what ends at the split and offers, as an Excel file, the list of units sold with warranty still running at the cut date** (a pure report over the earlier company, T-3) so the shop keeps it; (e) a per-unit history stub in the later company (a `unitStubs` table or a carried voucher) is **not built in v1** because a year of handset sales is tens of thousands of rows and no safe synced home for them exists yet (question 24).

### 4.7 Physical Stock and the unit chain

A Physical Stock line of a serial item may carry `serials`: **the units found present at that godown on that date** (a stock-take by scan, 7.6). The chain reads them as `present` events (a unit not at that godown is **found**, arriving there) and the line's `missing` entries as `absent` (a unit the books had at that godown and the count did not find, each with a reason). The item-level quantity is resolved exactly as today (counted quantity against the godown balance, **the balance after that date's other movements because the line is `eod`, 4.2**), the chain reads the line's events after every other event of that date for the same reason (rule W), and the unit effects are consistent with it by construction: the counted quantity is the present units **plus the pending identities the person confirms** (unidentified stock cannot be scanned, so it is counted by typing a number), missing = the units the books held at that godown that were not present, found = present units not held there. A line with `serials` **must** list every `missing` unit with a reason before it can be saved (T-3; the screen enforces it, the rule R17 checks it). A same-day sale **entered after** a count that listed its unit present makes the chain read "sold, then found at the close": R7 raises a Warn on that sale ("counted present at the close of 31-Mar, count 3") and the Data Check lists it as `counted-then-sold`; it is never silent and never a Block.

---

## 5. The rules (one function, four callers)

`checkTracking(ctx, draft) -> Finding[]` in `core/trackRules.ts`, pure, over the voucher log as it would stand **with the draft applied** (`patchVoucher`, 4.3). A `Finding` is `{ rule, severity, line?, unit?, message, blockedBy?: { voucherId, numberStr, date }, overridable?: 'inventory' | 'administration' }`. **It reports only findings the draft ADDS** (violations the log already had that the draft does not touch are Data Check rows, never reasons to refuse an unrelated edit; this is what lets an old voucher with a legacy quirk still be altered).

**The four callers (T8).** (1) `VoucherScreen.doAccept`, which turns findings into the Accept bar's warnings and, for a block, an inline refusal naming the document in the way ("IMEI 356938035643809 was sold on 12-Oct-2026, INV-87, to R. Kumar"); (2) the **write funnel**: a pre-write guard beside `writeGuard` in `data/writeGuard.ts`, called from `putDoc` for a `vouchers` row when `features.tracking` is on, so Bulk Entry, the importers, Mark Optional, Tally import and every writer that bypasses the screen is judged by the same function (a pull from the cloud and a restore skip it, as `writeGuard` does, and are caught by caller 3 and 4). **The guard is synchronous and reads the window's in-memory store, and the app has no cross-window data coordination** (no `BroadcastChannel`, `liveQuery` or storage event for vouchers [C grep of `src/`]), so its guarantee is **per open window**: the callers that may await (`doAccept`, and every bulk writer when it opens its `session`) first run `ensureFresh()`: it asks Dexie for the outbox rows written since the window last looked (`db.outbox.where('seq').above(last).primaryKeys()`, O(new rows)) and, if any key is not one this window wrote itself (a window-local set of its own `putDoc` results), reloads the company data before judging, so a second tab on the same profile that sold IMEI X a moment ago is seen (a few hundred milliseconds, only when something changed; a microsecond race between the check and the write remains and is caught by caller 4 at the next sync). The funnel itself stays synchronous. It honours `Voucher.trackOverrides` so a voucher saved with reasons stays saveable, and takes a `session` set for a bulk writer so 500 vouchers are judged against each other without rebuilding the index 500 times; (3) the Data Check (`core/integrity.ts`: new kinds `unit-duplicate`, `unit-not-in-stock`, `unit-count-mismatch`, `pending-identities`, `batch-negative-cell`, `expired-in-stock`, `unbatched-stock`, `legacy-batch-box`, `batch-name-merge`, `old-multiline-count`, `barcode-duplicate`, and, added by the review: `serial-line-short` (an outward line of a serial item with fewer units than its quantity: written by an older build), `unit-adjacent-imei` (a possible dual-SIM ghost, 6.3), `counted-then-sold`, `opening-cells-ignored`, `opening-pairing-assumed`, `unit-assumed-cost`, `serial-method-coerced`, `legacy-expiry-first-of-month`, `specific-older-builds`); (4) the post-pull scan in `sync.ts`, which runs `findClashes` over the vouchers that arrived (the existing `checkDuplicates` is skipped above 5,000 arrived vouchers [C `sync.ts`], so a second device's first sync would never be scanned) and records `duplicate-serial` (10.2); **it also flags a tracked outward voucher that arrived with no unit identity** (a writer that lacks `trk1`: a line of a serial item with fewer `serials` than its quantity) as a client-recorded `unit-unrecorded` mismatch row, because the server holds no item rows to know the item is tracked (10.5).

**Severity ladder [R-bst 7.4].** **Block**: the books would become false; nobody may proceed without an override where one is named. **Block, overridable**: the books are fine but the business may have a reason; the person with the named right gives a reason (at least 5 characters), which is written to `Voucher.trackOverrides` and printed on the Accept bar. **Warn**: a probable mistake; Continue is armed No (the existing warning pattern). **Inform**: no action. Every row says what it is for, who may override and where the reason is kept.

| # | Rule | Applies to | Check | Default | Override |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | A unit is unique among live units | any line with `serials` | the unit key has no live earlier `in` without an `out` after it; the same id twice in one voucher is refused outright | **Block** | Administration right, reason (a legitimately reused number) |
| R3 | An issue needs the unit in stock **here, as at the voucher date, and not out again later** | outward lines (Sales, Delivery Note, Debit Note, Stock Journal issue) | chain validity **in the order of rule W** (4.3: a same-day return or purchase with a lower or higher number is never read as time); godown holds it; the godown kind is `normal`; an unknown id may **adopt a pending slot dated on or before the voucher** (4.3); the message names the blocking document | **Block, overridable** | Inventory right, reason (e.g. "purchase bill not yet entered"); the unit is then flagged `sold before received` in the Data Check until the purchase arrives |
| R4 | The quantity is the unit count | serial and batchSerial lines | outward: `serials.length === qty`, **except a Stock Journal source line, which may carry fewer rows and move the shortfall as dated pending slots** (4.3); inward and opening: `serials.length <= qty` and the shortfall is pending (T10); **a serial line's quantity is at most 5,000 whatever the layer or any setting** (a pasted 15-digit number in Quantity is refused, never booked as 356 trillion pending identities); Physical Stock: R17 | **Block** (outward, and an impossible quantity); **Inform** (pending) | none |
| R5 | A return starts from the sale | Credit Note and Receipt Note (units), batch return | each unit's last event is an `out` by Sales or Delivery Note **to this party**; the batch of a return defaults to a batch **sold to this party** for this item (net of what was already returned); another batch needs a reason. **A unit with no event at all in this company** (sold before a Split Company Data cut) is `unknownUnit`: **Warn**, returned as stock with a note, never a Block (4.3, 4.6) | **Block** (serial with a different known party); **Warn** (batch not sold to the party; an unknown unit) | Inventory right, reason ("bought through a reseller") |
| R6 | A supplier return needs the stock; **expiry never blocks a return** | Debit Note, rejection out | the unit or cell quantity is in stock; no expiry, hold or recall check; a hold or recall **allows** it | **Block** unless in stock | none |
| R7 | An alteration, cancel or delete cannot orphan a later event | any voucher with units, **and any voucher with a batch or cell quantity** | **units:** `patchVoucher`: the **new** violations of the units this voucher touches. **Cells:** `patchCells`: the cells that **newly go below zero at any later date boundary** (the minimum of the running cell quantity over every later date; run on alter, cancel and delete of an inward line and on a back-dated outward line, 4.3). The screen lists the dependants (voucher numbers and what each did to which unit or cell); a same-day sale entered after a count that listed its unit present is a **Warn** (`counted-then-sold`, 4.7) | **Block** (units, cells); **Warn** (counted-then-sold); a **Soft** item (`strict: false`) only Warns for cells | none: cancel or alter the later documents first |
| R8 | Copy, recall, Alt+2, Bulk Entry and imports never carry units | duplicate, ghost recall, bulk, import | `itemRowFromLine(l, false)` drops `serials`, `billsNote`, `missing`; an **outward** copy also drops the batch (a suggestion only); an inward copy keeps the batch as a suggestion | always | none |
| R9 | Identifier format and uniqueness of every identifier | rows with `id` or `id2` | an IMEI item: 15 ASCII digits and Luhn; any other kind at most 32 characters; `id2` differs from `id` and from every other live identifier; all identifiers of a unit resolve to the same unit (**on a device with the purchase in its local index; on the server through `uid`**, 3.3, 10.2); an outward row **stores the primary id** (a scanned IMEI 2 is rewritten on entry, 3.3) | **Warn** (format, TAC); **Block** (duplicate) | duplicate: as R1 |
| R10 | A cell never goes below zero | outward lines of a tracked item | the cell's quantity **at the end of every date from the voucher's date onward** (the engine's cell map plus the draft, `patchCells`; never at a position among a day's other voucher types, 4.2) is at least 0; **a cell that was already negative or phantom before an Upgrade is exempt until the voucher touches it** | **Block, overridable** (a strict item); **Warn** (a Soft item, `strict: false`) | Inventory right, reason |
| R11 | Expired stock is not sold or issued | outward Sales, Delivery Note, Stock Journal issue | per `track.expiredSale`: `block`, `warn`, `allow`; the check date is the voucher date; **inside a warning day** the finding is Inform with the tier ("expires in 41 days"); **under `minLifeDays`** a Warn ("short-dated: 38 days left, the item wants 90") | item: new `block`, upgraded `allow` | Inventory right, reason; purchase return, write-off, count, transfer to quarantine are exempt (R6) |
| R12 | Batch identity and snapshot | inward lines | the same number with a different expiry or manufacturing date: Inform with the choice (keep first / rename); a different printed MRP is a separate cell (3.4); the attributes are **snapshotted on the line** (`mrp`, `salePrice`, dates), so editing a batch later never rewrites a printed invoice | **Inform** | none |
| R13 | A held or recalled batch is not issuable | outward lines | the overlay status is `hold`, `recalled` or `quarantine`; never applied to returns, counts, transfers into a quarantine godown, label printing | **Block, overridable** | Administration right, reason |
| R14 | Two devices must not both sell one unit | all | section 10 (reserve, detect, record) | **Detect and record** | resolved on the Mismatch Report |
| R15 | A note and its invoice issue the stock once | Sales and Purchase lines with `billsNote` | `stockMovements` skips such a line; the units on it must be units of that note, to the same party; without `billsNote` the second issue is an ordinary `soldTwice` | **Block** (units not on the note) | Inventory right |
| R16 | A transfer moves identity and cost, never creates or destroys | Stock Journal | the destination line starts from the source line (batch and units are mirrored, 7.5); it cannot be saved without them; the source must hold the unit | **Block** | none |
| R17 | A count is per cell, and for serials a scan | Physical Stock | tracked item: part lines per held cell (CF count group), **every line `eod`** (counted as at the close of the date, 4.2); a serial count's `serials` are the present units and every `missing` unit has a reason; no silent adjustment; **a second whole count of the same item at the same godown on the same date warns before Accept** ("a count for these items at this godown on this date exists: Physical Stock 3; this one replaces it", 7.6) | **Block**; **Warn** (a second same-day count) | none |
| R18 | An optional voucher never claims a unit or a batch | `optional` vouchers | the chain ignores them; **marking one regular runs every check** (Mark Optional / Regular judges each selected voucher and refuses those that fail, with the reason) | always | none |
| R20 | A month-only expiry is the last day of that month | all date entry | `parseExpiry` | always | none |
| R21 | Warranty is data | outward lines of an item with `warranty` | the **sale warranty is derived, not stored**: the item's rule (`from: 'invoice'` is the voucher date) gives the end date wherever it is read; `w` holds **exceptions only** (an `'activation'` start once set, a supplier or installation warranty, a replacement's copied end, an edit), and an edit of `w` needs a reason (`w[i].why`). Storing `w` on every unit would put a 5,000-row voucher at 670 KB (3.1) | **Inform** | reason |
| R22 | A used handset received carries its IMEI-check stamp | inward unit rows with `attrs.condition` of `used` or `refurbished` | `chk` is present | **Warn** | none |
| R23 | Overrides are rights and leave a trail | all overridable rules | the right is checked **before** the reason is asked; the reason is stored | always | n/a |
| R24 | Nothing chosen is the app as it was | everything | every rule is keyed on `features.tracking` and an item's `track.mode`; an untracked item's lines never reach any of it | always | n/a |
| R25 | A required field is present | inward and outward lines of a batch item | `mfg` / `exp` per `req`; `mrp` when `mrpAt === 'batch'` (Warn if absent); an outward line names a cell that exists **or** the "no batch" cell when that holds stock (Inform: "unbatched stock") | **Block** (req, strict item), **Warn** (req on a Soft item; mrp) | Inventory right (req) |
| R26 | MRP ceiling | outward lines with `mrp` | 4.4 `mrpCeiling`: the tax-inclusive unit price above the MRP | item: `warn` | Inventory right when `block` |
| R27 | No new batch on a sale | Sales, Delivery Note, Debit Note, Stock Journal issue | the line's cell must already exist; a typed new name is refused ("a sale picks from stock; a return or a purchase creates a batch"); **a typed name that exists only in another godown is not "new": the picker offers that cell with its godown, and R10 judges the quantity** | **Block** (strict item); **Warn** (Soft item: the first Upgrade's looseness) | none |
| R28 | Pending identities only where stock arrives | `serials.length < qty` | allowed on a Purchase, Receipt Note, opening cell and Stock Journal receipt when `pendingOk`; never on an outward line **except a Stock Journal source line that moves pending slots** (R4, 4.3) | **Block** (outward) | none |
| R29 | Caps | all | the caps of 3.1, including 5,000 rows and 400,000 serial bytes per voucher and a serial quantity of 5,000 | **Block** | none |
| R30 | A returning unit comes back at the cost it left at | inward rows of a `Specific` item whose chain shows an earlier `out` | the row's cost is the issued cost (4.5 rule 2); the panel pre-fills it; **an edit needs a reason** | **Inform** (the pre-fill); **Block, overridable** (an edit without a reason) | Inventory right, reason |
| R31 | A tracked outward line carries its units | outward lines of a serial item | `serials.length === qty` (R4); on a **pull**, a voucher arriving from a writer that lacks `trk1` with a short list is recorded as a `unit-unrecorded` mismatch (10.5) | **Block** (entry); **Detect and record** (arrival) | none |

**Not rows of the table:** R2 (a unit is in one place and one state at a time) is not a check but what `statusOf` derives (4.3); R19 (costing) is section 4.5; both keep their numbers from [R-bst 7] so the research and this spec cross-reference.

**Where a reason lives.** `Voucher.trackOverrides` (3.1). The Accept bar lists every override it will record. An alteration keeps the existing list (so re-saving a voucher saved with a reason does not ask again for the same finding), and the Data Check never lists a finding that a stored override covers.

**What a company that tracks nothing sees:** none of the above. `checkTracking` is never called for a voucher whose lines name no tracked item, `putDoc` does not even build the index (the guard's first line is the feature test), and the Accept path is byte-for-byte today's (asserted by identity, 16.2).

---

## 6. The voucher entry experience (keyboard first, opt-in per item)

### 6.1 The item master

The stock item form keeps **its walk**: the existing "Maintain in batches" slot (a Y/N toggle) is, in a company with `features.tracking`, a four-choice select **in the same place**, so Enter walks the same fields in the same order (a legacy item shows "Batch (basic box)"; choosing another mode upgrades it, 11.4). **New fields go last, in their own `tailGroup`** (the wave 3 precedent [C `partyTermsFields.ts`]): Enter on what was the last field still raises Accept and Down or Tab steps into **Tracking rules** (the keys of 2.3 that apply to the chosen mode), then **Barcodes** (a small list: code, kind, pack quantity; `Alt+A` adds a row as elsewhere), then **Pharma details** (**Manufacturer** and **Schedule**: none, C, H, H1, X; drawn only when a Pharma preset or the company's tracking setting asks for them, T34), then **Opening by cell** (a button that opens the panel of 3.6; while it holds cells **Opening Quantity becomes read-only and shows their total**, 3.6). **`serialKind` and `mrpAt` are shown locked, with the sentence why, once the item has a line.** An Upgrade writes `strict: false`; the form's **Tighten** button sets it true. Three assertions hold the order (the `party-terms` pattern, reading the source): the walk with every new key removed is today's; Enter on the old last field raises Accept; the new keys come after every existing tail. The costing select hides FIFO and LIFO for a serial mode (T6).

### 6.2 The walk and the identity proof

Today's item row walks Name, Godown (when the company has more than one), Quantity, Rate, Discount (when on), Amount, Description (when on) [C `VoucherScreen.tsx`, the `'item'` case]. **For a tracked item the walk gains one stop, between Quantity and Rate**: Enter on Quantity opens the **allocation panel** (6.3) instead of going straight to Rate; closing the panel with Done lands on Rate exactly where Enter on Quantity lands today, with the rate pre-filled when the item's `batchPrice` says so. **For every other row the code path is the one that exists**: a row is "tracked" only when `features.tracking` is on and its item's mode is not `none`; `trackedRow(row)` is the single predicate, and the assertion is a recorded key table (`scripts/assert/vectors/walk.json`: the sequence of focus stops for a sales voucher with 3 untracked items, with a godown column, with discount and description on, with Alt+2 and ghost recall) that must be **identical** with the feature off and with it on for untracked items. An existing batch item (legacy) keeps the Alt+B box and **no** extra stop.

An **Enter on a blank Quantity** of a serial row opens the panel too, and the **unit count** (units, never scans: 6.3 rule 11) becomes the quantity (today a blank quantity answers "Quantity is required!"; for a tracked row only, more input is accepted, never less). **A quantity typed into a serial row is refused above 5,000** ("that is too large to be a quantity: a barcode?"), with or without the scan layer (R4).

### 6.3 The allocation panel

One `Box` (with `onClose`, a visible Close, a dirty probe, rules B5 and B12 of the Back spec), opened by the walk, by `Alt+B` and by clicking the row's chip. Its state is **local to the panel** (the voucher screen is a 4,118-line component; a scan burst must not re-render it per character); it hands the screen one result on Done. Three shapes, by direction and mode:

**Outward batch (sales, delivery note, debit note, stock journal issue): the picker.**
```
┌ Batch · Paracetamol 500 (strip of 10) · Main Location · need 30 ─────────── Close ┐
│ Batch      Exp      MRP     In stock  Take  Note                                  │
│▶B-2031    08/2027  22.50       18      18   first to expire · proposed            │
│ B-2044    11/2027  22.50       40      12   proposed (the rest rolls over)        │
│ B-1990    03/2026  21.00        6       —   EXPIRED: blocked                      │
│ B-2102    12/2026  22.50       10       —   short-dated (69 days; 90 wanted): skipped │
│ Take 30 of 30 in 2 batches                                                        │
│ Enter accepts · ↑↓ row · type digits to change Take · Space on/off · Ctrl+A done  │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```
The proposal (4.4) is **pre-selected and shown**; Enter accepts it; typing letters or digits in the filter box narrows by batch number (and a scanned batch code selects its row); Take is editable per row (typing digits, or **a click on the Take cell**, so a mouse-only user can change it) and the panel keeps `Σ Take = quantity` (it asks to change the quantity when they disagree). A row blocked by a rule shows why in its Note and cannot take quantity; an overridable one shows an **Allow with a reason** button (Tab to it, Enter), which asks for the reason and needs the named right. The stock shown is the **cell's** (godown and batch), never the item's.

**Inward batch (purchase, receipt note, credit note, stock journal receipt, opening): the grid.** One row per batch of the supplier's invoice, columns in the order of an Indian pharma purchase bill [R-bst 6.4, J]: **Batch no, Mfg, Expiry, MRP, (Sale price when the item keeps one), Qty** (the columns the item asks; Mfg and Expiry hidden when `off`). Enter moves along the row; Enter on Qty adds a row while quantity remains and finishes when it is spent; the expiry accepts the forms of 3.4 and, with a shelf life on the item, proposes `mfg + shelfLifeDays`; MRP is proposed from the previous batch of the item. A name that exists with different attributes raises R12's choice inline.

**Serial (inward and outward): the unit grid.**
```
┌ Units · Samsung A15 128GB Black · Main Location · quantity 20 ────────────── Close ┐
│ Scan, type or paste: [______________________________]            14 of 20           │
│   #  IMEI 1            IMEI 2            Cost        Status                          │
│   1  356938035643809   356938035643817   10,450.00   ok                              │
│  14  356938035644021   —                 10,450.00   check digit differs (warning)   │
│ (windowed: only the visible rows exist as elements)                                  │
│ Range: type  first x count  (356938035643809 x 20)   Alt+D deletes the row           │
│ Enter adds · ↑ from the field edits a row (F2/Enter a cell, Esc back) · Ctrl+A done  │
│ 6 short: Done saves 14 and leaves 6 pending identities                [ Done ] [ Close ] │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```
Every entry (scan, typed, pasted) is judged **at once** by R1, R3, R9 and the TAC prompt, and each row carries a status chip (`Alt+D` deletes the highlighted row without a prompt, since a row can be scanned again; several selected rows ask first); a wrong row is corrected **in place** (the cell is editable; the edit is re-judged as a new entry, and if the unit has moved on the screen lists the later documents) so correcting one identifier never costs four screens [R-bst 6.2 rule 9]. Rules of the panel:

1. **A counter, not a prompt:** "14 of 20". The panel never closes itself when the count is reached (a stray extra scan must not be lost) and never accepts the voucher.
2. **Duplicates:** a second scan of the unit already in the grid is ignored with the soft tone and a flash of its row (the gun was pulled twice); a unit in stock already (inward), or not in stock or sold elsewhere (outward), is a red row naming the document and date.
3. **Paste** (`core/unitId.ts parsePaste`): split on newline, comma, tab and spaces; trim; drop blank lines; skip a header row (a non-id first line); a line of two tokens is a pair when the item keeps a second identifier; 20,000 rows at most; all rows judged by the rules above; rows are added in one batch and **rendered windowed** (the 10,000-row budget of 3.7).
4. **Range and auto-generate:** the shorthand `first x count` (also `first*count`, `first+count`) typed in the entry field previews the first and last generated id and, on Enter, adds them; the IMEI case steps the body and recomputes the check digit (3.3). **Auto-generate** is for a non-IMEI item with a `serialPattern`: `auto x N` (or its button) fills the next N ids after the highest existing number of that prefix in the company (derived from the units, never a stored counter; a collision between two offline devices is caught by R1); an **IMEI is never generated**, since the maker allocates it and the range tool only extends a real first IMEI.
5. **Per-unit extras sit in the row, not in a second dialog:** cost (inward), MRP when `mrpAt` is `unit`, the item's attribute columns (colour, storage, HUID, weights), and a note; the columns shown are the ones the item turns on.
6. **A wedge scanner works with no setup:** the entry field is an ordinary input, a scanner types the code and Enter, and Enter adds the row. The opt-in scan layer (section 14) only adds protection against a burst landing elsewhere and learns the scanner's habits.
7. **An outward scan** looks the unit up: found in stock at this godown, the row shows the model, batch, expiry, cost and warranty and is added; otherwise the row says why (sold on INV-87 to R. Kumar on 12-Oct; in another godown; not in stock) and adds nothing.
8. **Esc and a click outside keep scanned work** (rule B5): with rows in the grid, Esc and Close ask "Discard 14 units?" with **No armed**; an empty panel closes at once; the panel registers a `state/dirty.ts` probe so leaving the voucher asks too.
9. **The panel owns its keys while open:** Enter never reaches Narration or the Accept bar; the screen's chords (including Alt+2) are inert behind it as behind Go To.
10. **Done writes rows back:** the first allocation takes the typed row; every further cell is a **new row inserted directly beneath** (same item, same godown, the rate carried, a different `batchName` / MRP / quantity / units), so one grid row is still one `InventoryLine` and `validInvLines`, totals, tax and every downstream reader are untouched. A **batchSerial** item opens the batch grid first and then, for each batch row, the unit grid for that quantity (a sale of units from two batches becomes two lines, each with its units).
11. **Dual-SIM pair mode** (an item with `second: true`, which exists only for `serialKind: 'imei'`; the Mobile preset turns it on). Nearly every handset box carries **two IMEI barcodes** (the example of this spec is `...809` and `...817`, whose 14-digit bodies differ by 1), and the first text took one scan to be one row, so scanning both barcodes of 20 boxes with Quantity left blank made **40 rows, quantity 40 and a doubled purchase value**, with IMEI 2 of each phone a separate in-stock unit that passed R1, R9 and the TAC prompt, so a phone could later be sold once by IMEI 1 and once by IMEI 2 with nothing noticing. Therefore: each row takes **two consecutive scans** (the first is IMEI 1; the row then reads "waiting for IMEI 2" and the next scan completes it; **Esc** on the waiting row skips it for a single-SIM unit). A scan that is Luhn-valid, has the **same TAC** and whose **14-digit body differs by at most 3** from the body of an existing row that has no IMEI 2 raises **"IMEI 2 of row 5? Enter attaches it, Esc adds it as another unit"** (Enter attaches: the safe reading; it also guards an item whose `second` is off, and a carton whose phones really are consecutive is one Esc away, [R-bst 3.1.1]). **The counter counts units, not scans** ("14 units, 28 scans"), and a blank Quantity takes the unit count, so 20 boxes scanned twice each give 20. The Data Check kind `unit-adjacent-imei` lists units whose IMEI body is within 3 of another unit's on the same purchase line (a possible ghost). Recipe `trk-purchase-dual-sim` scans 20 boxes with two barcodes each, with Quantity blank and with Quantity typed.
12. **Done, edit and the mouse.** The panel has a visible **Done** button distinct from **Close** (Close with rows asks "Discard 14 units?", so before this a mouse-only user could not commit); **Up** from the empty entry field moves to the last row, **F2 or Enter** edits its highlighted cell, **Esc** returns to the entry field (so a wrong row 117 of 200 is reachable by keyboard, which the first text only said was "editable"); every row action has a click target.

### 6.4 What the row shows: the chip, always

A tracked row draws, under or beside the item name, a chip that is **always visible** (a carried value must be [CLAUDE.md Phase 20 to 21]): `B-2031 · 08/2027 · MRP 22.50` for a batch line, `12 units · 356938…809 +11` for a serial line (`5 pending` in amber when short), both together for batchSerial; a held batch or a rule finding adds a dot with its words. Clicking the chip or `Alt+B` reopens the panel. The chip is part of the voucher screen's row rendering (CF-3 adds line-field cells there; the chip and CF's cells share the row's sub-line, section 15 and Appendix R). The printed invoice prints the same facts (8.2).

**Clearing.** Changing the row's item, godown or direction clears its allocation (today `pickItem` leaves the old item's batch on the row, a bug [R-aud 3.3]); editing the quantity so it no longer equals the allocation clears the allocation and reopens the panel on the next Enter; a duplicate (Alt+2) and a ghost recall drop units and an outward batch (R8).

### 6.5 Quantity, pending identities and the Accept bar

For a serial item the quantity is the unit count (R4). At Done, an inward panel with fewer units than the quantity offers one Enter to save "14 units, 6 pending identities" (only where `pendingOk`); the row's chip says `6 pending`; the **Accept bar lists every line that is short** ("Samsung A15: 6 identities pending"), and the voucher saves. After Accept, the toast says "20 units received, 14 identified, 6 pending" with the **Print labels** action when the voucher type has `labelsAfterSave`. Pending units are unsaleable (a sale needs identified units, or adopts a slot with a real id, 4.3) and appear in the Pending Identities report (9).

### 6.6 The other screens that write lines

- **Bulk Entry** keeps refusing a tracked item in v1 ("enter it on the voucher screen") and gains batch and serial columns in T-8 (a `;`-separated serial column, the same rules through the funnel and the `session` set).
- **Stock Journal** (transfers, conversions, write-offs): the source section is an outward panel; the **destination section's row for the same item starts from the source row** (tagged "from source line"): batch, MRP, expiry and every unit are mirrored and the destination godown is the only thing typed (CF's rule for a variant, widened [CF AD.3.4]). A destination row for a **different** item (manufacture, a storage-size conversion) opens the inward panel; an IMEI-kind unit may be re-itemised by listing the same id on a receipt of the other item (the unit key is company-wide for an IMEI, T11).
- **Physical Stock:** choosing a tracked item and godown **pre-fills one row per held cell** at its book quantity (CF's rule: an untouched row moves nothing; a row deleted by hand counts 0 and the screen says so before Accept), saved with `countPart` and `eod` (the screen says **"counted as at the end of <date>"**); for a serial item it opens the **stock-take by scan** (7.6). **An alteration of a count written before this feature** (no `countPart`, no `eod`, one line per batch) **loads its lines exactly as stored, says "written before cell counts: lines as stored", and never adds `countPart` or `eod`**, so pressing Accept on an old per-batch count cannot retroactively change a closed period's quantity (the first text pre-filled per held cell on every open, which would have flipped a closed period from 7 to 12).
- **Manufacturing Journal:** component lines open the outward panel (FEFO proposal), the output opens the inward panel with an **auto-named batch** and generated or pending serials (7.8).
- **Credit and Debit Notes:** the panel opens in return mode (7.3, 7.4).

### 6.7 Search and the item picker

The item picker keeps matching name, alias and part number by substring. A tracked item's picker row adds "in stock 40 · 3 batches" from the same `positions` memo the screen already builds (the cell resolver is passed only when a tracked item exists, so a company with none runs today's call). A **scanned code** takes the exact-lookup path of section 14; an unknown code says so (today a typed code that matches nothing does nothing at all and the next characters append [R-aud 3.3]).

---

## 7. Workflows, voucher by voucher

Each row says what a correct system does; the rule numbers are section 5's.

### 7.1 Purchase, Receipt Note, opening

Inward panel (6.3). A batch item: batch, dates, MRP, quantity per batch, one row per batch of the supplier's bill (a line per batch). A serial item: scan, paste or range the units; the count equals the quantity or the rest are pending (R28); `cost` per unit defaults to the line's rate; a **used** unit gets `attrs.condition` and the IMEI-check stamp (R22). A **purchase warranty** (a `w` entry of type `purchase`) is entered only when the supplier gives one (a sale warranty is derived from the item's rule, R21, and is not stored per unit). Saving a Purchase whose units already sit in stock is refused (R1) unless the earlier chain left them (a customer return or trade-in re-enters as a new inward).

### 7.2 Sale, Delivery Note, POS-style counter billing

Outward panel. Batch: the proposal pre-selected (FEFO, `minLifeDays`, hold and expiry excluded), rolled over a short batch with a visible split, the batch's `salePrice` pre-filled when the item says so, the MRP printed and the ceiling checked (R26). Serial: scan the box; the unit must be in stock at this godown and saleable (R3); the line's quantity is the count; the sale warranty end is **derived** from the item's rule and the voucher date (nothing is stored per unit unless it is an exception, R21); a scanned IMEI 2 is stored as the unit's IMEI 1 (3.3); the invoice prints the IMEI (both for a dual SIM) and the warranty end (8.2). A typed batch name that does not exist is refused (R27; a Warn on a Soft item, and a name that exists in another godown is offered, not refused). A sale of a unit already delivered on a note is made with `billsNote` (7.10).

### 7.3 Sales return (Credit Note, Receipt Note in)

The inward panel in **return mode**. Serial: scanning a unit **finds its sale** (by either identifier), shows the customer, date, price and warranty end and fills the line; the unit must have been sold to **this party** (R5), otherwise the row says "never sold to R. Kumar (sold to M/s Verma on 3-Sep)" and adds nothing; the receiving godown is chosen on the line (a quarantine godown makes the unit **Held (Returned)** until a Stock Journal restocks, refurbishes or sends it back; the Main Location makes it saleable at once). A different handset of the same model cannot come back as the sold one: the IMEI in hand must match. **The returned unit re-enters stock at the cost it left at** for a `Specific` item (R30, 4.5 rule 2), never at the credit note's line amount (the sale price). A unit this company has **no record of** (sold before a Split Company Data cut) is accepted with a Warn and a note (R5, 4.3, 4.6). Batch: the batch defaults to one **sold to this party** for the item (net of earlier returns); a returned lot **keeps its original batch** (Zoho gives it a new reference [R-bst 6.4]; keeping it lets a pharmacy answer "how many of batch X did we sell and take back"); a damaged or expired return goes to the quarantine godown or is credit-only (no stock line) as the person chooses.

### 7.4 Purchase return (Debit Note), rejection, DOA

Outward panel in **supplier-return mode**: expiry, hold and recall never block (R6); the cell or unit must be in stock; a unit leaves as `rejected` (returned to supplier), which is also the DOA state. **Expiry returns** (the largest recurring pharmacy job) are made against the supplier from the **Expiring and Expired** report (9), whose rows carry "Return to supplier" and fill a Debit Note's lines.

### 7.5 Godown transfer

Stock Journal (6.6): the destination row **mirrors** the source row's cell and units; R16 forbids a destination line without them (**a carton booked with pending identities may still be transferred: the source line carries fewer rows than its quantity, the shortfall moves as dated pending slots that keep their original dates, R4, 4.3**); value is neutral by the existing transfer rule (a receiving line with no rate is valued at what the same voucher issued) and the unit's `cost` travels. A transfer to a `transit` godown is "in transit"; the receiving voucher (another Stock Journal from the transit godown) completes it. The batch moves with its **MRP, expiry and status** (they are the batch's, not the godown's).

### 7.6 Physical Stock and the stock-take by scan

**Batch items:** per cell (the count group), as 6.6. **Serial items:** the **Stock Take** screen (a data-entry screen, `stockTake`): choose godown (a location code can be scanned) and optionally an item or brand; scan every unit on the shelf; each scan is looked up and shown; at the end the screen lists **Missing** (the books hold the unit here, the scan did not find it) and **Extra** (scanned, the books say it is elsewhere or unknown), each needing a **reason** before Accept (a missing one: lost, theft, sold unbilled, in service, other; an extra one: received unbilled, moved from another godown, other). Accept writes **one Physical Stock voucher per godown, with one group of lines for each item counted** (a 300-model stock take is one voucher, one number, one server reserve and **one undo step**, tagged `origin: 'flag'` as every bulk writer must be; the first text wrote 300 vouchers and burned 300 numbers), every line `eod` (4.2): `serials` = the present units, `missing` = the absent ones with reasons, quantity = the present units **plus the pending identities the person confirms** (unidentified stock is counted by typing a number, since it cannot be scanned) (R17, 4.7). Batch items without serials can be taken the same way by scanning the item or batch code (each scan raises the cell's count). A stock take is **saved as a draft in the app's per-device storage** while it runs (a lost battery must not lose 300 scans); nothing reaches the books until Accept. **One counter per godown at a time, said on the screen:** a Physical Stock count is a whole-item count of a godown, the draft is device-local, and two clerks counting different aisles of one godown on two devices would each write a count that lists the other's aisle as missing (the later count replaces the earlier). The screen therefore says "count one godown from one device; for two counters, count different godowns or merge the scans on one device", and **Accept warns (Continue armed No) when a count of the same item at the same godown on the same date already exists** ("Physical Stock 3 counts these items here today; this count replaces it"). A shared draft keyed by godown that merges scans from two devices is **not built in v1** (section 18). The **Count Differences** report (9) lists open drafts and the differences of past counts by reason.

### 7.7 Stock Journal: write-off, conversion, scrap

A Stock Journal that issues units and receives nothing is a **write-off** (status `scrapped`, with the voucher's narration as the reason; the screen asks for one); a journal that issues one item and receives another is a **conversion or manufacture** (status `consumed` for the issued units). A conversion that re-itemises a unit (a phone re-listed under a different storage size) lists the same IMEI on the receipt; the chain (unit key company-wide for an IMEI) continues unbroken **and the unit carries its cost to the receiving item** (A falls and B rises by the same amount; under `Specific` the cost ledger does it, otherwise the existing transfer rule values the receipt at what the voucher issued, 4.5 rule 4); a non-IMEI serial re-itemised is a new unit of the receiving item and is costed by the line.

### 7.8 Manufacturing and job work

The Manufacturing Journal builds a Stock Journal: **components** are outward lines allocated by the FEFO proposal in the outward panel (a component's batch or units chosen as for a sale); the **output** is an inward line whose batch is **named by the item's `autoBatch` pattern** (`PFX-YYMMDD` or a sequence derived from the highest existing number, never stored), `mfgDate` = the voucher date and `expiryDate` = that plus `shelfLifeDays`, or generated serials (`serialPattern`) or pending identities to be filled when the units are labelled. **Genealogy is the voucher** (T15): the Batch Trace report links every output cell of a manufacturing voucher to every component cell it consumed; for a voucher with a single output the link is **certain**, for several outputs it is "possible: all outputs of this voucher" (the engine does not know which input lot went into which output lot, and says so). **Job work** (T15): a godown of kind `jobwork` holds stock sent to a worker (still ours, not saleable, listed in its own balance); the finished lot comes back through a Manufacturing Journal whose components are issued from that godown and whose output is a new batch (the worker's production batch may be typed as the batch name). No job-work chain, challan or order is built here (parity rows `material-in`, `material-out`, `jobwork-godown-type` stay missing).

### 7.9 Alter, cancel, delete, undo

R7 checks every alteration, cancellation (`Alt+X`) and deletion against the units and cells it touches and **lists the dependants**; a cancelled voucher is a shell with no lines (`voucherCancel.ts`), so its units and cells are released with no code, and the **server ledger is released on the same push** (10.2: events are replaced per voucher, a shell has none). Ctrl+Z reverses the document and so the allocation. Mark Optional / Regular runs the checks on regular (R18).

### 7.10 The Delivery Note / Receipt Note link (T14, optional)

A Sales or Debit line (or Purchase or Credit line for a Receipt Note) may carry `billsNote`: the id of a Delivery Note (Receipt Note) that already moved those units or that batch quantity. **`stockMovements` skips a line with `billsNote`** (it is billing only; the one generator every stock figure reads, so every report follows), the ledger and GST posting of the invoice are untouched, and R15 checks that the units belong to the note and the party. The entry is a picker, **"Bill delivered items"**, on an outward invoice that lists the party's undelivered-billed notes and fills lines from them. It writes nothing on a company that never uses it, adds no key to any existing voucher and is the one deliberate change to `stockMovements` besides CF's count merge (lane I order, section 15); a company that does not build it keeps today's double count and says so in its release note.

### 7.11 Warranty, replacement, service

A unit's warranty is data on its rows (3.1): the **purchase** warranty on the inward row, the **sale** (and optional **installation**) warranty on the outward row, each with its start (`invoice` or `activation`), months and end; "activation" leaves the start empty and the Warranty report lists "start not set" units; setting it is an alteration of the sale voucher's row with a reason (R21), done from the unit's history screen. **Service:** transferring the unit to a `service` godown is "with service centre", and the same transfer back ends it. **Replacement:** the replacement unit enters on a Receipt Note or Purchase with `replaces: <old id>`, **copies the old unit's remaining warranty**, and the old unit's history ends `rejected` or `scrapped`; the history screen links the two (T5, [R-vert 6.9 rows 2 and 22]).

---

## 8. GST, e-invoice and print

### 8.1 The e-invoice (`core/einvoice.ts`, one writer; the schema facts are [R-vert 3.2], read from NIC's sandbox page and the notified form)

The schema has, per item, a barcode (3 to 30 characters), **one** product serial number (1 to 20), batch details (a name of 3 to 20 characters, the only mandatory member; an expiry and a warranty date, `DD/MM/YYYY`), attribute pairs (name and value, each 1 to 100) and a cap of **1,000** lines per invoice; the printed date pattern for expiry and warranty admits only the years **2010 to 2029**. Comsky sends today only a batch name and expiry, silently drops a name outside 3 to 20 characters, never checks `ExpDt`, and never sends the others [C `einvoice.ts` `buildInv01`, the validator]. Decisions:

1. **Group the lines.** One invoice line per item and cell, however many units; the unit list is printed on the invoice annexure (8.2), not sent. A 1,200-handset dispatch must not become 1,200 lines (the cap, and the e-way bill warns above 250 lines [C `ewb.ts`]).
2. **`BchDtls`**: `Nm` only for a trimmed name of 3 to 20 characters; **`ExpDt` only when the year is 2010 to 2029**, otherwise omitted. `buildInv01` gains an `omitted: { line, field, why }[]` member that **the e-invoice preview prints** ("Batch B1 left out: under 3 characters"; "Expiry 08/2031 left out: the schema accepts years to 2029"), and the batch grid warns at capture when a name will not go. The checker's year range for `ExpDt` is aligned to the schema's. Never a substitute value, never an invented date.
3. **`WrDt` is not sent**: the schema describes it only as "Warranty Date" and does not say whether it is a start or an end [R-vert 3.2]; the preview says "warranty date not sent". It is revisited with an IRP-sandbox test (owner question).
4. **`PrdSlNo`** only in the **optional one-line-per-unit expansion** (`buildInv01` option, chosen on the e-invoice screen for a buyer that demands it, allowed only when the expanded count is at most 1,000; each expanded line quantity 1, `PrdSlNo` = the id when at most 20 characters, the second IMEI as an attribute `IMEI2`); never by default.
5. **`Barcde`** = the item's primary `ItemBarcode` when it is 3 to 30 characters (the one field that costs nothing, [R-vert 3.2 consequence 4]).
6. GSTR-1 Table 12 and the HSN summary aggregate quantity by HSN, unit and rate and are indifferent to line grouping; no GST engine changes.
7. **No double quote and no backslash in any string field.** The schema forbids both in every string field (`Barcde`, `PrdSlNo`, `BchDtls.Nm`, the attribute names and values included, re-fetched in the review pass). `einvoice.ts` already has `BAD_CHARS` and `clean()`, and uses them for the party and product fields, but the batch writer sends `Nm: l.batchName.trim()` unchecked and its validator checks only the length [C `einvoice.ts` `buildInv01`, the `BchDtls` check]. So the writers of `Nm`, `Barcde`, `PrdSlNo` and the `IMEI2` attribute value **go through `clean()`**, the checker applies `BAD_CHARS` to each, and a value that contains one is left out with the visible note **"left out: contains a quote or a backslash"** (an entry of `omitted`); a minted code or a typed batch such as `B-17"A` can no longer make the IRP reject the invoice.

### 8.2 The printed invoice (`ui/print/invoiceModel.ts`, `PrintVoucher.tsx`, `core/printConfig.ts`)

- **Batch line** (exists: `Batch: X  ·  Exp. date`, switchable) becomes `Batch: B-2031  ·  Exp. 08/2027  ·  MRP 22.50` where the item keeps MRP; the MRP is printed with its number only (the pack wording "inclusive of all taxes" is on the label, 13.8); a new switch `show.mrp` (default **off** for every existing company, on in the tracking presets). **For an item with a `schedule` (C, H or H1) the batch line also prints the manufacturer** (`Batch: B-2031 · Exp. 08/2027 · MRP 22.50 · Mfr: <manufacturer>`), because Drugs Rules 65(3)(1)(f) wants the manufacturer, batch and expiry in the record of such a supply [W]. **That does not make the invoice the Rule 65(3) record**: the rule's entry also carries the prescriber's name and address, the patient's name and address, the serial number of the prescription-register entry and the pharmacist's signature, and the memo-book route is available only to a licensee who opted for it in writing at licensing (65(3)(2)) [W]; Comsky v1 captures none of the prescriber, patient or register data, so the print says nothing to that effect and **the screens and section 18 say plainly that the prescription register is not modelled**.
- **Serial line:** `IMEI: 356938035643809, 356938035643817` as `.sub` strings **chunked to at most about 60 characters each**, so one row stays short (a row is atomic and never split across pages [C `paginate.ts`; R-aud 3.6]); more than **6 units** on a line prints `12 units: see annexure` and adds a **second page, "Serial number annexure"** (a `PagedDoc` page of the units in columns, with item, batch and warranty end), so a 40-IMEI invoice is never one tall unbreakable row. A dual-SIM unit prints both identifiers on one entry. New switches `show.serial` (default on for a tracked item) and `show.warranty` (the warranty end per unit, default on where the item has one); nothing prints for an untracked item and nothing changes for a company with the feature off.
- **The annexure and the labels never print an id the owner did not choose to print**: printing identifiers on an invoice is an item-level default plus a Print Configuration switch ("never print wording or bank details the owner did not choose", CLAUDE.md).

### 8.3 Exports

A serial, IMEI or barcode column is **text**, left-aligned: `reportSheet.ts` types a right-aligned cell of up to 15 digits as a number [C `reportSheet.ts` `groupedOk`], which would write an IMEI to Excel as a number; every tracking report declares its id columns `text`, the `.xlsx` writer stores them as shared strings (it already writes every string as a shared string [R-lab 6.2]), and the CSV export of a tracked report shows a one-line note ("Excel may show long numbers in scientific form: use the Excel export") because a CSV cannot carry a type. `core/csvOut.ts` keeps defusing `= + - @`.

---

## 9. The reports (Reports Center rows; pure engine plus a thin screen each)

**Principle.** The report grid (`core/listQuery.ts`, `ListTable`) is not built [R-aud 3.16], so these reports are **bespoke screens built the way the Dashboard v2 engines were: a pure engine in `src/core/trackReports/` that returns typed rows and totals, a thin screen that draws a `.scr-body` table** (so Print, CSV and Excel work through the existing engine), no logic in the screen. When the grid lands each screen is re-ported over the same engine rows. Every report **ties to the figure it stands beside, to the paisa or to the unit, and says its basis in a line under the title** (the Phase 26 and 28 gotchas). Each has F12 options (as-on date or period, godown, item or stock group, supplier, show zero rows) and a **scan target** search box (a scanned unit, batch or item code filters the report).

| # | Reports Center row | Screen (mode) | The question | Main columns | Ties to (asserted at 2k and 50k) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Batch-wise Stock** | `batchSummary` (stock) | what batches do I hold, where, how much, expiring when | item, batch, godown, MRP, mfg, expiry, days left, tier, quantity, rate, **value at batch cost**, **book value share**, status | per item: cells sum to the Stock Summary closing quantity and the share sums to its value to the paisa (Unallocated rows included, CF AD.3.5); per godown to Godown Summary |
| 2 | **Expiry Ageing** | `batchSummary` (ageing) | what do I act on this month | buckets expired, 0 to 30, 31 to 60, 61 to 90, 91 to 180, later, no expiry; by item or supplier | the buckets plus "no expiry" sum to row 1's total |
| 3 | **Expiring and Expired** | `batchSummary` (expiring) | what to return, by supplier and store | date range, supplier, store; "Return to supplier" fills a Debit Note (R6) | subset of row 1 |
| 4 | **FEFO Pick List** | `batchSummary` (fefo) | what should be sold first | per item and godown, the cells in the order `proposeAllocation` would draw them, days left, a "push" flag inside the warning days | the same function the picker calls (one engine) |
| 5 | **Unbatched Stock** | `batchSummary` (unbatched) | where is batch data missing (Busy's "Batch On Account") | stock in the "no batch" cell of a batch item, and sale lines with no batch | `Σ cells` including the no-batch cell equals the item |
| 6 | **Batch Trace and Recall** | `batchTrace` | who got batch X, where did it come from, and stop it | **backward**: supplier bill, receipt date, manufacturing voucher and component cells; **forward**: stock held by godown, parties, invoices, quantities, contact phone and email, returns, onward lots; buttons **Hold**, **Recall**, **Release** (3.4) and **Print recall list** | forward quantities plus stock held plus returns equal the received quantity; `forward(backward(x))` contains `x` |
| 7 | **Serial Stock** | `serialStock` (in stock) | what do I hold, by item, godown, age | unit, item, godown, status, days since received, cost, warranty end | identified units in stock plus pending equal the cell quantity and the Stock Summary quantity |
| 8 | **Pending Identities** | `serialStock` (pending) | what is not yet scannable | purchase line, supplier, date, quantity, identified, pending | `Σ pending` equals the difference of row 7's identity |
| 9 | **Unit History** | `unitHistory` | where is this unit, who bought it, is it in warranty | a lookup (IMEI 1 or 2, serial, batch, customer name, voucher no); the timeline: supplier bill, moves, sale (customer, invoice, price), returns, replacement link, status, warranty | every event names a voucher that opens |
| 10 | **Warranty** | `warranty` | what ends this month, what is under claim, what has no start | due in a range, expired, start not set, in service godown | units by warranty status equal the units sold |
| 11 | **Unit Profit** | `trackProfit` (unit) | margin per phone | unit, sale price (the line amount shared by units), the unit's own cost, margin | sum of margins equals sales less unit costs of the units listed; a line **"books use Avg. Cost: difference X"** where the item is not `Specific` |
