| 12 | **Batch Profit** | `trackProfit` (batch) | which lots earn | batch, quantity sold, sales value, quantity x batch purchase rate, margin | per item: sales equal the register's lines |
| 13 | **Count Differences** | `countDiff` | what was missing or extra, why, and what is still open | open stock-take drafts; past counts by godown, item, unit, reason; the old multi-line counts | missing plus extra equals the count vouchers' deltas |
| 14 | **Tracking Exceptions** | the Data Check, filtered | where is the data wrong | the kinds of 5 (duplicate unit, sold before received, count mismatch, pending, negative cell with a suggested re-balancing Stock Journal, expired in stock, unbatched, legacy box, merged names, old counts, duplicate barcode, short serial line, adjacent IMEIs, counted then sold, opening cells ignored or pairing assumed, units at an assumed cost, method coerced, first-of-month legacy expiries, `Specific` items on older builds) | each row opens the record |

**Drill and entry screens** (not Reports Center rows): `batchLedger` (the movements of one batch: Enter on a row of row 1, Tally's "Batch Vouchers" generalised), `stockTake` (7.6), `labelPrint` (13). **Dashboard widgets** (an optional T-4 item): *Expiring soon*, *Pending identities*, *Serials in stock* are registry rows plus a compute file under `ui/dash/widgets/compute/`, hidden widgets never computed [R-aud 3.5].

**New-screen checklist, once, by one writer** (CLAUDE.md and [R-aud 3.5]): the `Screen` union (`store.ts`), `router.tsx` route and crumb, `MenuScreen.tsx`, `modern/CommandPalette.tsx`, `overlays.tsx` Go To (late commands), `core/access.ts SCREEN_AREA` (`batchSummary` is already `inventory`; `batchTrace`, `serialStock`, `unitHistory`, `warranty`, `trackProfit`, `countDiff`: `reports`; `stockTake` and `labelPrint`: `inventory`), `state/recents.ts`, `ui/reportsCenter/catalogue.ts` (the 14 rows; `batchLedger` goes on `EXCLUDED_REPORT_SCREENS` with its reason), `ui/navTree.ts` plus the literal table in `sidenav.assert.ts` (four rows added under Inventory: Batch and Expiry, Serial Numbers, Stock Take, Print Labels; the table moves from 65 to 69; the stub allow-list in `sidenav.assert.ts` becomes empty and the suite says so), `ui/quickCreate.ts` (a "Stock Take" create and a "Print Labels" page action), `state/dirty.ts` probes for `stockTake` and `labelPrint`. Replacing the stub means `BatchSummary.tsx`, `router.tsx`, the three entry points (Gateway > Inventory Books > B, Go To, the palette) and the parity rows (Appendix P) change **in one commit**; the three entry points keep working throughout.

## 10. Sync, the distributed uniqueness problem, older clients, the server

### 10.1 The honest guarantee

| Situation | What can be promised |
|---|---|
| One open window of one device (online or not) | **Everything, within that window's book.** A local scan of its own book sees every unit; R1, R3, R10 block at Accept and at the write funnel. A restore replaces the book. **The guarantee is per open window, not per device**: the funnel is synchronous over the window's in-memory store and the app has no cross-window data coordination, so a second tab or Electron window on the same profile holds its own copy and could sell the same IMEI. `ensureFresh()` closes it (5, caller 2): at Accept and at the start of a bulk writer's session the window checks the outbox for rows it did not write and reloads first; a two-tab recipe pins it. |
| Two devices, both online | **Near-hard, with slice T-9b built:** the reserve at Accept (10.3) asks the cloud whether a unit is out by a voucher this device has not seen; a unit another device has just taken is refused at the second device. **Without T-9b these two devices have detection (T-9a) only**, at the next push. |
| A device on an older build | **Nothing can be forced.** An older build has no allocation panel, no R4 and no funnel: it saves a Sales line for a serial item with quantity 5 and no units, and the chain still shows those units in stock, so a second counter on the new build can sell one of those physical IMEIs undetected. The layer detects what it can and says so (10.5): device capabilities on the server (`sync_device.caps`), a refusal to switch tracking on while a device seen in the last 30 days lacks `trk1`, a new build's post-pull scan that records `unit-unrecorded`, and the Data Check kind `serial-line-short`. |
| Two devices, one offline | **Only** "this device's own book has no earlier sale". Both Accepts succeed. At the next sync the clash is **detected and recorded, never silently resolved, renumbered or refused** (10.2). A company that cannot accept that sets `trackingOffline: 'block'` (a synced company's devices then cannot sell serial units while the cloud is unreachable). |
| After both sync | **Knowable:** both vouchers are in the server's change log; the invoice may already be printed and handed over, so the resolution is an ordinary credit note, `Alt+X` or a corrected identifier (10.7). |
| Any writer that is not the voucher screen | Covered by the write-funnel guard on the device (5, caller 2) and by the server index (REST included, 10.2). A sync **pull** and a **restore** bypass the guard (as `writeGuard` does) and are caught by the post-pull scan and the Data Check. |
| Order | `base` tells concurrent from knowing: if the other sale's seq is above the pusher's base the pusher could not have seen it (an offline clash); at or below, it saw it (a deliberate re-sale after a return it also holds). The server records both and labels which. |

### 10.2 Detection (slice T-9a, mandatory)

**A server table, written inside `/sync/push`'s tenant lock after the change is planned** (`planPush` then the log insert, in the restructured `pushRun.js` of the CF worktree): `backend/sql/009_unit_events.sql`, idempotent and guarded like 008, RLS on `app.tenant_id`. The same migration adds `sync_device.caps` (10.5) and the lease table of 10.3.

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS unit_events (
  tenant_id  uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenant(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  cid        text NOT NULL,
  ukey       text NOT NULL,                 -- one identifier's key (3.3), built from the LINE's own serialKind snapshot, never from the item
  uid        text NOT NULL,                 -- the unit's canonical key: the primary identifier's ukey. An IMEI 2 row shares the uid of its IMEI 1
  voucher_id text NOT NULL,
  line_no    int  NOT NULL,
  item_id    text NOT NULL,
  godown_id  text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  dir        text NOT NULL CHECK (dir IN ('in','out','move','present','absent')),
  base       text NOT NULL,
  party_id   text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  vdate      date NOT NULL,
  vord       bigint NOT NULL,               -- the voucher's number: a TIE-BREAK ONLY (rule W, 4.3), never what decides validity
  eod        boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,-- a count event: last within its date
  seq        bigint NOT NULL,               -- the change_log seq of the push that wrote it
  PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, cid, voucher_id, line_no, ukey)
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_unit_events_uid  ON unit_events (tenant_id, cid, uid);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_unit_events_ukey ON unit_events (tenant_id, cid, ukey);
-- RLS policy unit_events_isolation (same shape as voucher_series_isolation)
```
- **Writing: from the STORED payload, never the pushed body.** The events are derived **from what is about to be inserted into `change_log`**: `plan.inserts[i].payload` and `ins.op` of the restructured `pushRun.js` [C `.wt-cf` `pushRun.js`: the insert loop uses `ins.op` and `ins.payload`], **after** the carry of 10.5 (the serials an older writer dropped are back) and **after** `conflict.js` has swapped in a cancelled shell. (The first text said "a pushed put whose payload has serials": an older client's narration edit that drops the serials of line 1 while line 2 keeps its list would then delete and reinsert, and line 1's units would vanish from the index although the carry restored them in `change_log`.) For a `vouchers` row whose stored payload has any line with `serials` (or `missing`) and which is neither `optional` nor `cancelled`: **delete every row of that `(tenant, cid, voucher_id)` and insert the new ones** (idempotent: a re-push replaces); for a delete, a cancelled shell or an optional voucher: delete the rows (**a cancel or delete releases the units on the same push**, 7.9). A voucher with `billsNote` lines writes none for them. The events come from `unitChain.eventsOf(voucher)` in `backend/src/unitChain.js`, the same code the app runs; **a row with `id2` writes two events (the `ukey` of `id` and of `id2`) that share `uid = ukey(id)`**.
- **Every writer indexes.** There are **three `change_log` writers in the tree**: `sync.js` push, `rest.js` (two inserts) and `scripts/seed/to-sql.mjs` [C grep]. `indexUnits(client, tenantId, inserts)` is called from each of them (the seed script emits the same rows as SQL, or is followed by the rebuild job below), and **the drift suite is extended**: `syncconflict.assert` already counts `INSERT INTO change_log` occurrences as a drift guard [C `syncconflict.assert.ts`, `allInserts`] and nothing extended it; a new rule fails if any `INSERT INTO change_log` site is not followed by `indexUnits`.
- **Statement shape and the lock budget.** `unit_events` has 15 columns; a statement of one row per event would stop at Postgres's 65,535 bind parameters (about 4,300 rows) while a voucher may hold 5,000 unit rows (10,000 events with `id2`). So events are inserted with **`INSERT ... SELECT ... FROM unnest($1::text[], $2::text[], ...)` in chunks of at most 5,000 events** (one array parameter per column), and the clash check reads **one set query per chunk**, `SELECT ... FROM unit_events WHERE tenant_id = $1 AND cid = $2 AND uid = ANY($3::text[])`, after one set query that resolves the touched `ukey`s to their `uid`s (`ukey = ANY(...)`); the clash logic then runs in memory. (The first text said "one indexed read per touched unit key": up to 10,000 sequential round trips inside `lockTenantLog`, the lock every push, both REST writes and the seed script take.) **Budget:** one push indexes **at most 50,000 events**; vouchers past it are accepted and not indexed (below) so a restore page of large vouchers cannot hold the tenant lock for seconds.
- **Aliases on the server.** A pushed outward row names whatever identifier was scanned; its `uid` is resolved by the set query above (an existing event with that `ukey` gives its `uid`), else it is its own. When a purchase row later arrives that introduces the alias (`id2`), one `UPDATE unit_events SET uid = ... WHERE ukey = ...` re-points the earlier sale, so a phone sold on device A by IMEI 1 and on device B by IMEI 2 is found as one `uid` with two `out`s whichever order the pushes arrive in (vector in Appendix V, suite `tracksync`).
- **Detecting.** After the writes, for every touched `uid` (including those of rows a delete or a cancelled shell just removed: `DELETE ... RETURNING uid`, so a clash that this push removed is seen and its row auto-resolves, 10.7), load its events, arrange each date by **`orderUnitEvents`** (rule W: `vdate`, `vord`, `line_no`, `party_id`, `base`, `dir` and `eod` are exactly its inputs) and run `findClashes`; each `soldTwice` or `receivedTwice` upserts a **`sync_conflict` row of kind `duplicate-serial`** (`dedupe_key = unit:<cid>:<uid>:<voucherA>:<voucherB>`, so a clash noticed by two devices is one row; `details` = both voucher ids and numbers, dates, parties, the pusher's `base` against the other's `seq` (concurrent or knowing), device labels), `detected_by: 'server'`. `KINDS` (`conflict.js`) gains `'duplicate-serial'` (and `'received-twice'` as its sibling), `RESOLUTIONS` gains `['acknowledged', 'dismissed']` for it; **`kind` is plain text with no CHECK, so no migration is needed for the kind** [C `007_sync_conflicts.sql`]; the kind is also added to the MCP formatter (`mcp/src/format/issues.ts`) and the client's mismatch list [R-aud 3.10 item 4: fifteen files name `'duplicate-number'`, the new kind goes through the same list].
- **Never reject.** The push is accepted whole (`conflict.js`: "nothing blocks sync"; a refused batch would strand a device's outbox [R-aud 3.10]); the response gains `unitClashes: n`.
- **REST.** `rest.js` inserts the body without `classifyPush` [R-aud 3.10]; the same `indexUnits(client, tenant, inserts)` function runs inside the `withTenantLock` callback of the REST write, and the response carries a `unit_clash` warning per clash (the CF-6b `variant_missing` precedent).
- **Limits and the row cap of the mismatch report.** At most **10,000 events per voucher** (5,000 rows, each with an `id2`), **50,000 events per push** and 5,000,000 rows per tenant; past any the voucher is **accepted and not indexed** with a `log.warn` and an `unitIndexSkipped` count in the answer (a hostile tenant cannot grow the table without bound; the device still detects locally). **`duplicate-serial` rows have their own cap, `LIMITS.unitRows = 2,000`, apart from `LIMITS.openRows`**: the shared 2,000-row cap meant a bulk offline sync of 3,000 clashes silently dropped 1,000 ("new ones beyond this are not recorded" [C `conflict.js`, `sync.js` `upsertConflicts`], which contradicts "never silent"), and enough false rows would also crowd out real edit-edit and duplicate-number records. Past `unitRows` **one aggregated row per tenant** (`dedupe_key = units-more`, `details.more = N`) says "N more units clash: open the Serial Stock report's clash filter", so nothing is unrecorded. Rows are small (about 160 bytes with `uid` and `eod`; a million units is about 160 MB with two indexes, a deploy-time figure to measure).
- **The rebuild job.** A voucher accepted and not indexed (caps), data pushed **before migration 009**, and the seed script's rows have no path back into `unit_events` from the push path. `scripts/pg/rebuild-unit-events.mjs <tenant>` (documented, idempotent, run under the tenant lock) **rebuilds `unit_events` for a tenant from `change_log`**, the source of truth: a full scan of the latest `put` of every voucher, `unitChain.eventsOf`, chunked inserts, a report of what it skipped and why. It is run once at deploy for a tenant that already holds serials (none does today) and is the documented cure for `unitIndexSkipped`.
- **Cost.** Zero for a push with no serials; otherwise one delete, chunked inserts (at most 5,000 events each) and one set query per chunk.

### 10.3 The reserve at Accept (slice T-9b, optional; narrows the window)

Wave 3's server numbering is the pattern, **deployed** [R-aud 3.11]: an idempotent reserve keyed on the voucher id, a provisional flag when the cloud is silent, a claim when the device syncs, "reported, never renumbered". A unit is not a number (it goes in, out, in again), so the ledger is a **lease**, and the check is deliberately modest because **the server cannot assert absence** (it may not yet know a unit another device received offline): it can only report *a unit out by a voucher this device does not hold*.

- **One request, not two.** The client already reserves a voucher number "after every question that can stop the save and immediately before the write" with a 4 s timeout and a 30 s pause after a silent cloud [C `vchNumbering.ts`]; a second cloud call would double the worst case. The reserve body `POST /sync/next-number` gains an optional `units: [{ itemId, ukey, dir }]` and the answer an optional `unitCheck: [{ ukey, state: 'ok' | 'taken', by?: { voucherId, numberStr, date, party }, seq }]`.
- **Server rule.** Under the existing per-series advisory lock plus a per-tenant unit lock: for each unit with `dir: 'out'`, read its events by `uid`, arrange them with `orderUnitEvents` (rule W) and look at the last; `taken` iff that is an `out` by a **different, live** voucher. A **lease** is a row of its own small table (`unit_leases (tenant_id, cid, uid, voucher_id, expires_at, PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, cid, uid, voucher_id))`, created by migration 009, **not** a `unit_events` row: that table's `seq` and `line_no` are `NOT NULL` and a reserve body carries neither) inserted for the reserving voucher so a second device's concurrent reserve sees it; a lease is removed when the voucher is pushed (the push writes the real events) and any lease past its `expires_at` (10 minutes) is deleted by the next reserve for that tenant. **This whole section is slice T-9b, and the promise "two online devices are stopped at Accept" is attached to T-9b alone**; with only T-9a built two online devices are detected at the next push (10.1).
- **Client rule.** `taken` matters **only when the holder voucher is not in this device's own log** (if it is, the local chain already decided: a return the server has not seen is legitimate): the Accept bar then says "IMEI 3569... was sold a moment ago on INV-87 by Counter 2; remove it (Enter) or sell anyway with a reason (Administration right)". With the cloud silent the voucher saves with its units **provisional** (a device-local list in `meta`, the `vnoPending:` family [C `sync/numbering.ts`]) and the units are **claimed in a batch at the next sync** (the claim call takes a list; the mobile readiness note already asks for a batch claim [R-aud 3.11]); a claim that finds the unit held by another voucher yields the same `duplicate-serial` row, never a renumber and never a refusal.
- **`trackingOffline: 'block'`** refuses a serial voucher when the reserve cannot be answered in a synced company (a local-only company is a single device and unaffected).

### 10.4 The batch overlay: hold wins

`batchRecs` rides the stock item document, a whole-document write. A recall must not be erased by another device's stale save of the same item. **Server rule (CF's capability-keyed shape, 10.5):** for a `put` to `stockItems` from a writer that does not state `trk1`, the server restores the current `batchRecs` it omitted; for a writer that does, it merges per key: the entry with the **higher `rev` wins**, and a `status` of `hold`, `recalled` or `quarantine` in the current row is kept against a stale write that clears it **unless the stale write's `rev` is higher** (an administrator's release, which always bumps `rev` above what it loaded). Each application is recorded as a `protected-fields` mismatch ("a stale copy tried to clear the recall of batch X"). The client mirrors the merge in `planRemote` so an unsent local edit never silently overwrites a pulled recall. The release lower than a hold is a deliberate asymmetry: hold is the safe state.

### 10.5 Older clients must not erase serial or batch data (T19)

The exposure is real and is the audit's risk 18: `VoucherScreen.save` builds its voucher as a **fresh literal**, so an older build that alters a voucher drops any line field it does not know, which for `serials` frees the units silently while the quantity stays [R-aud 3.3, 4.2 item 3]. Closed in layers, **all riding CF's machinery so there is one carry function with two bags and one capability marker, not two**:

1. **Capability marker.** The push body states `caps`; this work adds **`trk1`** beside CF's `udf2` (`src/sync/caps.ts`, one writer). A build that carries the funnel of 5, the line carry of 6.4 and the chip states `trk1`.
2. **Server carry** (`UDF_CARRIERS.vouchers.lines.keep` of CF 9.3 extended): `['countPart', 'serials', 'serialKind', 'eod', 'mrp', 'salePrice', 'billsNote', 'missing']`, paired by CF's **strong identity** (`stockItemId, godownId, batchName, qty, rate, amount`) first, then the weak one only where it is unambiguous. A voucher whose older writer changed a serial line's **quantity** cannot be strong-paired; the lone weak match restores the list, the count then differs from the quantity and the push records a `protected-fields` mismatch **and** the Data Check shows "serial count differs from quantity" (the honest outcome: not silent, not undone). The header-level key **`trackOverrides` is an array of up to 50 entries, so it is NOT merged "the way `udf` is"** (spreading arrays into an object literal, `{ ...current, ...incoming }`, yields `{0: ..., 1: ...}`, and the next client's `.map()` on it would throw on voucher open): **the current array is kept when the incoming voucher lacks the key, else the two are united by `(rule, line, unit, at)`** (dropping the oldest past 50), and the result is always an array.
3. **Item keys.** `CAP_KEPT.stockItems = { track: 'trk1', barcodes: 'trk1', batchRecs: 'trk1' }` (CF's capability-keyed list for the company row, extended to a table): a write from a writer lacking `trk1` keeps the current value of each; the godown's `kind` and the company's `features.tracking` / `trackingOffline` are kept the same way (`companyAlter.ts` carries them on the client; the server keeps the nested keys: the builder extends CF's `CAP_KEPT` to take a dotted path such as `features.tracking`).
4. **Backup schema 4** (3.7): an older app refuses a file holding tracking data.
5. **The editor refuses to switch tracking on in a synced company** while the last pull answer lacks `trk1` (`needsServerCap`, CF 7.4), so a mistaken release order is refused at runtime, not silently.
6. **MCP and REST:** the MCP write planner refuses a tracked item (15.5); REST applies the same carry (`restWrite.js`).
7. **A mixed-version fleet: record capabilities, flag what arrives, refuse the unsafe switch.** The layers above protect a unit list that already exists; they cannot make an older build create one. An older build (a web tab not reloaded, an Electron installer not rebuilt: the Phase 28 gotcha) has no allocation panel, no R4 and no funnel, so it saves a Sales line for a serial item with quantity 5 and no units, and nothing in the chain notices. The mitigations, all detective or preventive and **none able to force an old build to comply (stated plainly in the release note and on the screen)**:
   - **Device capabilities.** The push body already states `caps` (CF); the same migration adds `sync_device.caps text NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` and `touchDevice` stores what the body stated [C `sync.js` `touchDevice`, `007_sync_conflicts.sql`]. The endpoints that already read `sync_device` for the mismatch report (id, label, user, last seen [C `sync.js`, the conflicts routes]) return `caps` as well, or a small `GET /sync/devices` is added (the builder's choice; no such route exists today): each device with its label, last seen and whether it states `trk1`.
   - **The Company features screen** shows "Devices that cannot record units: Counter 2 (last seen 21-Oct)" and **the editor refuses to switch `features.tracking` on in a synced company while any device seen in the last 30 days lacks `trk1`** (a device unseen for 30 days ages out; no "forget device" action is needed), with the device named and the way out (update it).
   - **Arrival detection.** The server holds no item rows, so it cannot know an item is tracked; a **new build's post-pull scan** (5, caller 4) flags every arriving outward voucher whose line names a serial item with fewer `serials` than its quantity as a client-recorded `unit-unrecorded` mismatch row (idempotent `dedupe_key = unrec:<voucher>:<line>`), and the Data Check shows the same list as `serial-line-short`.
   - **What stays undetected** (said in section 18): a unit sold by an older build is invisible to R3 and to the server until a person enters its units or counts the stock; a second counter's sale of that physical IMEI is then accepted.

### 10.6 Release order and size

The backend change (migration 009, `unitChain.js`, the `conflict.js` / `pushRun.js` / `histories.js` / `restWrite.js` edits, `CAP_KEPT` for stock items) **ships in the same window as CF-6a**, gated on the owner's VM secrets (the VM runs the `a0fffd6` backend code with wave 3's numbering since 2026-10-23 [R-aud 4.1]). A **deploy-time smoke** on a throwaway tenant closes the gate because the suites cannot cover the SQL (`loadHistories` and the advisory locks need Postgres): push a voucher with serials from a device that states `trk1`; push the same voucher again from a device that states nothing, with a base at or past the first push, and read back that the serials survived; push a second voucher naming one of the units and read back one `duplicate-serial` row; cancel it and read back that the row's evidence is gone and the clash row auto-resolves. `scripts/pg/units-pg.mjs` (a real-Postgres script, the `numbering-pg.mjs` precedent) runs the SQL half. **No customer company may switch tracking on before the smoke has passed.** `sync.ts` push batching gains the **byte cap** of 3.7 and `/sync/pull` its **byte-bounded page** (3.7). The deploy-time smoke also pushes **one voucher of 5,000 unit rows with `id2`** (so the chunked insert and the 50,000-event budget are exercised on the real database) and one from a device that states no capabilities, and reads back `sync_device.caps`.

### 10.7 The resolution flow (the Mismatch Report, `ui/admin/SyncConflicts.tsx`)

A `duplicate-serial` row opens a **Unit clash** panel: the unit's whole timeline, both invoices side by side (party, date, price, device, whether the second device could have seen the first), and the actions, each producing an **ordinary voucher or edit** (nothing is silently undone). **Which invoice is wrong is the person's call**: the panel shows both invoices and asks, and "later" (by date and number, which across voucher types is not time, 4.3) is only a default sort. The actions apply to **either** invoice A or B: **(a) Replace the unit** on that invoice (the clerk typed the wrong id or handed a different box: opens that voucher on the line with a corrected id, re-judged by R1 and R3); **(b) Edit that invoice** (remove the unit or the line); **(c) Cancel that invoice** (`Alt+X`, keeps its number); **(d) Acknowledge** (two physical units that share an IMEI, which the grey market produces, or a sequence the clients saw differently, with a note; administrator only); **(e) Dismiss**. **There is deliberately no "credit note" shortcut.** A Credit Note records that the physical unit came back from that party; offered as a quick fix for the second sale it would leave the chain's last event an `in` while the phone is with the first customer, so the Serial Stock report would offer the unit for sale again, a third sale would pass R3, and the row would never resolve because the double `out` is still in the log (the first text's resolution (b)). A genuine return is entered the ordinary way after the wrong invoice has been corrected, and R5 then judges it against the right sale. The row resolves **automatically** when a later push removes the clash (an edit or a cancel), recording "resolved by edit".

---

## 11. Import, export, migration of existing batches, presets

### 11.1 Bulk units and batches in

- **In the panel:** paste and file (6.3). **File:** a CSV or `.xlsx` through the existing `xlsxRead` / `csv` readers into the unit grid, with a **validation preview** before anything is added: a table of row, value, status (ok, duplicate in file, in stock, format, check digit, TAC), inline fix, and the choice "add all valid rows" or "add selected"; at most 20,000 rows; hostile files are the `xlsx` reader's job (capped, linear [C `xlsxRead.ts`]).
- **Mapped importer** (`core/importMap.ts`, `ui/admin/MappedImport.tsx`; lane X): a new kind **"Tracked voucher lines"**: columns date, voucher type, party, invoice no, item (name, alias or barcode), godown, quantity, rate, batch, manufacturing, expiry, MRP, serial 1, serial 2, cost; rows are **grouped by invoice number** into one voucher with a line per distinct `(item, batch, MRP, rate)` carrying its serials; the preview shows every rule finding; it writes through `putDoc` and therefore through the funnel guard with a `session` set (500 vouchers are judged against each other, not 500 index rebuilds). A second kind, **"Opening stock by cell"**, writes `openingAlloc.cells` and the item's opening totals. `FIELDS.items` gains `barcode`, `pack quantity`, `tracking mode`, `MRP`.
- **Barcodes in:** an item column `barcode` adds an `ItemBarcode`; a duplicate across items is a Data Check row, never a silent winner (`BulkEntry` resolves by first match and silently ignores a later duplicate today [R-lab 6.3]).

### 11.2 Tally XML (`core/tallyImport.ts`, `tallyExport.ts`; lane X, sequenced with CF-10)

**In.** Read the batch allocations **under a stock item** (opening batches: `openingAlloc.cells`; today the importer reads one opening figure per item and drops them [R-aud 3.7]), the manufacturing and expiry dates of every batch allocation (the importer reads only the name and the dates are never read), and a line's godown and batch parts as it does. The tag names for the dates are those of Tally's XML reference and are [unverified here] against a real file; the importer must be run against three real exports before release (the CF-10 gate, owner question). **Conversion of the "IMEI as a batch" practice** (Tally documents it as the answer for phones: one batch per unit, closing quantity 1 meaning unsold [R-bst 2.2]): the preview step **"Convert batches of one to serial units"** flags an item when at least 80 % of its batches have quantity 1 and an **identifier can be extracted** from their names. **The extraction step** (the first text kept the whole batch name as the unit id, but Tally's own documented example prefixes a consignment or lot, "Feb-654321543214321" [R-bst 2.2], so a unit stored under the prefixed name never matches the bare IMEI a scanner returns and R3 would block every converted unit): the candidate id is, in order, (1) the **longest run of exactly 15 digits that passes Luhn**, (2) else the longest 15-digit run with the check digit failing (flagged), (3) else the whole name when it is 8 to 20 letters and digits with at least one digit (hyphens and spaces counted as part of the name only for this third case); the **prefix that was cut off is kept on the unit as its `note`** ("Tally batch name: Feb-654321543214321"), so nothing is lost. The preview shows **10 sample conversions** (name, extracted id, kind) and the choice "extract / keep the whole name / leave as batches", and the rule is **tested on the documented example**. On Yes the item becomes `serial` (kind `imei` when the extracted id is 15 digits), `valuationMethod` is set to Avg. Cost if it was FIFO or LIFO (4.5), each extracted id becomes a unit id (on the opening cell and on every voucher line, `batchName` cleared, `serials` filled), the lines' quantities are unchanged, and the import report says "312 batches of one became units". Never applied without the person's Yes; a name that fails normalisation stays a batch.
**Out.** A serial item exports each unit as a batch name of quantity 1 (the only shape Tally can read) with its manufacturing and expiry dates where the line has them, and the "not carried" notice names what Tally cannot hold (the unit's second id, warranty, attributes, status); the export of a batch item writes its dates (today `tallyExport.ts` counts such lines as "not carried"). Product copy names Tally only here, comparatively (CLAUDE.md branding rule).

### 11.3 Reports out

Section 8.3: text-typed id columns, an Excel export, a CSV note. **Label data** goes out as `.xlsx` for a shop that outgrows the designer (BarTender and others read it [R-lab 4.2]).

### 11.4 The migration of a company that already uses batches

1. **Nothing changes by itself.** A company that never switches `features.tracking` on sees today's app. One that does sees its legacy batch items listed as "Batch (basic box)".
2. **Data Check lines first** (read-only, before any change): "N batch items use the old batch box"; "N units of stock sit in no batch (opening stock or unbatched lines): M items"; "Physical Stock vouchers with more than one line for an item at a godown: N (history is not restated)"; "B-17 and b-17 are one batch under the new key: K names"; "batches with the same number and different expiry: N"; **"batch cells below zero or phantom: N, with the suggested re-balancing Stock Journal (move quantity from the no-batch cell to the named cell)"**; **"items whose legacy expiry dates are all the 1st of a month: N"**.
3. **Upgrade** (an action on the item, and "Upgrade all batch items" in bulk, from the item list and the Data Check): writes `track: { v: 1, mode: 'batch', exp: 'ask', mfg: 'ask', expiredSale: 'allow', pick: 'manual', mrpAt: 'none', strict: false }` (today's looseness: **`strict: false` makes R10, R25's required fields and R27 Warn instead of Block**, because the first text promised that Upgrade "must not start refusing yesterday's habits" but loosened only `expiredSale`, `pick` and `mrpAt`, while R10 would block a sale from a batch cell that history had left at zero or below, and R27 would refuse a batch name that was only ever entered on a purchase in another godown, and cells that existed before the Upgrade are exempt from R10 until a voucher touches them), keeps `batches: true`, changes **no voucher**. The cell engine reads the item's existing lines from then on; a line with no batch is the "no batch" cell; the opening stock sits there until the person runs **Opening by cell** (value-neutral; it moves quantity from the no-batch cell to named cells). The form offers, in one line, to **Tighten** (`strict: true`, `expiredSale: block`, `pick: fefo`) once the person is ready.
4. **History is never restated.** Old Physical Stock counts, old negative batches and merged names are reported, not corrected; correcting is a Stock Journal or a count the person makes now. **Two cases of legacy data are handled on purpose:** (a) the old Batch box parses only a full date [C `dates.ts` `parseDateInput`: `08/27` splits into a day 8 and a month 27 and is dropped], so legacy expiries are either missing or full dates typed as the 1st or the last of the month by habit, while the new rules read "saleable through the expiry day" (a batch typed `1-8-2027` for a printed 08/2027 would be blocked from 2 August although valid to 31 August): the Data Check lists items whose legacy expiries are **all the 1st of a month** and offers a bulk **"treat as month-only"** that writes the **overlay** (`BatchRec.expiryDate` = the last day of that month, which wins over the lines) and changes no voucher; (b) **an alteration of a Physical Stock voucher written before the feature loads its lines exactly as stored and never adds `countPart` or `eod`** (6.6), so a closed period's quantity cannot change by pressing Accept.

### 11.5 Presets (data, not code)

A preset sets the company switch, item defaults for new items, godowns to create, the voucher types' flags (`labelsAfterSave`), the print switches (`show.mrp`, `show.serial`, `show.warranty`), which label templates are pinned, which reports are pinned, and nothing else (the custom-fields starter sets are the precedent [R-vert 7]). **Five first, by the vertical research's own ordering [R-vert 5.5, 7]:** Pharma retail; Pharma wholesale and stockist; Mobile and electronics; Trading and FMCG distribution; Garments and footwear (the variant fields are CF's; this preset adds barcodes per variant and the hang tag). Then Grocery with a scale profile (and **a scale item file export: code, name, rate, as CSV or XML for the scale vendor, so the shop does not key its PLU table twice** [R-vert 3.3]), Manufacturer or packer, Hardware and appliances, Jewellery (which may default `Specific`: a jewellery piece is a unique unit), and the rest. Presets are offered at company creation, in Settings and through the sample company (T28); applying one later is additive and never edits an existing item.

| Preset | Switches on | Defaults for a new item | Godowns made | Labels pinned | Reports pinned |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pharma retail | tracking; `show.mrp` | `batch`, exp and mfg ask, MRP at batch, `fefo`, `expiredSale: block`, warn [90, 60, 30], `minLifeDays` 0, batch price off, MRP ceiling warn, `strict: true`; **the Pharma details fields (Manufacturer, Schedule) switched on** and the invoice prints the manufacturer on a scheduled item's batch line | Returns (not for sale) | pharmacy shelf; loose-dispense | Batch-wise Stock, Expiring and Expired, Batch Trace |
| Pharma wholesale | the above | the above plus batch price on | Returns, In Transit | dispatch carton | the above plus Expiry Ageing, FEFO Pick List |
| Mobile and electronics | tracking; `show.serial`, `show.warranty` | `serial` kind `imei`, second id on (**pair mode**, 6.3 rule 11), warranty 12 months from invoice, **`AvgCost` costing with per-unit cost kept as data (`Specific` is an opt-in with its caution, never this preset's default: AS 2 para 15, 4.5)**, MRP at unit, pending allowed, `strict: true` | In Transit, Service Centre, Returns | phone unit sticker; shelf card | Serial Stock, Unit History, Warranty, Unit Profit |
| Trading and FMCG | tracking | `batch` only for items the person marks, MRP at batch, `fefo` | Returns | shelf price; dispatch | Batch-wise Stock, Expiring and Expired |
| Garments | tracking (and CF's fields) | item `none`, barcode per variant | none | hang tag | CF's size-colour screen |
