## 12. Rights and settings

- **Rights use the existing areas** (no new right in v1, section 17 asks): **Inventory** to write any tracked line, to Upgrade, to hold a batch, to reasoned-override R3, R5, R10, R11, R25, R26; **Administration** to override R1 and R13, to **release** a held or recalled batch, to switch `features.tracking` on or off (it is the company-features screen's own right) and to resolve a unit clash; **Masters** for the item form; reports are **Reports** (read). A table-driven `access` test names every new screen's area and every override's right (the security-access suite fails on a screen with no `SCREEN_AREA`, and a screen without one is admin-only [R-aud 3.15]). The funnel guard raises `AccessDenied` for a denied override, which the screens already catch.
- **Settings window** gains a **Devices** tab (`ui/settings/tabs.ts` registry, a new panel file): **Scanner** (enable the scan layer, terminator, prefix and suffix, the learned timing **kept as statistics (mean, 99th percentile, maximum) and kept apart for a USB and a Bluetooth connection**, the keyboard mode, **`reads2D`: yes, no or unknown, set by a setup scan that includes a QR and a Data Matrix and reports which symbologies arrived (14.7)**, a "scan here" test area that shows exactly what arrived, sound off / errors / all); **Printers** (the profiles of 13.2 with their real dpi, a test label, calibration offsets); **Label defaults** (the template per purpose); **Scale** (the scale profile and the setup scan). These are **device** facts (a scanner and a printer belong to the PC), stored per device in a new `TW_DEVICE` localStorage key (the discipline of `TW_UI_PERSONAL`: every read and write in try/catch, the page works without it; keyed by the device, not by `device|operator`) and, in the desktop app, mirrored into the printer allow-list in `userData` that only the main process writes (13.6). Nothing in Devices can move `Ctrl+,`, Go To or the way back (the Phase 27 escape hatch); the whole tab is **opt-in and changes nothing unset** (asserted by identity, 16.2). **`?uireset=1` must also clear `TW_DEVICE`**: `index.html` clears the `TW_UI_*` and `TW_THEME_*` families before first paint [C `index.html`], which does not match this key, so a scan layer that swallowed or held keys would have had no way back from the address bar; T-5b adds `TW_DEVICE` to that list (and a recipe presses Space and a letter on every control kind with the layer on, 14.3), and the Phase 27 rule "a customisable app needs an escape hatch no setting can move" is asserted for it.
- **Company switch** `features.tracking`, `features.trackingOffline` (F11). A company-wide **policy block is deliberately not built in v1**: every strictness knob is on the item (2.3) and the presets set it, which avoids a second synced company field with the Phase 27 older-client duty; the one company-wide knob is `trackingOffline`. If the owner later wants company defaults ("all new items expire-block"), they ride CF-14's `entryPolicy` object as a sibling section (its writer, mirror and capability-keyed server rule already exist), not a new mechanism.

---

## 13. Labels

### 13.1 What a Comsky label is, in one paragraph

A label is **data plus a template, drawn by one layout engine** (T21). The data is a **source row**: an item, a batch cell or a unit. The template is a JSON document in millimetres. The engine returns a **drawing** (text runs, bars, matrices, rules, in millimetres, with every bar already a whole number of printer dots), and three renderers consume it: **SVG** (the live preview, the A4 sheet and the browser print of a roll), a **1-bit bitmap at the printer's own dots** (the raw path), and later native printer text. The preview and the sticker share **the same bars and the same positions** because they are the same drawing [R-lab 4.3]; text is rasterised by the browser's canvas and, in the SVG preview, by the browser's own hinting, so a glyph can differ by a pixel (the first text said "the same pixels", which holds for bars only). The leaders' labels are item labels, and Busy puts the design behind a paid service [R-bst 8.3, R-lab 4.1]; the room to be better is a label **born from the receipt** (one per unit or per quantity just booked, carrying the batch, expiry, MRP or IMEI just entered), from a fixed library that works on the first print, with a test label and a scan-it-back check, because Busy's own FAQ is mostly skipped labels, misalignment and bars that are too small [R-lab 4.2].

### 13.2 The data model

```ts
// src/trackTypes.ts (types) / src/core/labelTemplates.ts (the library, as data)
export type LabelKind = 'shelf' | 'unit' | 'batch' | 'carton' | 'hangtag' | 'jewel' | 'dispense' | 'revisedMrp' | 'bin' | 'prepack' | 'pack';
export interface LabelTemplate {
  v: 1; id: string; name: string; kind: LabelKind; verticals: string[];
  stock: RollStock | SheetStock;
  fields: LabelField[];
  copies: 'one' | 'qty' | 'perUnit' | 'perBatchLine' | { fixed: number };   // the default; every row's copies stay editable
  needsPacker?: true;                       // kinds prepack and pack only: offered to a company that says it packs or imports (13.8)
}
export interface RollStock  { kind: 'roll';  wMm: number; hMm: number; across: 1 | 2 | 3; gapXMm: number; gapYMm: number; printWidthMm?: number; sense?: 'gap' | 'bline' | 'none' }
export interface SheetStock { kind: 'sheet'; page: 'A4' | 'Letter'; cols: number; rows: number; marginTopMm: number; marginLeftMm: number; pitchXMm: number; pitchYMm: number; labelWMm: number; labelHMm: number }
export type LabelField =
  | { t: 'text'; src: FieldSrc | { lit: string }; xMm: number; yMm: number; wMm: number; hMm: number; fontMm: number; bold?: boolean; align?: 'l' | 'c' | 'r'; fit: 'shrink' | 'clip' | 'wrap'; maxLines?: number; fmt?: 'amount' | 'date' | 'monthYear' | 'upper'; prefix?: string; suffix?: string; mono?: boolean }
  | { t: 'barcode'; sym: 'code128' | 'ean13' | 'ean8' | 'upca' | 'code39' | 'qr' | 'dm'; src: FieldSrc; xMm: number; yMm: number; wMm: number; hMm: number; hri: boolean }
  | { t: 'mrp'; xMm: number; yMm: number; wMm: number; hMm: number; fontMm: number; form: 'full' | 'short' }   // locked wording, 13.8
  | { t: 'rule' | 'box'; xMm: number; yMm: number; wMm: number; hMm: number }
  | { t: 'logo'; xMm: number; yMm: number; wMm: number; hMm: number };
export type FieldSrc =
  | 'item.name' | 'item.alias' | 'item.partNo' | 'item.code' | 'item.hsn' | 'item.mrp' | 'item.unit' | 'item.group'
  | 'batch.no' | 'batch.mfg' | 'batch.exp' | 'batch.expMY' | 'batch.mrp' | 'batch.code' | 'batch.salePrice' | 'batch.supplier'
  | 'unit.id' | 'unit.id2' | 'unit.warrantyEnd' | 'unit.warrantyStart' | 'unit.lot' | 'unit.condition' | `unit.attr.${string}`
  | 'price.sale' | 'price.mrp' | 'price.unit' | 'variant.key' | `variant.${string}`
  | 'company.name' | 'company.gstin' | 'company.address' | 'voucher.date' | 'today' | `input.${string}`;
```
`price.sale` resolves unit, then batch `salePrice`, then the price level, then the item's standard sale rate; `price.mrp` unit, batch, item. **`input.*` fields are typed at print time** (net quantity, packed-on date, "marketed by", consumer-care phone, FSSAI number, licence number, country of origin) and remembered per template on the device: the statutory facts are the person's, never the app's (13.8). A `barcode` whose source is empty draws nothing and raises a warning, never a blank bar. Template ids of the library are stable and never reused.

**Printer profile** (a **device** fact, kept apart from the template so one design prints on a counter TSC and a godown Zebra): `{ id, name, transport: 'browser' | 'system' | 'network', printerId?, lang: 'driver' | 'tspl' | 'zpl', dpi: 203 | 300 | 600, printWidthMm, labelWMm, labelHMm, gapMm, across, offsetXMm, offsetYMm (may be negative), startCell?: { col, row } (a part-used sheet), minModuleDots (default 2; 3 for the GS1 trade kinds, 13.8) }`. **Dots are computed from the real dpi: `dots = round(mm x dpi / 25.4)`**: 203 dpi is 7.99 dots per mm, 300 dpi **11.81** (not the 12 the TSC manual rounds to [R-lab 1.7]), 600 dpi 23.62. The first text stored `dpmm: 6 | 8 | 12 | 24`, so on a 300 dpi jewellery printer a 40 mm tag was laid out in 480 dots against its real 472 and everything within 0.7 mm of the right edge was clipped (a 100 mm label drifted 1.6 mm); with `dpi` the arithmetic is exact (Appendix V) and the whole-dot bar rule is unchanged. The same millimetre label prints at another physical size on another density, so the profile, not the template, owns the dots.

### 13.3 The layout engine (`core/labelLayout.ts`, pure)

`layoutLabel(template, row, profile, metrics) -> Drawing` is deterministic given a **text-metrics provider** (`metrics.width(text, fontMm, bold, mono)`; the browser supplies a canvas measure with the face the print host loads, the suites supply a fixed-pitch stub). Rules:

1. **Dot alignment.** For a barcode of `M` modules (quiet zones included) in a field `wMm` wide, `moduleDots = floor(wMm x dpi / 25.4 / M)`; below `minModuleDots` the field **does not fit**: the drawing carries a warning ("this IMEI needs 38.5 mm at 2 dots; the field is 30 mm: widen it or use the QR") and draws it at the minimum anyway, never at a fractional width. **A GS1 trade barcode has its own floor** (13.8): `minModuleDots` is 3 at 203 dpi for it, because 2 dots is 0.250 mm and GS1's minimum X-dimension for an EAN-13 on a consumer item is 0.264 mm [W: GS1 Sweden's size guide], and its bar height is at least 18.28 mm; a smaller field warns "below the GS1 minimum". Every bar edge is a whole dot; `modulesToRuns` and the SVG path use integer dot coordinates, so the SVG preview, the bitmap and the printer's own bars agree. Quiet zones: EAN 11 left and 7 right, Code 128 10 each side [R-lab 1.1, 1.7].
2. **Text fit.** `shrink` reduces the font in steps of 0.1 mm to a **legibility floor of 2.0 mm of font size at 203 dpi** (a 1.2 mm font is about 10 dots tall, which a 1-bit threshold turns to noise) and the engine warns **"too small to read at this dpi"** below it; **2.5 to 3.0 mm for Devanagari**, whose conjuncts need the height. The statutory kinds (`prepack`, `pack`, `dispense`) are held to **Legal Metrology Rule 7(2) Table-I on the height of the numerals and letters** (13.8), a different and larger floor that is measured on the glyph, not on the font size. `wrap` up to `maxLines`; `clip` ends with an ellipsis; a long Hindi name does not overflow a 38 mm label. The `mrp` field is a fixed string and is never shrunk below the floor: a warning instead. **Text width is measured with the face the host actually draws with**: the bundled IBM Plex covers Latin only, and a Devanagari, Tamil or Gujarati name falls through to **the OS face** [C `styles.css` `--paper-font`], so the same label can fit on one PC and overflow on another; the **test label prints a line of Devanagari and the screen names the face in use** (a bundled Devanagari face is an owner decision, question 25; the default is the OS face, stated).
3. **Sheets.** `sheetSlots(stock) -> Slot[]` (row-major), `paginate(rows, slots, startCell)` skips the slots before `startCell` on the first page (a part-used sheet), and the profile's `offsetX/Y` moves every slot. A roll with `across` > 1 prints rows of `across` labels; the engine checks `across x wMm + (across-1) x gapXMm` against `printWidthMm` (a 3-up roll of 38 mm labels needs about 114 mm of media, more than the 104 mm of a 4-inch printer, [R-lab 1.7]) and refuses the combination with the reason.
4. **Calibration.** `calibrationDrawing(profile)` draws the **test label**: a 10 mm ruler grid, an EAN-13 and a Code 128 at 2 and 3 dots, a QR, text at three sizes, a frame at the nominal label edge, and an empty box for the **scan-it-back** check (the dialog asks the person to scan what was printed and shows what the scanner delivered next to what was meant: it catches a bar that is too thin, a dead terminator and a missing separator in one step, and doubles as the scanner setup screen of 14.7). Offsets are saved on the profile as the cheque leaf saves its shift [C `chequeLayoutEditor.tsx`].

**Renderers** (`core/labelRender.ts` for SVG and bitmap; `electron/labelLang.cjs` for printer bytes): `toSvg(drawing)` (every bar and matrix a filled SVG path: background graphics are off by default in print and a filled CSS box vanishes [C `print.css`]; `QrSvg` already works this way); `rasterise(drawing, dpi, drawText) -> { widthBytes, heightDots, bits }` (the host supplies `drawText`, a canvas; a label of 50 x 25 mm at 203 dpi is 400 x 200 dots, 10,000 bytes; 100 x 150 mm is 120 KB [R-lab 2.3]); the printer-language emitter lives **only** in `electron/labelLang.cjs` (13.6). A bitmap carries the rupee sign and Devanagari that the printers' built-in fonts and code pages do not (the TSPL code-page table has none [R-lab 2.3, 3.6]), which is why **the v1 raw path is one bitmap per label**; native bars and text are a later, optional emitter, once a printed proof of their quality exists.

### 13.4 The encoders (`src/core/barcode/`, pure TypeScript, no runtime dependency; T22)

| File | Does | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `check.ts` | `eanCheck(digits12)` (weights 3 and 1 from the right of the data digits), `isValidGtin(s)`, `gtinFromEan13`, `luhn*` (re-exported from `unitId`), `code128Check(values)` (start + each value x its position, mod 103), `mod43` | **one validator per family, never a shared "check digit" function** [R-lab 1.4]: the EAN rule on an IMEI passes or fails the wrong codes |
| `ean.ts` | `encodeEan13`, `encodeEan8`, `encodeUpcA` (an EAN-13 with a leading zero), add-ons EAN-2 and EAN-5 (books) | returns `{ modules: number[] (alternating bar and space run lengths, bar first), text }`; a 13-digit scan and a typed alias have **different** strictness: a bad check digit on a typed alias is a soft warning (today's aliases are free text), on a scan a hard reject (a scanner would not have sent it) |
| `code128.ts` | Sets A, B, C with a simple optimiser (Set C for runs of 4 or more digits, B otherwise, the shift codes), `FNC1` first for GS1-128, **ASCII 0 to 127 only** (a non-Latin character is refused, never mangled) | a 15-digit IMEI is 12 symbols: values `105, 49, 1, 54, 20, 32, 37, 51, 100, 24, check 93, stop 106` (Appendix V) |
| `code39.ts`, `itf.ts` | read and print Code 39 (optional mod 43) and ITF-14 (cartons) | printed rarely; mostly to be **read** |
| `gs1.ts` | `parseGs1(text, opts)`, `buildGs1(elements)`; the AI table of 14.4 | element strings only; 2-D carriers are `qr.ts` and `datamatrix.ts` |
| `datamatrix.ts` (T-6d, optional) | ECC 200: ASCII encodation with digit pairs, Reed-Solomon over GF(256), the placement pattern, FNC1 as codeword 232 for GS1 data, square symbols 10x10 and up | a 14x14 symbol holds 16 digits, so a 15-digit IMEI is about 4 to 6 mm square [R-lab 1.7]; **owner question: write it (about 400 lines) or load bwip-js lazily for that screen** |
| `render.ts` | `modulesToRuns`, `runsToSvgPath`, `matrixToSvgPath` (shared with `qrSvgPath`), `runsToBits(runs, moduleDots)` | integer dot coordinates only |
| `mint.ts` | `mintItemCode(existing): string` (EAN-13 in the **040 to 049** restricted block: `04` + 10 random digits + check, collision-checked against the registry; **never 890**, which only GS1 India issues [R-lab 1.2]), `mintBatchCode(existing)` (`B` + 7 characters of an alphabet with no look-alikes) | needs the Masters right (an item write) or the Inventory right (a batch overlay write) |

`core/qr.ts` stays as it is (byte mode, levels L and M, versions 1 to 40, an independent decoder test [C `qr.ts`]): a 15-digit IMEI is 15 bytes, version 1 at level L holds 17, so the QR unit sticker is a 29-module symbol (7.3 mm at 2 dots). A Digital Link QR is just a URL and works today; a GS1 QR with the GS character and level Q would extend `qr.ts` and is not built.

**Proof (the `barcode` suite, 16.1):** (1) an **independent decoder written for the test** reads every encoder's output back (module strings to symbols to text) for EAN-13 across all ten first-digit parity patterns, Code 128 across **all 107 values** and every set switch, Code 39 and ITF, on 5,000 random inputs and on the hostile ones; (2) **vectors recorded once from bwip-js** by `scripts/assert/barcode.record.mjs` (run offline with an oracle that is **not** a dependency of the app, the `viz-tokens.record.mjs` precedent), committed as JSON, compared module for module; (3) an optional manual recipe reads printed output with the macOS Vision detector (the labels research read seven codes that way at 4 and 2 pixels per module, exercising 29 of the 107 Code 128 rows [R-lab 1.9]: a build must encode and decode every one).

### 13.5 The template library (T21): twelve fixed, two for packers

Each is data in `core/labelTemplates.ts`, with a stable id, sizes that are **starting points to confirm with five shops per vertical on real stock** [R-lab 4.4, R-vert 5.6], and a pinned example row in the suite.

| # | Id | Stock | Fields (source) | Code | Copies | Vertical |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `shelf-50x25` | roll 50 x 25, 1 across | name two lines, **selling price** large, optional MRP, rack | Code 128 of `item.code` (or EAN-13 where the item's primary is one) | qty | retail, grocery, hardware |
| 2 | `shelf-38x25` | roll 38 x 25 | the same, compact | Code 128 or EAN-13 | qty | retail |
| 3 | `shelf-a4-40up` | A4, 4 x 10 grid (a free-grid preset; 21, 24, 40, 44 and 65-up presets exist) | the same | Code 128 | qty | shops with an ordinary printer |
| 4 | `phone-unit-50x25` | roll 50 x 25 | model, storage, colour, **IMEI 1** text, warranty end | Code 128 of `unit.id` (38.5 mm at 2 dots fits 50 mm); a second sticker for IMEI 2 as an option | perUnit | mobile, electronics |
| 5 | `phone-unit-qr-38x25` | roll 38 x 25 | model, IMEI, warranty end | QR of `unit.id` (7.3 mm); **needs a 2-D imager: with `reads2D: no` the picker substitutes template 4 (Code 128), with `unknown` it warns and offers both** | perUnit | mobile |
| 6 | `carton-100x50` | roll 100 x 50 | model, colour, serial(s) or both IMEIs, date received | Code 128 and QR | perUnit or perBatchLine | electronics |
| 7 | `pharma-shelf-50x25` | roll 50 x 25 | name, **batch**, **expiry MM/YYYY**, MRP, rack | Code 128 of `batch.code` | perBatchLine | pharmacy (an internal shelf or rack tag; the maker's pack is never re-printed, 13.8) |
| 8 | `pharma-dispense-75x50` | roll 75 x 50 (or A5) | supplier name and address, patient, quantity, **register serial number as typed from the shop's own prescription register (Comsky keeps no register; the field is labelled that way and left empty rather than numbered by the app)**, dose, "for external use only" (the Rule 94(2)(i) particulars [W]; **the exemption those particulars buy is conditional on condition (3) of Rule 65 being satisfied (94(2)(ii)) [W], which the label's screen says in one line**) | none | one | pharmacy (the dispenser's own label) |
| 9 | `mrp-revised-38x25` | roll 38 x 25 | "MRP Rs. __ (revised, incl. of all taxes)" | none | qty | any: **allowed only as a lower MRP and not covering the maker's own**; a warning says so (13.8) |
| 10 | `garment-hangtag-50x25` | roll 50 x 25 | brand, style, size, colour, MRP, price code | EAN-13 or Code 128 of the **variant** code | qty | garments, footwear |
| 11 | `jewel-tag-40x30` | roll 40 x 30 (a 25 x 15 variant) | tag number, gross, stone, net weight, purity, **HUID as typed text**, price code | Code 128 of `unit.id` | perUnit | jewellery (the HUID is recorded after hallmarking, never generated) |
| 12 | `bin-75x50` | roll 75 x 50 or A4 12-up | godown, rack, bin, item | QR and Code 128 of a location code | one | hardware, stock-take locations |
| 13 | `prepack-60x40` | roll 60 x 40 | name, **MRP (locked wording)**, **unit price**, net quantity, packed on, batch, expiry, packer, consumer care | EAN-13 only if the owner holds a GS1 prefix (**a code a stranger's till will scan: at least 3 dots at 203 dpi and 18.28 mm of bar height, else the label warns "below the GS1 minimum"**, 13.8) | qty | grocery in-store packing |
| 14 | `pack-100x60` | roll 100 x 60 | every statutory field the owner fills, with the checks of 13.8 | as above | qty | manufacturers, packers, importers |

**2-D gating.** Cheap 1-D laser and CCD scanners are common at Indian counters and **cannot read a QR or a Data Matrix** (the first text and its four research documents never asked which kind the shop owns). Templates 5, any template using a QR or Data Matrix as its only code, and the scan-back test of those codes depend on `reads2D` (14.7); the template picker marks them "needs a 2-D scanner" and, where a 1-D code exists for the same data, substitutes it; templates 4, 6 and 12 carry a Code 128 and are unaffected. Rows 13 and 14 carry `needsPacker`: offered only after the company says in Label defaults "We pack or import goods for sale". Weighing-scale labels are **printed by the scale, never by this software**; the grocery preset sets a **scale profile** for *reading* them (14.4).

### 13.6 Print paths, and the Electron bridge

**Order of build and what each needs** (T23, [R-lab 2.4 and 2.5]):

| Path | Works on | Silent | Needs | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P1 browser dialog**: A4 sheet grid; a roll through an injected `@page { size: Wmm Hmm; margin: 0 }` (the `PrintLeaf` precedent [C `chequeLeaf.tsx`]), each label a page, SVG fills only, `ensurePrintFonts()` first | the HTTP site and Electron | no (a dialog per job; a 500-label job is a 500-page job and the dialog says so) | nothing **in code; a one-day printed spike (T-6s, below) before the label half of the headline is promised** | T-6s, T-6c |
| **P2 Electron driver print** to a **listed** printer | Electron | yes | `webContents.print` with `silent`, `deviceName`, a custom `pageSize`; **Electron 31's units for the `pageSize` object are not stated and whether `print()` honours a CSS `@page` size is not stated** [W], so the T-7 gate runs both on a **packaged binary on Windows and macOS** and the bridge uses whichever proves out; if neither does, P2 falls back to "save a PDF at the exact size (`printToPDF`, `preferCSSPageSize`, known to work in the existing bridge) and print it from your PDF viewer" | T-7 |
| **P3 Electron raw bitmap** (TSPL, ZPL) over TCP 9100 to an allow-listed network printer, then the Windows spooler's RAW data type through **a bundled, hash-checked PowerShell script** (decision below; not `node-printer`: its page shows a decade-old toolchain and 118 open issues [R-lab 2.4]) | Electron on **Windows** (macOS raw/CUPS is **out of scope for v1**: a Mac prints through P1 or P2) | yes, fast, with status | a printer profile and a **real printer test** (nobody has run any of this) | T-7 |
| **P4 WebUSB / Web Serial / Web Bluetooth** | Chromium on HTTPS only | no (a chooser per device) | **TLS on the site** (the live site is HTTP) | T-10 |

**The one-day printed spike (T-6s): a precondition of promising the label half of the headline.** Everything about P1 is unproven and the repo's own cheque-leaf code says so: "a driver that insists on its own stock will still need actual size" [C `chequeLeaf.tsx`, the `PrintLeaf` comment], R-lab 2.4 rates Chrome's label-size behaviour "anecdotal" [Ws], and whether Electron 31's `print()` honours `@page` is not stated [W]. In a browser there is no PDF writer, so the only fallback is the person choosing "Save as PDF" in the dialog. The spike: **Chrome and the packaged Electron on Windows 10 and 11 against a TSC, a TVS and a Zebra desk printer, one 50 x 25 roll, the calibration label of 13.3**; record per printer the **exact driver settings that give exactly one label per page at 100 %** (paper size, margins none, scale 100 %, headers and footers off, background graphics off) and whether a blank page follows each label; **and, for the raw TSPL path, the DIRECTION each printer wants and what a job from this app leaves behind in it** (TSC's manual says DIRECTION "will be stored in the printer memory", so one job from this app changes how a program that also drives that printer prints afterwards: the profile carries a `direction` of 0 or 1, default 1, and the Print Labels screen says so beside the printer picker). Those settings are **shown on the Print Labels screen** next to the Print button. Until the spike has been run, the label half of the headline is **conditional**, and the named fallbacks are: the **A4 sheet grid** (the default, which works on any printer), "Save as PDF, then print from a viewer at actual size", and, only if the spike fails, a downloadable raw printer file (a second import path of `labelLang`, deliberately not built now: the emitter stays in `electron/` so a renderer can never send a command to a printer). Time for the spike was not budgeted in the first text, whose own hand test came last in T-6c; it is now **first**, ahead of T-6c's code.

**The bridge (`electron/labelBridge.cjs`, a new file modelled on `exportBridge.cjs`).** The renderer is not trusted. Four IPC handlers, each a pure factory taking its collaborators so a suite runs the same function against a fake `electron` (`scripts/assert/labelbridge.assert.ts`), wired in `main.cjs` through `trusted(e)` and exposed by `preload.cjs` as `tallyweb.labels`:

- `labels.printers()` returns `[{ id, name, kind: 'system' | 'network', isDefault?, status? }]`. System ids are `sys:<name>` built from `getPrintersAsync()` (the **live** list, re-queried by main on every use); network ids are `net:<uuid>` from the allow-list. Nothing else about the machine.
- `labels.addNetwork()` opens **a main-process window (a local HTML page with its own preload)** where the person types a name, a host and a port; main validates (an IP or a single-label or `.local` host; **resolved to a private address, loopback or link-local only; a public address is refused at connect time as well**; port 9100 to 9109) and stores it in `userData/printers.json`, which **only main writes**. The renderer can **never** name a host, a port or a path: a hostile page cannot make this process open sockets to arbitrary addresses. `labels.removeNetwork(id)` removes one.
- `labels.print(job)` takes **a structured job, not a command string**. A **page** job (P2) is `{ printerId, kind: 'page', widthMm, heightMm, copies }` and prints the renderer's print host. **A raw job (P3) is one job carrying MANY labels**: `{ printerId, kind: 'raw', lang: 'tspl' | 'zpl', widthMm, heightMm, gapMm?, labels: [{ bitmap: { widthBytes, heightDots, dataB64 }, copies }] }`. The first text took a single `bitmap` per call, but the headline "one sticker per unit" needs a **different bitmap per label**, so 40 phones were 40 sequential calls, the stated 20,000-label ceiling was unreachable (20,000 distinct labels would be 20,000 calls), and each USB call is its own Windows spooler job or its own TCP connection (the 1,240 labels of the first example would be 1,240 spooler jobs, plausibly 6 to 20 minutes [J, unmeasured], each overlapping call refused as `busy`). Now **main concatenates `SIZE`, `GAP` and `DIRECTION` once and then, per entry, `CLS`, `BITMAP` and `PRINT n` into one spooler write or one TCP stream**, polls the status byte **once before and once after** (not per label), and sends `labels:progress` events (label k of n). **Ceilings, one set, stated once and used by 13.7:** at most **2,000 `labels` entries** and **8 MB of bitmap data** per job (a 50 x 25 mm bitmap at 203 dpi is 10,000 bytes, so about 800 distinct labels per job), `copies` 1 to 5,000 per entry (the count goes in the printer's own language, `PRINT` in TSPL and `^PQ` in ZPL, so 500 identical labels are one entry), and **at most 20,000 labels in total** (the sum of `copies`) per job; **the Print Labels screen splits a longer queue into sequential jobs** ("job 2 of 2": 1,240 distinct 50 x 25 labels are about 12.4 MB, so two jobs, two spooler writes), one at a time. Unknown keys, wrong types, sizes outside 15 to 300 mm, a bitmap whose `dataB64` length is not `widthBytes x heightDots`, a total over a ceiling, a printer id not in the live or allow-listed set: **refused with a reason, not repaired**. **For a raw job main builds the printer-language bytes itself** (`electron/labelLang.cjs`: `SIZE`, `GAP`, `DIRECTION`, `CLS`, `BITMAP`, `PRINT` for TSPL and the matching few for ZPL; **no command from the renderer ever reaches the printer**, which closes the dangerous part of a raw path: the TSPL manual lists `INITIALPRINTER`, `MENU`, `EXPORT`, `DOWNLOAD` and immediate ESC commands that change or restart the printer [R-lab 2.6]). The ZPL bitmap command (`^GF`) was not read by the research and is **proven against Zebra's manual and a printer before it ships**; TSPL's `BITMAP` is read.
- `labels.status(printerId)` for a TSPL network printer asks the printer's status byte (`<ESC>!?`: normal, head open, paper jam, out of paper, out of ribbon, paused, error, [R-lab 2.6]; the bit table is read from the manual by the builder) before and after a job so the screen can say "the printer is out of paper".
- **The Windows RAW helper (decided; the first text left it as "a small helper process" with no language, signing or spawn policy).** A **fixed PowerShell script bundled in the app** (`Add-Type` P/Invoke of `winspool.drv`: `OpenPrinter`, `StartDocPrinter` with datatype `RAW`, `WritePrinter`, `EndDocPrinter`), run **by main only** with fixed arguments (`-NoProfile -File <the bundled path>`), the printer name taken from the live `getPrintersAsync` list (never from the renderer), the bytes passed in a **temp file main writes under `userData/labels/` and deletes after**, and the script's **SHA-256 checked by main before every run** against the hash baked into the build. Rejected for v1: a **signed prebuilt exe** (no code-signing certificate exists in the build today [unverified]; an unsigned executable is exactly what SmartScreen and antivirus flag on a shop PC) and `node-printer`. Known risks, stated: a managed PC whose policy blocks PowerShell (P2 driver print remains), and some antivirus products treat any PowerShell that calls Win32 as suspicious; both are checked on Windows 10 and 11 default policy in T-7a's gate, and the allow-list and packaging notes name the script. **macOS raw (CUPS) is out of scope for v1.**
- One job at a time (`busy`, as `pdf:save`), the ceilings above, the answer `{ ok, bytes, how, status? }`, and **the screen says "sent to the printer", never "printed"** (Phase 29: "Share means open, never send"). Packaging: only a **rebuilt installer** carries it, and the Phase 29 PDF bridge **has never run on a real Electron binary**, so this bridge's first real run is part of T-7's gate, not an assumption.

### 13.7 Entry points

1. **After Accept** on a Purchase, Receipt Note, Stock Journal receipt or opening: `VoucherType.labelsAfterSave` (off by default, a tail field of the voucher type next to `printAfterSave`) opens **Print Labels** filled from the voucher's received lines: copies = quantity per line (**one per unit** for a serial line, **one per batch line** for a batch line), every row's copies editable. The toast action "Print labels" does the same for a type without the flag.
2. **The page `...` menu and Go To** ("Print labels"), the left-menu row, and the voucher's `...` for a tracked voucher: the same screen.
3. **From a selection.** The Phase 26 selection bar selects **vouchers** (its keys today: `P`, `E`, `I` bulk-print, `O`, `R`, with Space to toggle [C `selectionBar.tsx`]): **a bare `L`** joins them in the Day Book and the registers and opens Print Labels with the voucher's tracked lines. The selection engine is voucher-specific, so the tracking reports (Stock Summary's item rows, Batch-wise Stock, Serial Stock) select **item, batch and unit rows** with a small shared row-picker hook of this work (`ui/inventory/tracking/useRowPick.ts`: Space toggles, the same keys, a bare `L`), which the report grid's own selection bar (wave-a2 S4) replaces when it lands. A **queue** is refused whole with the reason above its ceiling: **2,000 source rows and 20,000 labels** for the raw path (the screen then splits an allowed queue into the bridge jobs of 13.6, each at most 2,000 entries and 8 MB, so one ceiling set serves both), **500 labels** for the browser path with the page count shown (the bulk-print rule: never cut, never silently capped [C `bulkPrint.ts`, MAX_BULK 100 for invoices]).
3a. **From the masters:** the stock item form's `...` menu and the Stock Items list (a selection with `L`) open Print Labels for that item (a shelf, hang-tag or bin label); the Batch Summary and Serial Stock rows do the same for a batch or a unit.
4. **Reprint:** from Print Labels' **Recent jobs** (a device-local log, 13.10) and from a unit's history screen ("Reprint label"); a unit's label is **reprinted, never re-minted**: its identifier is the same.
5. **Chords:** **none new** (T26): inside Print Labels the existing Print chord prints, `Tab` moves between the queue, the template and printer pickers and the preview, Enter edits a copies cell. A new chord, if ever wanted, is a row of `core/shortcutRegistry.ts` first.

**The Print Labels screen** (`labelPrint`, a pushed screen with a dirty probe while the queue is edited): a queue table (source, copies, an "IMEI 2 sticker" tick where the template offers it), the template picker (the library, filtered by the item's trade), the printer profile picker, the **live preview** (the same drawing), the compliance panel (13.8), buttons **Print**, **Test label**, **Calibrate**, and a total ("1,240 labels on 3 rolls of 50 x 25").

### 13.8 Compliance: warnings, never a guarantee (T24)

`core/labelRules.ts` is a pure `checkLabel(template, row, ctx) -> Warning[]`. It prints what the owner chose and flags what a rule seems to require; **it never says "compliant"** and never fills a date or a number silently. Rule text is paraphrased from the research (each cites its source); what could not be read is **not shipped**.

| Check | Kinds | What | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **MRP wording is locked** | any template with an `mrp` field | the field prints "MRP" and the amount and, in the `full` form, "incl. of all taxes" (an MRP is a maximum retail price inclusive of all taxes); free text beside the field cannot change it; no MRP field without those words | [R-lab 3.1 rules 2(m), 6(1)(e)] |
| **Numeral and letter height, by panel area (replaces the first text's flat 1 mm floor and its empty band table)** | `prepack`, `pack`, `dispense` | **Rule 7(2) of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules 2011, as substituted by GSR 629(E) of 23 June 2017 (in force 1 January 2018), puts the height of any numeral AND letter in the declarations into Table-I by the area A of the principal display panel** [W: the Department's consolidated book, https://consumeraffairs.gov.in/public/upload/admin/cmsfiles/whatsnews/Book_on_Legal_Metrology_Packaged_Commodities_Rules,2011_with_all_amendments_whatsnews.pdf]: **A under 50 cm2: 1.0 mm; 50 to 100: 1.5; 100 to 500: 2.5; 500 to 2,500: 4.0; over 2,500: 6.0**; where blown, formed or moulded onto the container: **2.0, 3.0, 4.0, 6.0, 6.0** (the first cell corrected by GSR 1373(E) of 7 November 2017); and the width of a letter or numeral at least **one third of its height** except for the numeral 1 and the letters i, I and l (7(3)). The "letters at least 1 mm, 2 mm embossed" sentence the first text relied on, via a commentary [Ws], is the **superseded** old 7(3). The five rows are **data in `core/labelRules.ts` with the citation**, not code. **Inputs:** a typed **principal display panel area (cm2)** on `prepack` and `pack` labels; **blank takes the band of the label's own area as a lower bound** (the label sits on the panel, so the panel is at least that large: it can under-state the band but never raise a false alarm) and the message says "enter the pack's panel area for an exact check"; at a boundary value (exactly 50, 100, 500 or 2,500 cm2) **the LOWER band applies** (corrected after the build and its review read the rendered pages of the consolidated book: Table-I is A <= 50 for 1.0 mm, 50 < A <= 100 for 1.5, 100 < A <= 500 for 2.5, 500 < A <= 2,500 for 4.0 and A > 2,500 for 6.0, so a boundary value belongs to the lower band; the first text read the extracted text, which had lost its inequality signs, as "the larger band" [unverified at the time]). **Measured on the glyph, not the font:** the numeral height checked is `fontMm x capHeightRatio(face)`, the ratio read from the face's own metrics by the layout engine's metrics provider (IBM Plex Sans digits are about 0.70 of the font size [J]: **read from the bundled font file in T-6b, never assumed**); for a face that cannot be measured (the OS face of a Devanagari name) the check says so. The area rule of 7(5) (a rectangular panel is height x width; a cylindrical pack 40 % of height x circumference; any other shape 40 % of the total surface) is printed beside the input, and the exemption where another law already requires the declaration (7(5): it still applies to net quantity, retail price, expiry or best-before and consumer-care details) is stated, not modelled. | [W: Rules 7(2), 7(3), 7(5)]; [R-vert 3] |
| **Do not cover the maker's declarations** | `shelf`, `revisedMrp`, `prepack` | a placement warning: a shop's own sticker is allowed for any other declaration but must not cover the maker's printing, and a lower-MRP sticker must not cover the original; nobody may obliterate or alter the printed MRP | [R-lab 3.1 rules 6(3), 18(5)] |
| **Revised MRP only lower** | `revisedMrp` | the revised figure must be below the batch's printed MRP | [R-lab 3.1; R-vert 3] |
| **Selling price above MRP** | `shelf`, `hangtag` | warns when a printed selling price exceeds the MRP | [R-lab 3.1 rule 18(2)] |
| **Packer-only kinds** | `prepack`, `pack` | hidden unless "we pack or import" is ticked; the declaration fields are `input.*` the person types (packer name and address, consumer care, licence numbers, country of origin); required ones left empty are flagged; a pre-pack in advance is the shop's own responsibility and **whether a given repack counts is not for the software to decide** | [R-lab 3.1, 3.5; Ws law-firm note] |
| **Food** | `prepack`, `pack` of a food item | a batch or lot, the date of packing and the expiry or use-by must be present and the FSSAI licence number; **date form (regulation 5(10)(b)(i), re-read in the review pass):** DD/MM/YY for a shelf life of up to 3 months, **month and year above 3 months, and since the proviso (operational 1 July 2022) DD/MM/YY is also acceptable above 3 months, so both forms pass and neither is flagged** (the first text flagged DD/MM/YY on a 6-month product); **regulation 8(1): a package of NOT MORE than 100 cm2** is exempt from the ingredient list, **lot or batch number**, nutrition information, additives declaration, licence number and logo, importer address and instructions (information, not an error; "not more than", so exactly 100 cm2 is exempt); **8(1)(a): a package under 30 cm2 need not carry the date of manufacture** (the multi-unit pack must); **8(3): a shelf life of 7 days or less needs only the expiry or use-by** | [W: FSSAI Labelling and Display compendium, Version-II 29 Dec 2021, regs 5(10), 8(1), 8(1)(a), 8(3); later versions not read]; [R-lab 3.2; R-bst 3.2] |
| **Drugs** | `batch` (the pharmacy shelf tag), `dispense` | the shelf tag is an internal rack tag and is fine; the **maker's** batch, dates and licence number are never re-printed onto a maker's pack. **The dispense label carries the Rule 94(2)(i) particulars** (supplier's name and address, patient's name and the quantity, the serial number of the entry in the prescription register, the dose for internal use, "for external use only" where it is) **and the exemption from rules 96 to 101 it buys is conditional on condition (3) of Rule 65 being satisfied (94(2)(ii))** [W]; Rule 65(3) is the prescription register (or, for a licensee who opted for it in writing, a serially numbered cash or credit memo book) whose entry carries the serial number, the date of supply, the prescriber's and patient's names and addresses, the drug and quantity, **for a Schedule C, H or H1 drug the manufacturer's name, batch number and date of expiry**, and the pharmacist's signature, with Schedule H1 in a separate register kept three years (65(3)(1)(a) to (h)) [W]. **Comsky keeps no prescription register**, so the label's register field is typed by the person, the label says so, and **the printed invoice is not the Rule 65(3) record** (8.2) | [W: CDSCO's consolidated Drugs Rules 1945, https://cdsco.gov.in/opencms/resources/UploadCDSCOWeb/2022/drug_rules/Drugs%20Rules,%201945%20(1).pdf, rules 65(3), 65(4), 94(2)]; [R-lab 3.3] |
| **HUID** | `jewel` | the HUID is a typed attribute **recorded after hallmarking, never generated or invented**; duplicate HUIDs across units are informed (one per article) | [R-lab 3.4, R-vert 3] |
| **GS1 trade barcode** | `prepack`, `pack`, and any EAN or UPC printed by an owner who holds a GS1 prefix | an EAN-13 for consumer items has a **minimum X-dimension of 0.264 mm (recommended 0.330, maximum 0.660) and a bar height of at least 18.28 mm (recommended 22.85)**, quiet zones 11 and 7 modules [W: GS1 Sweden's size guide, https://gs1.se/en/guides/how-to-guides/size-guide/]; at 203 dpi **2 dots is 0.250 mm, below the minimum, 3 dots 0.375 mm**, so the profile's `minModuleDots` is 3 for these kinds (the first text defaulted 2 for every template, including a code "a stranger's till will scan") and a field below 18.28 mm of bar height warns "below the GS1 minimum"; Code 128 unit stickers are the shop's own and keep the 2-dot floor | [W]; [R-lab 1.7] |
| **Does not fit** | all | the barcode needs more dots than the field gives (13.3) | the engine |

A **label rules suite** pins the wording, **the five rows of Table-I and the moulded column with their citation and the boundary rule**, the width rule, the cap-height measurement (and "cannot be measured" for an OS face), the FSSAI date-form and exemption cases, the GS1 minimums, that no code path produces the word "compliant", and that `prepack` and `pack` never appear without the packer flag.

### 13.9 The designer (T-7: constrained, second)

Not a free drag-and-drop product (hit-testing, snapping, stacking, text fitting and undo are a product of their own, and BarTender exists for the shops that need it [R-lab 4.2]). A **field picker with a live preview**, shaped like the cheque layout editor [C `chequeLayoutEditor.tsx`]: choose a library template to start from; edit the **stock** (size, across, gaps, or the sheet grid); add a field from the `FieldSrc` list; each field is a row of numeric cells (x, y, width, height in millimetres, font, align, fit) walked with Tab and nudged with the arrow keys in **one-dot steps** (0.125 mm at 203 dpi, 0.085 mm at 300); the preview is the real drawing; the checks panel (13.3, 13.8) updates live; a **test print** sits beside Save. Limits: 40 custom templates, 30 fields each, the same caps as 3.1. Custom templates are stored in the **`labelTemplates` table** if the owner accepts the one optional Dexie bump (T20, section 3.7) and otherwise device-local with a JSON file export and import (the Settings precedent); the library is code either way, and library ids are never reused.

### 13.10 The label log, minted codes, settings

- **Log:** a device-local `meta` row per job (`labelLog:<cid>:<timestamp>:<random>`: who, when, template id, printer id, copies, the source voucher or unit keys, a count), the newest 1,000 kept; it is the **Recent jobs** list and drives reprint. It is not synced in v1 (a synced log would be a table or a voucher-sized document; it can be added with the T-7 table).
- **Minted codes** (13.4): an item printed without a primary code asks "assign a barcode to this item?" (needs the Masters right, writes one `ItemBarcode` of the **040 to 049** block) or prints its alias in Code 128; a batch label mints `BatchRec.code` on first print (an overlay write, Inventory right) so a **batch scan resolves exactly** (14.4); a unit label prints the unit's own identifier (nothing is minted).
- **Rights and settings:** printing labels needs the **Inventory** area's view (a person who can see stock can print a label); reprinting a unit's label the same; template editing is **Masters**. Printer profiles, scanner and the "we pack or import" flag are **device** settings (12). A thermal-transfer ribbon for a durable sticker (a phone's warranty, a pharmacy shelf) against direct-thermal stock that fades is a **hardware choice the owner confirms** [R-lab 2.1, unverified].

---

## 14. Scanning

### 14.1 What works with no scan layer at all

A keyboard-wedge scanner types the code as ordinary key events and, usually, Enter. In any plain field that is already a scan: the serial grid's entry field takes the IMEI and Enter adds the row (6.3 rule 6); the item field takes a code stored in the item's alias or part number and Enter picks the first substring match [C `VoucherScreen.tsx` item `popup`]. What fails today, silently, is everywhere else: an unknown code does nothing and the next scan **appends** to it; a burst that lands in Narration ends in Accept (Enter in Narration accepts [C]); on the Accept bar a scan's Enter answers **Yes**; on a menu or report the letters are hotkeys; a 13-digit EAN in Quantity becomes a quantity of 8.9 trillion and a 15-digit IMEI of 356 trillion with no limit [P, R-aud 3.14]. The scan layer exists to **make those safe and to add exact lookup, GS1 and scale parsing, scan-to-add, the stock-take and the setup screen**. Everything in this paragraph needs no layer; the layer (14.2 and 14.3) is the opt-in part (T25), as the labels research recommended: *fix the silent failure first, add the detector second, and keep it opt-in* [R-lab 5.3]. **Without the layer**, in a company with `features.tracking`, Enter on the item field resolves a typed or scanned text that **exactly** equals a registry code before falling back to today's substring list, and an unknown code is **said** ("not found") instead of ignored; a company that never opted in keeps today's behaviour byte for byte. **Two protections exist without the layer, because the hazard is present from the first day a counter has a scanner** (the first text made every guard opt-in): (1) a **serial line's quantity is capped at 5,000** whatever any setting says (R4: a 15-digit IMEI typed or scanned into Quantity is refused, not booked as 356 trillion pending identities); (2) a company with `features.tracking` on and **no scanner configured** runs a **passive burst observer**, one capture listener that **only measures key timing**, never holds, swallows, delays or alters a key, and whose only output is a one-line hint ("This looks like a barcode scanner: turn on scanner protection in Settings > Devices", with "Caps Lock is on" where it is). **The identity claims are therefore about events, not listeners**: with the layer off no key is ever held, swallowed, delayed or altered and the dispatcher hands every handler the same event object (asserted); a company without `features.tracking` installs nothing at all.

### 14.2 The detector (`core/scanInput.ts`, pure state machine)

`feedKey(state, ev, now) -> { state, action }` over a minimal key record `{ key, code, ctrl, alt, meta, shift, repeat, composing, altGraph, t }`; `action` is one of `pass` (the key goes on as it always did), `hold` (suppress this bare key briefly), `swallow`, `emit(scan)`, `release(heldKeys)`. Testable with synthetic events and timestamps, no DOM.

- **The clock is `KeyboardEvent.timeStamp`** (the time the browser created the event), **never `Date.now()` or `performance.now()` read inside the handler**: while React is rendering a controlled input the handler runs late and every key would be stamped a render apart, so a scanner sending 13 characters 3 ms apart on a slow screen (66 ms a render at a 30,000-item master and 120,000 vouchers, [R-lab 5.2], computed from the POS code audit and not re-run) would read as a human typist and detection would fail on exactly the slowest screens, where the protections matter. The key record's `t` is that stamp; the first test of the detector holds the main thread for 66 ms inside every keydown and still sees 3 ms gaps.
- **What a scan is.** A run of at least `minLen` (default 4) printable keys, each gap at most `gapMs` (default 35, **learned** from **several** setup scans as twice the **95th** percentile of their gaps, clamped to 15 to **60** (the first text said the 99th percentile and 120: with up to 400 pooled gaps the 99th is the maximum, so one stray 60 ms gap taught 120 ms, at which a person typing `7000` and Enter at 110 ms a key read as a scan and the Enter was swallowed; the review corrected it. A person's OWN value may still go to 120, the setup screen warns above 60, and a gap above 60 asks for at least 6 keys); the setup screen keeps **mean, 99th percentile and maximum** and **a separate profile for a USB and a Bluetooth connection**, because a Bluetooth scanner's gaps are wider and less regular [J, no hardware]), the 90th percentile of the gaps at most `gapMs` and no gap over `3 x gapMs`, ended by a terminator (Enter or Tab, per setting) or by `idleMs` (default 100) of silence for a scanner configured with none. The rule is read over the WHOLE run, the first gap included (no head key is trimmed: a scanner whose first character is late, 36 to 105 ms, keeps it), and **a burst that stalls and then resumes is never read as two scans**: a run dropped by a stall or an expired hold is remembered for 1 s if it was scanner-shaped (two keys or more) and 300 ms otherwise, and the run that follows is discarded as a broken read with its terminator (review of 2026-10-24); a second terminator within `gapMs` of a scan's own is the same scan's. A human typing a short code quickly (80 ms a key) fails the test; the defaults begin from the measured behaviour of a well-known browser library (100 ms to end, about 30 ms a character, 6-character minimum, Enter or Tab to end) and are widened or narrowed only by the learned numbers [R-lab 5.3].
- **Never read as a scan:** a key with `composing` or `key === 'Process'` or keyCode 229 (an IME: Indian shops switch to Hindi and other script layouts, [R-lab 5.2]); `key === 'Dead'`; **AltGr** (`getModifierState('AltGraph')`, and Ctrl plus Alt together on Windows [C `keys.ts translateEvent`]: a character being typed); `repeat` (a held key); any chord with Ctrl, Alt or Meta that is not part of a recognised scan; Shift alone is fine. Each ends the run and passes through.
- **Alt plus keypad ("keypad emulation")**: some scanners send every character as Alt held while digits are typed on the keypad; `combo()` reads such a press as `Alt+<digit>` because it recovers the physical key only for `Digit` codes, so a scan containing a 2 on a screen that binds `Alt+2` (Duplicate) could duplicate a voucher [R-lab 5.2]. With the layer on, an `Alt` + `Numpad*` keydown is **swallowed** (never reaching the stack) and the setup screen names the mode and tells the person to switch it off; decoding it is not attempted (nothing to test it against).
- **Prefix, suffix, symbology identifier.** Configured printable prefix and suffix characters are stripped; a leading `]E0`, `]E4`, `]C1`, `]d2`, `]Q3` or `]A` + digit is stripped and kept as a **hint** (a `]d2` or `]C1` read is GS1 by declaration, a bare 13-digit string is not); the same code is accepted **with and without** the identifier [R-lab 1.6]. `Ctrl+]` inside a run is the group separator and becomes `\x1d` (a keyboard cannot type GS; a scanner sends the chord, drops it or substitutes Tab or a space [R-lab 1.3, 5.2]).
- **Layout.** A wedge scanner sends key **positions**; the OS turns them into characters through the active layout, and a shop that switches to a script layout gets the wrong characters. Setting `keyboard: 'os'` (default; use `e.key`) or `'us'` (decode from `e.code` plus Shift as the US layout the scanner is normally programmed for). The setup scan (14.7) tells which one reproduces the test string. **This is a design proposal, not something that could be tested** [R-lab 0.8, J, unverified].
- **Caps Lock (the first text never mentioned it; R-lab 5.2 lists it as a trap).** In the default `os` mode a scanner that sends a capital as Shift plus the letter arrives **case-inverted when Caps Lock is on** (the browser inverts it), and Caps Lock is common at accounting desks; exact-lookup codes bear case (a minted batch code `B7K2Q9X`, a letter-prefixed scale label `W00319801500`, GS1 AI 10 and 21 values). The layer reads `getModifierState('CapsLock')` at the first key of a run and, when it is on, **derives every ASCII letter of the run from `shiftKey`** (`shift ? upper : lower`, which is the case the scanner meant: exactly the `us` rule), so the code arrives right whatever the lock; the chip says "Caps Lock is on: read as B7K2Q9X". **Lookups of the registry, aliases, scale prefixes and batch codes are case-insensitive** (the mint alphabet is upper-case only and today's item alias match already lowercases both sides); a stored GS1 batch or serial **keeps the case it was read with**. With the layer off, the passive observer hints "Caps Lock is on" (14.1); nothing can repair a wedge scan it never sees. The setup scan includes upper and lower case and tells the person to toggle Caps Lock and rescan if the two decodes differ.
- **Cap and linearity.** The buffer is capped at 256 characters and every scan of the text is linear (the Phase 24 rule: cap before any pattern test).

### 14.3 Where it sits and how a scan reaches the field

**Installed only when enabled.** With "I use a barcode scanner" off on this device **no scan-layer listener is added and nothing is held** (the timing-only passive observer of 14.1, which exists only for a tracking company with no scanner configured, never holds, delays, swallows or alters a key): the dispatcher keeps handing every handler the **same event object** (asserted, 16.2, the Phase 27 identity). When on, `installScanLayer()` adds **one capture-phase `keydown` listener on `window`**, the place `installAltReveal` already sits and for the same reason (a capture listener runs before the dispatch stack's bubble listener, so it sees every key and can stop one from reaching the stack by `stopPropagation()`, which also prevents the later bubble phase on `window`) [C `keys.ts installAltReveal`; the claim that `stopPropagation` in a window capture listener prevents the same window's bubble listener is the DOM event model and is a **required first test** in a real browser]. `keys.ts` is one writer (Back adds `runEscape()` there); the dispatch loop body is extracted to an exported `dispatchKey(ev)` so a held key can be **replayed through the same stack**.

**Three contexts, three behaviours, and a backstop under them:**

1. **A scan target** (a field that wants a scan: the unit grid's entry field, the stock-take box, the label scan-back box, the search box of each report, and **the item-name field of an item voucher when the layer is on**). These are rendered as **`ui/scan/ScanInput`**, a thin controlled-input wrapper that is the plain `<input>` when the layer is off and the same element with a registration when on. For `keyboard: 'us'` the layer **holds up to 3 keydowns** (about 100 ms) with `preventDefault`; if no burst follows it **replays them as typed** through `ScanInput.insertTyped(key)` (a human types through the OS layout, so a held key is inserted as `e.key`); if a burst follows it consumes the whole run into its buffer. For `keyboard: 'os'` nothing is held: the characters reach the input as typed and **at the terminator** the layer recognises the run by the recorded `KeyboardEvent.timeStamp`s (14.2), restores the field's text to what it was before the run, swallows the terminator and calls `onScan(code)`. **The first text said "either way one scan is one change", but in `os` mode thirteen characters reach a controlled input one by one, which is thirteen renders** (the 1.5 s budget of 3.7 concedes about 115 ms a character at 120,000 vouchers, and the item popup refilters every item per render). So **`ScanInput` is uncontrolled while a run is in progress**: it keeps the DOM value itself and tells its owner (the screen's state, the popup's filter) **once per run**: the first key of a run is delivered at once, keys arriving within `gapMs` of the previous one are **coalesced** and flushed when a gap exceeds `gapMs`, at the terminator, or after `idleMs` (a typist at 80 ms a key is never coalesced; a scan is therefore at most two changes of the screen's state, not thirteen). **An exact code is resolved (14.4) before the substring list is built**, so a scan never refilters 30,000 items per character. The burst test (16.4) runs against a 30,000-item master and a 120,000-voucher book.
2. **An input that is not a target** (Quantity, Rate, Amount, Narration, a master field): characters reach it (they cannot be un-typed without holding, which would delay a person's typing); **at the terminator the layer restores the field to its pre-run text, swallows the terminator** (so Narration's Enter does not Accept) and shows "Scan into an item field" (a lane message, `ui/feedback.tsx`). A bare-key accident inside a **numeric** field is the other guard: with the layer on, a Quantity that is **8 or more bare digits** is refused with "That looks like a barcode, not a quantity" (more input is never refused otherwise; no real quantity has eight bare digits, and it is on only for a device that chose the layer).
3. **A bare-key context** (a menu, a report, the Accept bar, a screen with no focused input). **Defined narrowly after review**: focus is on `body`, or on a **non-interactive container**: no `input`, `textarea`, `select`, `button`, `a`, `[contenteditable]`, no `[role=button|option|menuitem|checkbox|radio|tab|switch]`, **nothing inside `#navrail`, `.nv-tree`, the Settings window or an open dialog**. The first text defined it as "no focused input", which includes a focused rail row, a report row and a Settings control, whose keys are handled by **React** (React 18 delegates at `#root`, below `window` [C `NavRail.tsx` header: "React 18 delegates at #root, which sits below `window`"], and `keys.ts` has one window bubble listener [C `keys.ts`]): a window-capture hold that cancels the first key and replays it through `dispatchKey` reaches only the key stack, never a React handler and never a native default action, so a held Space on the Settings choice groups or the Lock button [C `settings/controls.tsx`: Space is handled in React] would do nothing, including on the very "I use a barcode scanner" control, which a keyboard-only user could then not switch off. **Only in a bare-key context** is the first bare printable key **held for up to `gapMs`**; if the next key arrives inside it the run is a scan and every key until the terminator is swallowed (letters never fire hotkeys); if the timer expires the held key is **replayed through `dispatchKey`** into the key stack (a human's `D` on a menu arrives `gapMs` late, a delay only on a device that turned the layer on). **Everywhere else the key passes as it always did**; a scan landing in the rail or a Settings control is simply typed there. Only an unmodified single-character key is ever held; Enter, Tab, arrows and chords are never delayed. A recipe presses Space and a letter on every control kind with the layer on.
4. **A timing-independent backstop for the two destructive terminators (the guarantee is "reduces", not "never").** The layer's protection rests on timing, and a Bluetooth scanner can stall 150 ms mid-code on a retry [J, unverified: no hardware]: the run then splits into two short runs, neither reaches the scan rule, and the code's trailing Enter lands on the Accept bar, where **Enter and Y answer Yes with Yes armed** [C `widgets.tsx` `AcceptPrompt`: `initial = 'yes'`, `c === 'Y'` and `c === 'Enter'` call `onYes`], or in Narration, where **Enter accepts the voucher**, saving it with a barcode as the narration. So, with the layer on, **independently of any gap measurement**: an **Enter or Y on the Accept bar that follows a printable key by less than about 300 ms is ignored** (with a one-line "Looks like a scan: press Y again to accept"), and **Narration's Enter-accept is ignored when 6 or more characters were typed in the last 300 ms** (no human types 20 characters a second; a scan stalled mid-code still has 6 characters inside the window). These two cannot protect the first character of a run (a `Y` that opens a scan) or a different field; they are the cheap floor under the timing rule, and the irregular traces of 16.4 (10, 10, 150, 10 ms) pin both. Every statement of this section is therefore "**reduces**": **nothing here proves a scan can never reach the wrong field**, and the owner's own scanner is the test (14.7).

**Routing.** `ui/scan/targets.ts` is a stack like the key stack: `registerScanTarget({ id, kinds, onScan, priority })` returns an unregister function; the top target wins (an open dialog over a focused field over the screen's own target over the global "find"); a screen with no target answers "Not here: scan into an item field". A scan is resolved by `core/scanResolve.ts` (14.4) and the **result**, not the text, is handed to the target (a target may ask for raw text).

### 14.4 What a code means (`core/scanResolve.ts`, pure)

One exact lookup through a **derived registry** (`barcodeIndex(items, batchRecs, unitIndex)`, a `Map<code, Target[]>` built once per change of the items and overlays, **never a substring filter**), in this order, stopping at the first hit [R-bst 8.2, R-lab 6.3]:

1. a **unit** (any identifier of any live or past unit, through the unit index; an IMEI-shaped code that passes Luhn is a candidate);
2. a **batch code** (`BatchRec.code`, minted at the first label print, 13.4);
3. an **item, pack or variant code** (`ItemBarcode`: `pack` adds `packQty`; `variant` resolves to `(item, VariantKey)` by **option ids**, so a renamed colour keeps its printed code [R-vert 6.5]; a retired code (`off`) says so and never resolves silently);
4. a **scale code** under a configured scale profile (below);
5. a **GS1 element string**, parsed (below) and then resolved by 1 to 3;
6. the item's `alias` or `partNo` (today's behaviour);
6a. **in a party field, Go To and a report's find box only:** a ledger's `alias` that equals the code exactly (a customer card or a supplier code a shop prints) finds the party (scan-to-find a party); it is never tried in an item field;
7. **unknown**: said, never guessed, never appended anywhere.

**Collisions.** A code on two items, or an item code with several live batches or MRPs, opens a **picker** (the proposed batch pre-selected) instead of choosing silently (Busy needs a "duplicate barcode" setting plus "allow multiple MRP for the same barcode" for this, in three FAQs [R-bst 8.2]); a duplicate across items is a Data Check row.

**GS1 (`barcode/gs1.ts parseGs1`).** The AI table is deliberately small: fixed length **00** (18), **01** and **02** (14), **11, 13, 15, 17** (6 each, `YYMMDD`), **310n** (6; `n` is the number of decimals); variable up to 20 **10** (batch or lot) and **21** (serial), up to 30 **240** and **241**, up to 8 **30** and **37** (count). AI 11 to 17 and 31 to 36 are fixed length and need no separator; a variable field ends at `\x1d` or the end [Ws: the Release 16 table as a vendor copy and GS1 change notices; the current General Specifications were **not read** and the table is checked against them before release]. **An unknown AI is rejected with the raw text shown, never guessed. A variable field followed by more data with no separator is refused, not split by a rule of thumb** (a wrong batch number recorded silently is worse than an error; the labels research read this as "shortest rule and flagged", and this spec departs from it on purpose), the message saying "no group separator found: set the scanner to send it, or type the batch". Dates: `YYMMDD` to ISO as `20YY` (a documented simplification of GS1's sliding century window, which only matters for dates from 2051), a day of `00` meaning **the last day of the month** (the same reading as 3.4 [J; GS1's own wording was not confirmed]). A GTIN resolves an item by its `ItemBarcode.code` as the 14-digit form, the 13-digit EAN (leading zero stripped) or the item's code. **Reading a pack's QR or a GS1 DataMatrix needs a 2-D imager**: a shop with a 1-D laser or CCD scanner (`reads2D: no`, 14.7) cannot read them at all, so "a scan fills a batch row" never happens there and the batch grid says so once ("your scanner reads bar codes only: type the batch and expiry"). **A drug pack's QR on the top-300 brands carries the manufacturer's own string, not necessarily GS1, and often only a web address** [R-bst 3.2, R-lab 3.3]: when the text is not GS1 it is reported as "unrecognised pack code; type the batch and expiry" (a URL gets "this pack's code is a web address: it carries no batch") and kept as a reference; **a scan fills a batch row only when its payload parses**. A taught-layout slice for a maker's text format is a later add (section 17 notes it).

**Scale codes (`core/scale.ts`).** A **profile** per scale: `{ prefix (digits or letters; **matched case-insensitively**), itemDigits, valueDigits, value: 'weightKg' | 'weightG' | 'pricePaise' | 'priceRupees' | 'count', decimals, check: 'ean' | 'none', valueCheckAt?: number (an embedded check digit over the value block, which some schemes add [R-lab 1.5]), second?: { digits, value, decimals } (a second value block: the Indian POS vendor's 16-character `W361802000004000` carries **weight and price** [R-vert 3.3]) }`, set once per device by the setup scan ("scan one printed label, type what it weighs, confirm the split"). Built-in **test vectors**: the 13-digit form `21` + 5 item digits + 5 weight digits (two whole, three decimal) + the EAN check (`2100123012503` is item 00123 at 1.250 kg), and the Indian POS vendor's letter forms (`W` + 6 item digits + 5 grams; `U` + 6 item digits + 5 count) [R-vert 3.3, R-lab 1.5]. An item's `ItemBarcode` of kind `scale` holds the digits the scale prints (the PLU). A label whose item digits match no item is **unknown**, never a guess. No Indian scale maker's own format was found [unverified]; the shop's own printed label is the test.

### 14.5 What a scan does

| Where | Result kind | Action |
|---|---|---|
| **Item field of an item voucher** (the target) | item, variant, pack | picks the item; quantity 1 (or `packQty`), godown default; **a repeat scan of the same item and cell on a non-serial line adds to that line's quantity** (a line flash, no new row); the cursor stays in a **new blank row's name field** for the next scan; a tracked batch item allocates by the proposal automatically on an outward voucher when "auto-allocate scans" is on (default on), otherwise opens the panel; a tracked serial item opens the unit grid |
| | unit (IMEI or serial) | outward: the unit's item row is found or made, the unit **added to that row's `serials`** (quantity = count) after R3 judges it, **stored under its primary id (a scanned IMEI 2 is rewritten, 3.3)**; **a repeat is a duplicate and never increments** ("already on line 3"); inward: "scan the item first" (with the **TAC suggestion** of 3.3: units of one item share a type code, so an unknown IMEI whose first 8 digits match one item's units suggests that item) |
| | batch code | outward: a line for that cell, quantity 1 (a repeat increments); inward: the batch grid with the batch filled |
| | GS1 | fills item, batch (AI 10), expiry (AI 17), serial (AI 21) and count (AI 37) **when it parses**; every filled value still passes R11, R12, R25 |
| | scale | item by PLU; quantity from the weight or count; for a price-embedded label the amount is the price and the rate is derived; never a guess |
| | collision / several MRPs | the picker (14.4) |
| | unknown | the field clears and keeps focus; a **prompt** "Code 8901234567890 is not in your items: Create item with this code (C), Attach to an existing item (A), Esc" (create opens the stock-item form pre-filled with the code; attach picks an item and adds an `ItemBarcode`; today an unknown scan is silent [R-aud 3.3]) |
| **Unit grid** (inward or outward) | unit | one scan, one row, judged at once (6.3) |
| **Credit Note, return mode** | unit | finds the sale, shows the customer and warranty, fills the line (R5, 7.3) |
| **Stock-take screen** | unit, batch or item code | raises the count of the cell or lists the unit as present (7.6); a location code selects the godown |
| **Stock Journal** | unit, batch | fills the source rows of the chosen godown (the destination mirrors them) |
| **Party field, Go To, a report's find box** | any | a party code (a ledger's alias) picks or opens the party; an item, batch or unit code filters or opens the stock view |
| **Print Labels** | item, batch, unit | adds a queue row; scanning an old label **reprints** it (13.7) |
| **Elsewhere** | any | swallowed with "Not here: scan into an item field" (burst) |

### 14.6 Feedback

A scan always **says what it did**: a chip under the field ("Read as: GS1 batch B2410A, expiry 31-Mar-2027", "Read as: unit 356938035643809"), a **flash** of the row it touched or added to, and an optional **sound** (setting `scanSound`: off, errors only (default), all: a short low double tone for a duplicate or an error, a single tone for "ok" only when set to all; WebAudio, no asset). A duplicate says which line already holds it; a partial read (under `minLen`, or no terminator inside the idle time) is discarded with the soft tone only if sound is on. A wrong EAN check digit on a **typed** alias is a soft warning and kept; on a scan a hard reject; an IMEI failing Luhn or length warns and allows with the row's chip (R9). **Nothing a scan does is ever applied silently:** every effect is visible on the screen it landed on.

### 14.7 The scanner setup screen (Settings, Devices, Scanner)

A short guided check that doubles as the label scan-back: (1) **a "scan here" box** that shows exactly what arrived (the raw characters, the key codes, a symbology identifier if present, the terminator and the measured gaps), so a bad day is diagnosable; (2) scan the printed test sentence `Ab-1_9/Zz`: the screen decides `keyboard: 'os'` or `'us'` by which decode reproduces it and says so; (3) it reports the terminator (Enter, Tab or none) and sets `idleMs`; (4) it measures the gaps and writes `gapMs`; (5) scan a code with a `GS` (the test label carries a GS1-128 sample): it says whether the separator arrives and as what (Ctrl+]; nothing; Tab; space), and advises serial mode for GS1 work; (6) it flags **Alt keypad emulation** (the characters appear slowly with Alt flicker) and tells the person to switch it off; (7) it flags a **language input method** (a `Process` key) and tells the person to switch the keyboard to English for scanning; (8) **it scans a printed QR and a printed Data Matrix (both on the test label) and records `reads2D`**: `yes` when both arrive, `no` when neither does (a 1-D laser or CCD scanner: the template picker then substitutes Code 128 for QR-only templates and the pack-QR reading of 14.4 is switched off with a sentence), `unknown` until the test is run; the screen also names the scanner model the person types, which section 17 asks for; (9) **Caps Lock**: it reads the lock at the first key and, if the decode with and without the shift correction differ, says "Caps Lock is on: scan again with it off, or leave the correction on" (14.2); (10) it stores the result per device **and per connection** (a USB and a Bluetooth profile are kept apart, 14.2). **None of these steps was ever run against a real scanner**: the owner's own scanner is the test (section 17).

### 14.8 Camera scanning (T-10, later; behind one interface)

A screen asks for "a scan" through `ui/scan/source.ts` (`ScanSource`: the wedge, and later a camera) and never cares which. The camera needs a **secure context**, and **the live site is HTTP only** [R-aud 1 item 11], so it is for the desktop app (a packaged `file://` page is expected to count as secure: **test `window.isSecureContext` in the packaged build**, and install a main-process permission handler limited to the main window and `media`; the macOS build needs the camera usage text in the bundle's `Info.plist`, absent today [R-lab 5.4]) and for a phone shell or a TLS site. Decoding: the `BarcodeDetector` API where present (Chromium, partial; off by default in Safari; absent in Firefox), else a **self-hosted WebAssembly decoder** (a dependency decision: about 0.9 MB for the reader build [R-lab 5.4]); formats EAN, UPC, Code 128, Code 39, ITF, QR, Data Matrix. The camera is for a phone doing a stock-take or a price lookup, **not** a counter doing 300 bills a day (a handheld wedge reads in a fraction of a second without aiming). T-10's scope is the interface, a camera mode of `ScanInput` for the unit grid and the stock-take, and the TLS gate; WebUSB and Web Serial printing ride the same TLS gate.

---

## 15. File ownership, slices, exit gates, effort, and the order against the running work

### 15.1 Lanes and the one-writer files

Single writer per file at any moment (wave-a2 rule A2-5; CF's lane names are reused where a file is shared). **Lanes:** **M** model and pure engines (new files); **I** the inventory engine (CF's lane I, one writer per file); **E** masters and the editor; **V** voucher entry; **R** reports and the new-screen checklist; **S** scan input; **P** print, labels and the Electron bridge; **X** exchange; **B** server and sync; **D** data, backup, store.

**Files with one writer at a time** (the audit's list [R-aud 6], with who else wants them): `src/types.ts` (CF-0, then T-0b) · `src/core/inventory.ts`, `openingStock.ts`, `valuation.ts`, `stockSummary.ts`, `splitCompany.ts` (CF-9a to CF-9d, then T-0b, then T-3 for the `billsNote` rule) · `src/ui/VoucherScreen.tsx` (Back's `onClose` edits, then CF-2, CF-3, **T-2a** (which creates `useStockDims` and the allocation stop), then CF-9c adopting that hook, **then T-2b**, then T-5b's item-field target; question 22 may move T-2a ahead of CF-2) · `src/ui/widgets.tsx` (Back's `Box.onClose`) · `src/keys.ts` (Back's `runEscape`, then T-5b) · `src/state/store.ts` (CF-0, CF-4a, then the `Screen` union once for T-4) · `src/ui/router.tsx`, `core/access.ts`, `state/recents.ts`, `ui/reportsCenter/catalogue.ts`, `ui/overlays.tsx`, `ui/modern/CommandPalette.tsx`, `ui/MenuScreen.tsx`, `ui/navTree.ts` + `scripts/assert/sidenav.assert.ts`, `ui/quickCreate.ts` (the new-screen checklist: CF-4a once, then T-4 once) · `src/ui/inventory/masters.tsx` (CF-1, then T-1) · `src/core/backupMigrate.ts` (CF-0, then T-0b) · `src/sync/sync.ts`, `src/sync/caps.ts`, `backend/src/{conflict,sync,pushRun,histories,restWrite,rest}.js` (CF-6a, then T-9) · `src/core/einvoice.ts`, `src/ui/print/{invoiceModel.ts,PrintVoucher.tsx}`, `src/core/printConfig.ts`, `src/styles/print.css` (CF-2's print block, then T-4) · `src/core/tallyImport.ts`, `tallyExport.ts`, `importMap.ts` (CF-10, then T-8) · `src/core/integrity.ts` (T-0b) · `electron/{main,preload}.cjs` (**two writers, sequenced**: the Back spec's optional phase 2, slice B-6 / decision B8, edits both (Windows `app-command` `browser-backward`, macOS `swipe`, one IPC message [C `back-nav-spec.md` section 10.2, B8]) and is built only if the owner says so (its Q3); T-7a edits both for the label bridge; whichever goes second rebases on the first, and neither starts while the other is open) · `docs/parity-data.json` · **`mcp/engine-lock.json` and `mcp/src/data/fields.ts`** (last).

### 15.2 The slices

Effort is builder-days **[J]**, tests and screenshots included, with the owner's pace in mind; no calendar is promised (it depends on how many lanes run at once). Every slice ends with its suites green, **every mutation of its list killed**, `tsc` 0, the build green, and the sweeps unchanged where it says so.
