**Total about 145 to 180 builder-days [J]** across T-0 to T-10 (T-0 17 to 21; T-1 7 to 9; T-2 16 to 20; T-3 13 to 16; T-4 12 to 14; T-5 14 to 17; T-6 with the spike 27 to 33; T-7 14 to 18; T-8 9 to 11; T-9 12 to 15; T-10 4 to 5). The first text said 130 to 165; the review added the rule W and cost-ledger work, the dual-SIM pair mode, the printed spike, the multi-label raw job, the device capabilities and the rebuild job. **The T-5b and T-7a figures are floors**: both depend on hardware and on an Electron binary that nobody has run, so they can only grow. The **owner's headline scenarios** (receive 40 phones by scan, sell with FEFO, a unit's history, a recall, a sticker per unit) need **T-0, T-1, T-2, T-4 and T-6 (a, s, b, c)**, about **75 to 92 of those days**, without the server work, the Electron bridge or the designer. **Two statements the first text left out:** (1) those days **start after T-0b, which waits for CF-9a to be committed, reviewed and green on a rebased `main`, and T-2a, which sits after CF-3 on the voucher screen unless the owner moves it** (15.3): the elapsed time is set by that order, not by the sum of the days, and until then Phase 0 delivers only inert files that nothing imports (about 32 to 39 days of T-0a, T-5a, T-6a, T-6b and the pure half of T-7a), which no user can see; (2) **the label half is conditional on the one-day printed spike (T-6s)**: if the browser dialog cannot print one label per page on the shop's printers, every web customer has only the A4 sheet or a PDF until T-7a, which needs the owner's hardware.

### 15.3 The order against the running custom-fields and Back work

**The facts that decide it:** the CF build is in `.wt-cf` on branch `cf-back-nav` (base `baa0c45`, the **pre-gate snapshot** of wave 3; against the committed `2cee555` it lacks 84 files and 3,338 inserted lines of gate fixes, so it needs a rebase before it merges [R-aud 4.2 item 4]); it edits `inventory.ts` (CF-9a), `types.ts` (CF-0), `VoucherScreen.tsx` (Back's `onClose`, then CF-2, CF-3, CF-9c), `masters.tsx` (CF-1) and `backend/src/sync.js` (split into `pushRun.js`, `histories.js`, `mismatch.js`, `restWrite.js`) [C `.wt-cf`]. **Its state at the review pass:** CF-9a is written but **uncommitted and unreviewed** in the working tree (the branch tip `f446674` is "WIP checkpoint 2 ... unreviewed", and `git status` there lists dozens of modified files); the CF lane totals **96 to 115 builder-days** and is **mid-build** (`VoucherScreen.tsx` is already +329 lines there); and the CF spec's own order on the voucher screen is Back's overlay edits, then CF-2 (10 to 12 days), CF-3 (6 to 7) and CF-9c (7 to 8) with "never two of them at once" [C `custom-fields-spec.md` sections 16 and 17]. **Written against that, the first text's plan had three defects:** it put about 50 builder-days of T-0b, T-1, T-2, T-3 and T-4 against the tip of an unreviewed, ungated branch whose base lacks 84 files and 3,338 lines of wave 3 gate fixes; it made T-2 wait for CF-9c, so **no batch or IMEI could appear on a voucher until CF-0, CF-1, CF-2, CF-3, CF-9a and CF-9c had landed and been gated**, while Phase 0 delivered only inert files; and it quoted "70 to 85 days" without saying they start after that. **Decisions (T27, question 22):** (1) **T-0b is gated on CF-9a being committed, reviewed and green on a rebased `main`**, and until then **only T-0a's cell algebra** (`stockCell.ts`, new files) is a dependency of the pure slices; (2) **the tracking lane builds `useStockDims` and the allocation stop (T-2a) between CF-3 and CF-9c, and CF-9c adopts the hook** (15.4) instead of T-2 waiting behind CF-9c: CF-2 and CF-3 are not delayed, CF-9c waits about 9 to 11 days; **the owner may instead jump T-2a ahead of CF-2** (the owner's own request would reach a voucher about 16 to 19 days of CF voucher work earlier, at the price of holding CF-2 for T-2a's 9 to 11 days and rebasing CF-2 over it), or leave tracking entirely behind CF-9c (the first text's order; tracking then reaches a voucher only after Back's overlay edits and CF-0 and then CF-2, CF-3 and CF-9c, which are 23 to 27 sequential voucher-screen days on their own [J, summed from the CF spec's effort column], plus T-2a). **Nothing here may edit those files in parallel** (the Edit tool refuses a stale file, and a stale full-file write would silently drop another builder's edit).

```
 PHASE 0 — NOW, no collision: a worktree cut from `main` (name: .wt-trk0). New files only (about 32 to 39 days, invisible to any user).
   T-0a  unit/batch/cell/rules engines        ║  T-5a  scan detector, resolver, scale, GS1
   T-6a  barcode encoders                     ║  T-6b  label model, layout, templates, rules, labelLang.cjs
   T-7a (pure half)  electron/labelBridge.cjs ║  fixtures: scripts/fixtures/trackingBook.ts, vectors
   T-6s  the one-day printed spike (the owner's printers; no code)   <- run it NOW: it decides what T-6c promises
     └─ merges into main as inert files (nothing imports them); no MCP re-lock (no locked file touched)
 ── GATE: CF-9a committed, reviewed and green on a rebased main (written, uncommitted, in .wt-cf today); CF-0 and CF-6a landed; Back merged ──
 PHASE 1 — a NEW worktree (.wt-trk) cut from `cf-back-nav` after that gate, rebased onto main at the end
   T-0b (needs CF-9a)  →  T-1 (needs CF-1)  ║  VoucherScreen.tsx, one writer at a time:  Back overlays -> CF-2 -> CF-3 -> T-2a -> CF-9c -> T-2b
   then T-3  →  T-4 (needs CF-4a's checklist, CF-2's print block)
 PHASE 2 — alongside or after
   T-9a (with CF-6a's deploy window, or the next one)  ║  T-6c (needs CF-4a's checklist and T-6s)  ║  T-5b (keys.ts after Back)  ║  T-8 (after CF-10)  →  T-9b
 PHASE 3 — needs the owner or the network
   T-7a (hardware: a TSC or Zebra, an Electron binary, Windows 10 and 11; electron/main and preload after or before Back's B-6)  →  T-7b   ║   T-10 (TLS)
```

**Which slices are pure new files and can start now:** T-0a, T-5a, T-6a, T-6b, the pure half of T-7a, the vectors and fixtures; their only shared dependency is `src/trackTypes.ts` (new, types only), which is why the types live there and `types.ts` receives only re-export and optional-member lines in T-0b. **Which wait, and for what:** T-0b for CF-9a **committed, reviewed and green on a rebased `main`**; T-1 for CF-1; **T-2a for CF-3 on `VoucherScreen.tsx` (question 22), T-2b and T-5b's item field for CF-9c**; T-4 for CF-4a's single registration of the checklist and CF-2's print edits; T-8 for CF-10; T-9 for CF-6a's backend restructure (**serial detection belongs in the restructured `pushRun.js` shape, behind the same fake-client test seam**, not in the main tree's 496-line `sync.js`); T-6c for the checklist. **The rebase at the end:** `.wt-trk` is rebased onto `main` once the CF branch has been rebased and merged; the Phase 0 files rebase trivially (new files); `types.ts`, `inventory.ts`, `VoucherScreen.tsx` are where conflicts could occur and are why T-0b, T-2a, T-2b and T-3 are written **against CF's reviewed tip, not against `main` and not against the WIP**.

**CF-9a has been written** (in the uncommitted `.wt-cf` tree, as of the review pass): **Appendix R's fallback is now the plan**: T-0b adds `cells` beside `byVariant`, re-points `byVariant`'s update to the projection for a batch-tracked item, and **CF's own `customfields-stock` suite is the oracle**: it must pass unchanged. What T-0b needs from CF-9a is its **review and merge**, not a change to it; **if the CF lane changes CF-9a's shape while reviewing it, T-0b is written against the reviewed shape**, which is why T-0b is gated and not started against the WIP.

### 15.4 The "stock dimension" mechanism (so the walk is built once)

CF-9c needs: a cell drawn only for tracked items, a stock hint in the picker, a negative-position warning that names the position, a Stock Journal row that starts from the source row's position, a Physical Stock line that counts exactly the position it names. This work needs the same machinery with a different editor and cardinality [R-aud 4.2 item 5]. **One hook, `ui/vch/useStockDims.ts`, answers per row which stops the walk makes and which cells are drawn** (`{ stops: ('cf:<fid>' | 'alloc')[], cells: ... }`); **T-2a creates it with the `'alloc'` stop after Quantity, and CF-9c then adopts it for the variant cell** (the reverse of the first text, which had CF-9c implement the variant through a hook and T-2 add `'alloc'` later: that order made tracking wait for CF-9c; the hook's one job is unchanged). A row tracked by both a batch and a variant writes **both** from the panel: choosing a cell in the panel sets the line's batch **and** its variant (the variant field then shows pre-filled at CF's Amount step, and editing it clears the allocation), so the proposal never has to guess a variant before it has been typed.

### 15.5 The MCP server (T29)

Four couplings [R-aud 4.5]: (1) `mcp/engine-lock.json` pins 52 `src/` files; every slice that edits a locked file lists the **drift check and, once the reconciliation suites pass, the re-lock** in its exit gate; the re-lock is the last step of T-0b, T-3 and T-4. (2) `mcp/test/fields.test.ts` fails on a key of a loaded interface that is in neither `keep` nor `drop`: **`StockItem.track` and `barcodes` and `batchRecs`: keep** (read tools may show a batch's status and a tracking mode); `InventoryLine.serials`: **keep**, projected to ids only (the IMEI list is what an auditor's AI tool needs), capped at 200 per line in the projection with a "+N more"; `mrp`, `salePrice`: keep; `billsNote`, `missing`: keep; **`InventoryLine.serialKind` and `eod`: keep**; `Voucher.trackOverrides`: keep; `Godown.kind`, the feature keys: keep; **`ItemTracking.strict`, `StockItem.manufacturer` and `schedule`: keep** (all inside the `track` object or beside it; the classifier names each new key of a loaded interface). **The MCP's locked engine also reads a `Specific` item as Moving Avg. and a tracked count without `eod`** (it is an older reader of both until re-locked): `stock_report` says so on such an item. (3) The write planner refuses a batch item today (`buildVoucher.ts`) and the tool description says so; **it refuses every tracked item in the same release** (a serial item must not be drafted with no serial), with the reason; supporting a tracked draft is a later, deliberate step. (4) `stock_report` reads `stockPositions` with no options: a count group is read by the locked copy of the engine, so the MCP bundle is an **older reader** of a count group until re-locked (the CF release note says the same); a batch or serial reading is an optional later tool.

---

**Source-scanning assertions** (the walk table, the form order, `main.cjs` and `preload.cjs`) each assert they **scanned something** (Phase 28 gotcha). **Mutation runner:** the new suites import `backend/src/unitChain.js` and `electron/labelLang.cjs`; the runner already copies `backend/src`, `electron`, `scripts/perf` and `src` [C `mutations/run.mjs`].

### 16.2 The identity proof, in plain words

For a company that never switches `features.tracking` on (and for every untracked item of one that does): the same outputs from every stock engine, the same walk, the same item form, no new key in any saved document, no listener, no new column, no new menu row (the four rows of 9 are gated on the feature), no new Go To answer **ranking** (new commands are `late`, the Phase 27 rule: a golden capture of the 103 Go To queries is compared [C `scripts/shots/golden/goto.capture.mjs`]), the sweep of every existing screen byte-identical in both schemes. This is the single most important gate of T-0b, T-1, T-2 and T-5b.

### 16.3 Performance rows (added to `scripts/perf`, reference rows first, gated once measured)

`trk.cells`, `trk.units`, `trk.units.heap`, `trk.accept`, `trk.specific`, `trk.paste10k`, `trk.search` (a unit lookup), `trk.fefo` (a proposal over 500 cells), `lbl.layout` (a label with a Code 128 and a QR in under 2 ms), `scan.burst` (the harness's key feed, **with an item-count dimension: 80 items, 5,000 and a 30,000-item master, against 120,000 vouchers, since the item popup refilters every item per render**), **`trk.order`** (rule W over 250,000 serial events: the per-unit search is at most 8 events per unit-day, linear in events), **`trk.costledger`** (`unitCostLedger` over the same events). A perf mirror is a copy of source text and goes stale when code moves (Phase 29): rows that mirror a literal are re-pointed in the same change.

### 16.4 Hostile and degenerate inputs (each a named test)

A 10,000-serial paste and a 100,000-row paste (refused whole); duplicate and malformed IMEIs; **Unicode digits** (Arabic-Indic, Devanagari, fullwidth) in an IMEI; right-to-left marks, zero-width and NUL inside an id; a 1 MB single cell; an id of `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString`; a CSV cell starting `=`, `+`, `-`, `@` in a label export; an IMEI with a leading zero through Excel; an `.xlsx` of 200,000 rows ending at column IV; a 5,001-unit line; a voucher of 10,001 units; a batch name of 5,000 characters; `Ctrl+Alt` presses mid-scan; **scanner bursts**: 3 ms spacing, 1,000 in a row, a burst with a `GS` and with a `Ctrl+]`, a burst during IME composition, a burst whose terminator is Tab, a burst arriving while the Accept bar is up, a half burst with no terminator, **an irregular burst (gaps of 10, 10, 150, 10 ms) whose trailing Enter reaches the Accept bar and Narration (the backstops of 14.3 must hold)**, **a burst with Caps Lock on and off**, **a burst at 3 ms against a 30,000-item master and 120,000 vouchers (at most two state changes)**, **Space and a letter pressed on every control kind (button, checkbox, radio, select, rail row, Settings choice group, the Lock button) with the layer on**, **20 dual-SIM boxes scanned twice each with Quantity blank**, **two windows of one profile selling one IMEI**, **a 5,000-row voucher with `id2` pushed to the server in one statement**, **a quantity of 356938035643809 typed into a serial row**, a batch name holding U+0001; Code 128 text with a non-Latin character (refused); an EAN-13 with a bad check digit typed and scanned; a label template with a 40-character barcode field in 20 mm.

### 16.5 Fixtures, the second sample company, recipes

- **`scripts/fixtures/trackingBook.ts`** (pure, deterministic, seeded like `scripts/perf/synth.ts`): a company with a pharmacy (50 batch items, 4 batches each, expiries spread across 2026 to 2028, two MRPs for some), a mobile shop (30 models, 4,000 IMEIs, dual SIM, used units with stamps, returns, a DOA), a distributor with transit and quarantine godowns, a manufacturing voucher, a Physical Stock with an old per-batch count (the 12 to 7 history), a legacy batch item, and a 50,000-voucher variant with 250,000 serial events. **`core/demoTracking.ts`** builds a small public version of it as the **second sample company** ("Mobile and pharma sample"), offered on the welcome screen by a key of its own, so a prospect can try the features and the recipes can reach them; **`core/demo.ts` is not edited** (its stock goldens and `scripts/assert/perf.assert.ts` fingerprints are pinned [R-aud 3.17]). `scripts/shots/app.mjs` gains `openTrackingSample(page)` (the welcome key, as `openSample` presses `T`) and the harness `page` gains `burst(text, gapMs)`, which sends synthetic key events at a chosen spacing (the recipes drive real key presses, **never Accept on a live company**, the existing rule).
- **Recipes** (`scripts/shots/recipes/tracking-*.mjs`, each in light and dark, compact and comfortable, and at 150 % text, with zero console errors): `trk-item-form`, `trk-opening-cells`, `trk-purchase-serial` (scan 20 units with two unscannable: "2 pending"), `trk-purchase-dual-sim` (20 boxes, two barcodes each: 20 units, never 40), `trk-two-tabs` (a second window sold the IMEI a moment ago), `trk-soft-upgrade` (an upgraded item with a negative cell warns and sells), `trk-purchase-batch`, `trk-sale-fefo` (the proposal, an expired batch refused, an override with a reason), `trk-return-by-unit` (a different unit of the same model refused), `trk-transfer`, `trk-physical-batch`, `trk-stocktake-scan` (20 phones, 18 scanned: the 2 missing need reasons), `trk-cancel-release`, `trk-manufacture-batch`, `trk-batch-trace` (a recall list and Hold), `trk-unit-history`, `trk-reports-*` (every report of 9), `trk-labels-preview`, `trk-labels-sheet`, `trk-labels-roll`, `trk-scan-setup`, `trk-scan-burst`, and flows with the mock cloud (`scripts/shots/flows/_mockCloud.mjs`): `trk-offline-clash` (two browser contexts, one offline, the same IMEI) and `trk-unit-clash-resolve`. The recipes that exercise every existing voucher screen are **unchanged and must pass with the feature off**; the sweep grows by the new screens only.
- **Owner's acceptance list** (how the owner can tell it is better [R-bst 10.5], adopted as the closing scenarios): receive 40 phones by scanning with two unscannable boxes and the bill books today with "2 pending identities"; sell one IMEI on two devices, one offline, and the second is caught at sync and shown on the sync report; return a different phone of the same model and it is refused naming the unit that was sold; bill a pharmacy counter an item with four batches and the soonest valid expiry is pre-selected while an expired one cannot be billed without a reason; mark a batch recalled and it cannot be sold while one screen lists who bought it; count 20 phones by scanning 18 and the two missing need a reason before the books move; import a company whose phones are IMEI-named batches and be offered "convert 312 batches of one to serial units"; print one sticker per unit after a purchase on the owner's printer and scan it back into the sale.

## 17. Questions that genuinely need the owner (each with the default taken so work can proceed)

| # | Question | Default taken |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Which label printers and scanners do your customers already own?** (the language, the sizes and the first hardware test follow from it) | Ask ten. Until then assume 203 dpi desk printers (TSC TE244 and TTP-244 Pro, TVS LP 46 Neo, Zebra ZD220) and keyboard-wedge scanners. The driver path (P1, P2) first, then **raw TSPL** (the one language the research read in full), **ZPL second and only after Zebra's graphic-field command has been read and proven on a printer**; the TVS and Godex G500 command languages are unconfirmed [R-lab 2.2] |
| 2 | **Which label sizes?** | 50 x 25, 38 x 25 and 100 x 50 rolls, 75 x 50 and 60 x 40 for dispense and pre-pack, 40 x 30 (and 25 x 15) for jewellery, and an A4 sheet with a free grid (21, 24, 40, 44, 65-up presets). The library's sizes are starting points to confirm with five shops per vertical on real stock |
| 3 | **Must serial uniqueness be hard-blocking, or detect-and-flag when a device is offline?** | **Both, each where it can be true:** hard block in one open window (always; **per window, not per device**, 10.1) and between two online devices **only if the optional slice T-9b is built** (the reserve at Accept); **detect and flag** when a device is offline, because nothing else is possible (10.1). The company switch `trackingOffline` (default `allow`; `block` stops a synced company's offline devices selling serial units) is the owner's lever |
| 4 | **Which verticals first?** | The vertical research's order: pharma (retail and wholesale), mobile and electronics, FMCG and trading distribution, garments (CF's variants plus barcodes and a hang tag), then grocery with a scale profile, manufacturers and packers, hardware and appliances, jewellery [R-vert 5.5]. T-0 to T-4 serve the first four at once; the ranking is judgement, and the top 100 customers by vertical would replace it with counts |
| 5 | **What hardware can you test with?** | Ask for: a USB wedge scanner and a Bluetooth one (one that can be set to serial mode), **with the make and model of each and whether it reads only bar codes (a 1-D laser or CCD) or also QR and Data Matrix (a 2-D imager), because a 1-D scanner cannot read the QR phone sticker or a drug pack's QR (T35)**, a 203 dpi TSC and a Zebra desk printer and a Bluetooth label printer, an Android phone, a roll each of 50 x 25, 38 x 25 and 100 x 50 and a jewellery tag, a **real label printed by a customer's weighing scale**, and a packaged Electron build on Windows and macOS. Nothing in 13.6 and 14.7 has run on any of it |
| 6 | **Specific (per-unit) costing for serial items: offered, defaulted, or never?** (**the default was reversed by the second review**) | **Offered as an opt-in** for every serial item, behind an AS 2 caution line and an explicit confirmation; **the Mobile preset defaults to Avg. Cost and keeps the per-unit cost as data** (AS 2 para 15 calls specific identification inappropriate for large numbers of interchangeable items, and handsets of one model are interchangeable [W]); `Specific` may be the default only where units are genuinely unique (a **jewellery** piece, segregated project stock); **the owner's chartered accountant confirms** whether any handset line can use it and what a used-phone margin-scheme invoice needs; the margin invoice is **not built here** [R-bst 3.1.4] |
| 7 | **An expired batch at sale: block, warn or allow?** | **Block with an Inventory-right override and a reason** for a new item; an Upgrade of a legacy batch item keeps `allow` (yesterday's habits) until its owner tightens it; return, write-off and count are never blocked (R6, R11) |
| 8 | **Build the Delivery Note / Receipt Note link (`billsNote`, T14)?** | Yes, in T-3, off until used. It is the one deliberate extra change to `stockMovements`; a company that does not use delivery notes with tracked goods never sees it, and without it a unit delivered and then invoiced is issued twice [R-aud 3.2.3] |
| 9 | **Custom label templates shared across counters (the one optional Dexie bump)?** | Yes at T-7b, as `labelTemplates` (additive `version(9)`); if not, device-local templates with a file export and import. The 14-template library is code either way |
| 10 | **Data Matrix: write the encoder or load bwip-js lazily?** | Write it (about 400 lines, T-6d, optional): a 14x14 symbol carries a 15-digit IMEI in 4 to 6 mm and GS1 data with FNC1; the QR unit sticker already works without it |
| 11 | **Batch identity includes the printed MRP?** (one batch number at two MRPs is two cells) | Yes: what the counter sees is what is on the shelf, and it keeps the unlawful-above-MRP check exact. The pharmacy research asked for uniqueness per `(item, batch number)` with a dated revision [R-vert 6.2] |
| 12 | **Mint shop-own EAN-13 codes for items (the 040 to 049 block, never 890)?** | Yes, needs the Masters right, collision-checked against the registry; 890 is GS1 India's alone [R-lab 1.2] |
| 13 | **E-invoice: `WrDt`, an expiry above 2029, serial and barcode fields: test against the IRP sandbox?** | `WrDt` not sent, `ExpDt` above 2029 omitted with a visible note, `PrdSlNo` only on the opt-in expansion; run one sandbox invoice with a 2030 expiry, a serial and a barcode when IRP sandbox access is available [R-vert 3.2] |
| 14 | **A new right for reprinting labels or editing unit warranty?** | No new right in v1: Inventory for the reprint and the warranty edit (with a reason), Administration to release a recall and override a duplicate unit (12) |
| 15 | **A company-wide tracking policy block ("all new items expire-block")?** | Not in v1: the item and the presets carry every knob; if wanted it rides CF-14's `entryPolicy` object as a sibling section, not a new mechanism |
| 16 | **When will the live site have TLS?** (the camera and WebUSB printing wait for it) | T-10 waits; the desktop app and a wedge scanner cover the counter meanwhile [R-aud 1 item 11] |
| 17 | **Three real TallyPrime exports from partners** (the CF-10 gate, and the first sight of how batch dates and "IMEI as a batch" look in a real file) | T-8's Tally half does not ship before them [R-aud 3.7] |
| 18 | **Teach a maker's QR layout?** (the top-300 drug packs carry the maker's own string; a scan fills a batch only when it parses) | Later slice; v1 types the batch and expiry and keeps the raw scan as a reference [R-bst 3.2] |
| 19 | **Old Physical Stock counts made one line per batch (12 became 7) in existing books: restate or leave?** | **Leave**: the Data Check lists them; a new count made now is a count group [R-aud 4.2 item 2] |
| 20 | **Is the opt-in scan layer worth the 35 ms hold of a bare key on a scanner counter?** | Yes, per device, off by default; a counter with a scanner turns it on in Settings, Devices, Scanner and the setup scan measures its own numbers |
| 21 | **CF-9a is now written (uncommitted, unreviewed, in `.wt-cf`): accept Appendix R's fallback, i.e. tracking widens CF's shipped `byVariant` shape rather than CF changing it first?** | Yes. T-0b is gated on CF-9a being committed, reviewed and green on a rebased `main`; the cost is the re-point of the variant update in `stockPositions` and of the readers Appendix R names, with CF's own `customfields-stock` suite as the oracle. The alternative (ask the CF lane to change CF-9a now) costs the CF lane a rework of code it has just written |
| 22 | **Who goes first on `VoucherScreen.tsx`: tracking's thin T-2a, or the custom-fields chain?** | **T-2a between CF-3 and CF-9c** (CF-2 and CF-3 are not delayed; CF-9c waits 9 to 11 days and adopts `useStockDims`). Alternatives with their cost (15.3): T-2a ahead of CF-2 (a voucher 16 to 19 days of CF work earlier, CF-2 held 9 to 11 days and rebased) or behind CF-9c (the first text's order: tracking reaches a voucher last) |
| 23 | **May the editor refuse to switch tracking on in a synced company while a device seen in the last 30 days cannot record units?** (10.5) | Yes: refuse, name the device and its last-seen date, and say to update it; a device unseen for 30 days no longer counts. An old build still cannot be forced to comply, which the release note says |
| 24 | **Split Company Data: carry per-unit history of units SOLD before the cut (so a warranty claim or a return finds its sale), or accept the limit?** | **Accept the limit in v1**: the later company knows only units still in stock; R5 warns rather than blocks for an unknown unit; Unit History and Warranty say the history is in the earlier company; the split screen offers an Excel list of units sold with warranty still running. A `unitStubs` carry needs a synced home for tens of thousands of rows and is a later slice if the first split customer needs it (4.6) |
| 25 | **Bundle a Devanagari font for labels, or use the PC's own?** | The PC's own face, **named on the test label**, and Devanagari held to 2.5 to 3.0 mm; bundle one later if customers print Hindi names. The bundled IBM Plex is Latin only (13.3) |
| 26 | **Model the prescription register (prescriber, patient, the Schedule H1 register, the pharmacist's signature) so an invoice can be the Rule 65(3) record?** | **Not in v1.** v1 adds the manufacturer and the schedule to the item and prints the manufacturer on a scheduled drug's batch line; the screens and section 18 say plainly that **the invoice is not the Rule 65(3) record**. The register needs capture at the counter and its own design; ask the pharmacy customers whether they use the register or the memo-book option (65(3)(2)) |
| 27 | **Who runs the one-day printed spike (T-6s), on which printers?** | The owner supplies a **TSC, a TVS and a Zebra desk printer**, a **Windows 10 and a Windows 11 PC**, one 50 x 25 roll and the packaged Electron build; the result decides the browser-print settings shown on the Print Labels screen and whether the label half of the headline is promised without T-7a |
| 28 | **Is a Physical Stock count "as at the end of the date" the right reading for your shops?** (4.2) | Yes: a tracked item's count is the close of its date and is applied after that day's other movements; a count taken before the day's trading is dated the previous day. Old counts are read as they always were |
| 29 | **The MRP wording: keep "MRP Rs. 79.00 (incl. of all taxes)", or spell it out as "Maximum Retail Price (inclusive of all taxes)"?** (review 2026-10-24: the Rules' abbreviated illustrations are struck out of the consolidated book, so the short form is this app's own wording, not prescribed text; 13.8, `labelModel.ts`) | **Keep the short form as the app's own fixed wording**, say so in the warning, and offer the spelled-out form as a full-form option for a shop or adviser that wants the Rules' words |
| 30 | **The shortest bars the label engine draws without a warning, and the GS1-128 limit of 48 data characters: confirm against the standards?** (`labelRules.ts` `MIN_BAR_HEIGHT_MM` is 5 mm [J]; the figure quoted for Code 39 and Code 128, the greater of 15 % of the symbol length and 6.35 mm, would warn on the 38 x 25 shelf label's 6.1 mm of bars; `gs1build.ts` refuses above 48 data characters counting every separator) | Keep 5 mm and the strict count until someone reads the General Specifications and the two symbology standards; the first printed spike (T-6s) tests the 38 x 25 label's bars with the shop's own scanner |

## 18. What this specification did not verify (read this before relying on any claim)

1. **No hardware.** No scanner, label printer, scale or phone. Every statement about how a wedge scanner types (its speed, terminator, layout, group separator, Alt-keypad mode), how a driver resamples bars, how a printer reports status, how Bluetooth pairs, and what a thermal-transfer ribbon is needed for is from a manual, a vendor page or a forum summary and is **a hypothesis for the owner's own hardware to test** (14.7, 13.6, section 17 item 5).
2. **Nothing was run.** No suite, build, screenshot or perf run (the trees belong to other work). The probes quoted as **[P]** are the audit's. **Required first tests in a real browser:** that a `stopPropagation()` in a `window` capture-phase `keydown` listener stops the same window's bubble-phase dispatcher (the DOM event model says so; the whole scan layer rests on it); that holding and replaying a bare key through an exported `dispatchKey` reaches the handlers intact; that restoring an `<input>`'s text at a terminator under React's controlled inputs behaves (the native value setter plus an `input` event is the known route); **that `KeyboardEvent.timeStamp` is unaffected by a main thread blocked for 66 ms per key (the clock of 14.2) and that a scan target uncontrolled during a run coalesces a 13-character burst into at most two state changes (14.3)**; that a window-capture hold is correctly **skipped** for a focused button, a rail row and a Settings control (the narrow bare-key definition). The review pass could not run a local script in the browser pane, so the capture-phase claim is still the DOM event model, not an observation.
3. **Electron 31 was not run.** `webContents.print` does not state the units of its `pageSize` object or whether a CSS `@page` size is honoured [W]; the Phase 29 PDF and share bridge **has never run on a real Electron binary** [R-aud 4.1], so the label bridge's first real run is part of T-7a's gate. The ZPL graphic-field command (`^GF`) was not read, and the TSPL `BITMAP` command's **bit polarity was reported not stated** in the TSC manual pages the review lens read (so "prove it on a real printer" is the rule, not a formality); the same lens reported the `<ESC>!?` status-byte table as matching 13.6 (**the architect pass did not re-read the TSC manual: both are the review lens's report**). **The bundled PowerShell RAW helper has never run**, and whether a given shop PC's policy or antivirus lets it run is unknown (it is part of T-7a's gate). **The browser-dialog label path (P1) is unproven** until the one-day spike T-6s has run on real printers.
4. **Dexie and an older tab.** Dexie 4.4.4 retries a `VersionError` with no version (read in `node_modules/dexie/dist/dexie.js`), which tolerates a newer database; an older tab was not exercised in a browser.
5. **The CF engine is written but uncommitted, unreviewed and ungated.** The first draft said it was not written; by the review pass `variantCore.ts`, `stockByField.ts` and the `inventory.ts` edits exist in the worktree's working tree (file times 06:33 to 06:40 on 2026-10-23, on top of `f446674`, "unreviewed"). Every statement about CF-9 is about that code as read once and about the design text (AD.3); **nothing of it was run here**. Re-read AD.3 and the code before T-0b and re-check Appendix R against whatever has been reviewed and merged.
6. **No Postgres.** The server design (`unit_events`, the push-lock writes, the reserve) is reasoned from reading `sync.js`, `conflict.js`, `numbering.js` and the migrations; `loadHistories` and the advisory locks cannot be unit-tested without a database (the CF plan says the same). The row size (about 150 bytes) and the 5,000,000-row cap are estimates.
7. **Statutory facts: five were re-read at source in the review pass [W]; the rest are carried from the research documents with their tags.** **Read at source:** Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules 2011 **Rule 7(2), 7(3), 7(5) and Table-I** (the Department's consolidated book with all amendments; **the first draft's flat 1 mm floor was the superseded old 7(3) and its empty band table was wrong: the bands are now shipped**; one residual doubt, since RESOLVED: the extracted text lost the inequality signs at the band boundaries and the first text applied the larger-height band at exactly 50, 100, 500 and 2,500 cm2; the rendered pages show A <= 50, <= 100, <= 500 and <= 2,500 as the upper bounds, so a boundary value is in the LOWER band (labelRules.ts header, item 1)); Drugs Rules 1945 **65(3), 65(4) and 94(2)** (CDSCO's consolidated file, whose edition date is not stated in the text read); FSSAI Labelling and Display Regulations **5(10), 8(1), 8(1)(a), 8(3)** (**Version-II of 29 December 2021, with the proviso operational from 1 July 2022; a later version of the compendium was not read**); GS1 Sweden's EAN-13 size guide; and AS 2 paragraphs 14 and 15 **as quoted in an ICAI Expert Advisory Committee opinion** (the standard's own PDF failed a certificate check). **Not re-read at source:** the IMEI law (a PIB advisory, not the Act), BIS hallmarking, the GST margin scheme, Cosmetics Rules and the rest of the Legal Metrology, FSSAI and Drugs Rules, which come from `batch-serial-tracking.md` and `labels-and-scanning.md` and their own lists of what was not read. GS1's General Specifications were not read at source (the fixed-length AI table is a vendor copy of Release 16 with GS1 change notices); what GS1 means by a day of `00` was not confirmed. **None of this is legal advice**; no lawyer or Legal Metrology officer was consulted, and the app never says "compliant".
8. **Competitors.** "What Busy, Marg, Zoho, Odoo do" is each vendor's own pages as the research read them; there is **no live account** of any. The premise that Busy's customers appreciate its batch management is only weakly confirmed (one page of 20 reviews mentions no batch work and one electronics seller praises serial and IMEI tracking [R-bst 2.1]).
9. **Tally.** The importer's date tags for batch allocations are from Tally's XML reference as the repository's own importer knows it and are **unverified against a live file**; whether Tally's plain Stock Journal carries a batch across a godown transfer is not stated by the pages read [R-bst 2.2].
10. **Sizes, ranks and budgets are judgement.** Every effort figure, performance budget and "about N lines" is **[J]** until measured; the 80 % rule for the Tally batch-of-one conversion, the 35 ms default gap, the 6-unit annexure threshold, the 60-character chunk and the caps of 3.1 are chosen, not derived, and are meant to be tuned on the first real data.
11. **Not modelled in v1, said plainly:** "use before" wording (cosmetics), a dated MRP history and MRP-keyed GST valuation for pan masala and tobacco (the batch MRP is the input a later valuation rule would read [R-vert 1 item 13]), **the prescription register, the Schedule H1 register and the pharmacist's signature of Drugs Rules 65(3) (the invoice is NOT the Rule 65(3) record; it needs the prescriber and patient captured at the counter and its own design, question 26)**, schemes with free quantity (`actual-billed-qty` stays missing), brand price-protection claims (an "in stock on date X" list is what the Serial Stock and Batch-wise Stock reports give), serial tracking inside a job-work chain (no chain exists), the used-device margin invoice, an online IMEI blacklist check (no public API was found; a stamp and a link-out only), per-variant barcodes and prices beyond the registry (a CF-9 sibling feature), the TLS-dependent camera and WebUSB paths; **added by the second review:** a **re-key action for `serialKind` and `mrpAt`** after use (both are locked instead; a re-key would restate history), a **shared stock-take draft** that merges two devices' scans of one godown (the screen says one counter per godown), a **per-unit history carry for units sold before a Split Company Data cut** (`unitStubs`, question 24), **macOS raw label printing** (CUPS), a **downloadable raw printer file** for a shop whose browser print fails, a **bundled Devanagari face** (the PC's own face is used and named), a **Rule 65(3) prescription register**, and **forcing an older build to record units** (detectable, not preventable, 10.5). **A sale of a physical unit by an older build stays invisible to R3 and the server until a person enters its units or counts the stock.**

## Appendix R. Reconcile note for the CF-9 lane (CF-9a is now written: read it as what T-0b widens, and what the CF lane's review should keep stable)

**Status after the review pass (2026-10-23).** This note was written when CF-9a was not yet written, as a request to adjust it first. **CF-9a has since been written in `.wt-cf`'s uncommitted tree** (unreviewed, ungated), so the request is overtaken and **the fallback at the end of this appendix is the plan of record** (T-0b widens the shipped shape; question 21 asks the owner to accept that). What the shipped code does against the items below, as read once:

| Item | As shipped in `.wt-cf` (read 2026-10-23) | What T-0b does |
|---|---|---|
| 1. one per-movement function | **Not as asked**: an ordinary line's variant effect is built inline in `stockPositions`, a count's by `countVariantMoves`, both applied by `applyVariantMoves` (beside `byGodown`, after the valuation call) | folds the three into `movementCellEffects` over `(godown, batch, variant)`; the invariant and CF's outputs unchanged |
| 2. `countDeltas` key-agnostic | **As asked**: lives in `variantCore.ts`, keyed by an opaque `VariantKey`, `held: ReadonlyMap<VariantKey, number>`, `parts: { key, qty }[]`, `wholeKey` | T-0a's `countSpread` is the same algorithm over `CellKey`; `countDeltas` becomes its variant-only wrapper; asserted equal on CF's five vectors |
| 3. `StockMove` | `parts?` (the original lines) and `variants?` shipped as designed; the merged `line` drops `udf` and `countPart` | adds `cells?` and reads each part's `batchName`, `expiryDate`, `mrp`, `serials` from `parts` |
| 4. `openingVariantMap(it)` | imported by `inventory.ts` from `openingStock.ts` | `openingCellMap` is read first when present (3.6) |
| 5. `heldVariants` | CF-9c is not written | widened to `heldCells` |
| 6. `useStockDims` | CF-9c is not written | **T-2a creates the hook** and CF-9c adopts it (15.4) |
| 8. server | `UDF_CARRIERS.vouchers.lines.keep = ['countPart']` and `CAP_KEPT = { companies: { entryPolicy } }` as expected, `SERVER_CAPS = ['udf2']` | T-9a extends `keep`, adds `CAP_KEPT.stockItems`, `trk1` and `mergeBatchRecs` through that extension point |
| key shape | `VARIANT_SEP = '\u0001'` joins the option ids of a two-field variant | **a separator-joined cell key would be ambiguous** (2.1): cell keys are length-prefixed |

**The finding in one line (as originally written):** CF-9's design already has the right shape (a resolver that is absent for every existing caller, a count group read from `countPart` in `stockMovements`, a key-agnostic `countDeltas`, `parts` kept whole, pooled costing); **the adjustment is small, costs almost nothing now and a re-key of five readers if agreed late.** The decision: **variant, batch and godown are three parts of one stock key (a cell); a serial is a unit identity under a cell, not a fourth part** (section 2.1).

What must change **before CF-9a is written** (each is a sentence in AD.3 or a naming choice in the new files; none changes a public name another CF slice reads):

1. **AD.3.3, row `stockPositions`.** Add: "the variant map is a **projection** of the cell map when the item is also tracked by batch; `byVariant`'s shape and every consumer (`CF-5`, `CF-9b`, `fieldPlStock`, `stockFlow`) are unchanged." Write the per-movement variant effect as **one pure function `movementVariantEffects(m, ctx) -> { key, dQty }[]`** in `core/stockByField.ts` (an ordinary line: one effect; a count: `countDeltas`), called once per movement beside the `byGodown` update; T-0b widens its key from `(variant)` to `(godown, batch, variant)` by renaming it `movementCellEffects` in `core/stockCell.ts`. Nothing else in `stockPositions` changes.
2. **`countDeltas` is already key-agnostic** (`VariantKey` is a string, `held: ReadonlyMap<VariantKey, number>`, `parts: { key: VariantKey; qty: number }[]`): **keep that, put no variant logic inside it**, and add the doc line "the function is `countSpread` of the batch and serial work with variant-only keys; both are asserted equal on the five AD.3.3 vectors". If `core/stockCell.ts` (T-0a, new file) is already merged, import `countSpread` and define `countDeltas` as the wrapper; if not, write `countDeltas` as designed and T-0b swaps it by rename.
3. **`StockMove`.** Keep `parts?` and `variants?` exactly as designed; T-0b adds `cells?: { key: CellKey; dQty: number }[]`. `parts` must keep the **whole original lines** (it does: "the original lines, voucher order"), because T-0b reads each part's `batchName`, `expiryDate`, `mrp` and `serials` from it: **do not strip fields from `parts`** (the merged `line` may drop `udf` and `countPart` as designed).
4. **`openingVariantMap(it)`** is written as a function of the **item** (it reads `openingAlloc.variants` under the two trust tests), so T-0b can make it read `openingAlloc.cells` first when present. The cells' trust rule is one test (the cells carry their own godown; 3.6).
5. **CF-9c's Physical Stock pre-fill** is written through a function `heldVariants(positions, itemId, godownId, asOn) -> { key, qty }[]` with the **key opaque**; T-2 widens it to `heldCells` (the same shape with a cell key). Keep the "untouched row moves nothing, a row deleted by hand counts 0, never a mix of part and whole rows" rules; they are the batch count's rules too.
6. **The walk.** CF-9c implements the variant cell through a hook **`ui/vch/useStockDims.ts`** (per row: which stops the walk makes and which cells are drawn) instead of inline, so the allocation stop can be added after Quantity without a second walk mechanism (15.4). **As written here originally CF-9c would create the hook and T-2 would add the stop; the review reversed it: T-2a creates the hook with the `'alloc'` stop and CF-9c adopts it, so tracking does not wait behind CF-9c.** A row tracked by both a batch and a variant: the allocation panel **sets both** the batch and the variant when a cell is chosen; CF's Amount-step variant cell then shows pre-filled.
7. **Do not add a refusal of "variant and batch on one item"** (the first vertical research suggested it [R-vert 8.1 decision 10]; with one shared key there is nothing to refuse). The editor may warn that the combination multiplies cells.
8. **CF-6a (the server).** `UDF_CARRIERS.vouchers.lines.keep` is `['countPart']`; **extend it** to `['countPart', 'serials', 'serialKind', 'eod', 'mrp', 'salePrice', 'billsNote', 'missing']` (the strong-identity pairing already covers a serial line whose older writer changed nothing and the weak step covers the lone edited line); add the capability `trk1` beside `udf2` (`SERVER_CAPS = [UDF_CAP]` in `conflict.js` gains it, as does `src/sync/caps.ts`, the push body and the pull answer; the `caps` validation of 9.3 already allows 8 names of 16 characters); add `CAP_KEPT.stockItems = { track: 'trk1', barcodes: 'trk1', batchRecs: 'trk1' }` (the table to field to capability shape that `CAP_KEPT.companies = { entryPolicy: UDF_CAP }` already has in `.wt-cf/backend/src/conflict.js`) and the pure `mergeBatchRecs` (higher `rev` per key, a hold kept against a stale clear) beside `carryUdf`; keep `histories.js`'s `need.current` read for a `stockItems` put from a capless writer. T-9a builds these **on the restructured `pushRun.js` / `histories.js` / `restWrite.js`**, so CF-6a should leave a clearly named extension point (a `carriers` table and a `mergeRules` list) rather than special-casing `udf`.
9. **Backup.** CF takes `BACKUP_SCHEMA_VERSION = 3` when custom data exists; this work takes **4** when tracking data exists (a higher number implies the lower one's semantics). `core/backupMigrate.ts` is one file: write the step as "the highest of the features present", not as a single flag.
10. **Release note.** The CF release note's "an older build reads a count group last line wins" caveat also covers a count by batch; the MCP bundle is such an older reader until its lock is re-recorded.
11. **Back spec table.** Add the dialogs of this work to the table of section 5.2 of the Back spec, each a `Box` with `onClose` and a visible Close, each with a dirty probe where it holds typed or scanned work (rules B5 and B12): the allocation panel (batch picker, batch grid, unit grid), the Opening by cell panel, the Stock Take screen, Print Labels, the label designer, the scanner setup, the Unit clash panel. A dialog that holds **scanned** work must not close on a stray Esc or click (the panel's own Discard prompt, No armed, 6.3 rule 8).

**Fallback if CF-9a has already landed when T-0b starts:** T-0b adds `cells` **beside** `byVariant` in `stockPositions`, re-points `byVariant`'s update to `byVariantOf(cells)` for an item that also has a batch, leaves it as written otherwise, and uses CF's own **`customfields-stock` suite as the oracle: it must pass unchanged** (every existing field of every position identical, the five Physical Stock vectors identical through the five readers). No CF file's public API changes. The cost of agreeing now against agreeing later is the difference between eight sentences and a re-key of `stockPositions`, `resolvedMovements`, `stockItemRows`, `core/stockAgeing.ts`, `stockFlow` and five suites.

## Appendix P. The parity board when work starts (`docs/parity-data.json`, `docs/FEATURE-PARITY.md`; this document edits neither)

- **Re-state `batches` and `expiry-reports` as partial** in the first commit that touches them: their `ours` text says "No batch field on item or line" and "No batch/expiry concept" while the status says covered, and the Batch and Expiry Summary row opens a stub [R-aud 1.2]. `covered` only when the builder **and** the review agree and each says what was not compared with a live product. `item-opening-multi` stays partial (note: opening by cell arrives in T-1); `mrp-pricing` stays missing until MRP per batch ships in T-2, then partial (one undated MRP; no dated history, no RSP valuation).
- **New `beyond` rows (never in the parity percentage):** `b-batch-cells` (batch stock per godown with a derived master), `b-fefo` (first-expiry proposal with a visible override), `b-expiry-rules` (expired block, warning days, minimum life, month-end expiry), `b-unit-engine` (serial and IMEI state machine with integrity rules), `b-unit-trace` (unit history, batch trace and recall with hold), `b-warranty`, `b-stocktake-scan`, `b-scan-layer` (opt-in scan layer with setup screen), `b-labels` (fixed library, test label, scan-back), `b-label-checks`, `b-cross-device-units` (clash detection and resolution), **`b-dual-sim` (one phone with two identifiers, pair-mode entry, alias-aware clash detection) and `b-device-caps` (devices that cannot record units are listed and block the switch-on)**. Each says BETA, built-not-deployed until its release, and what was checked only in headless Chrome on macOS.
- **The status honesty rule applies to every slice:** a row and its note must agree; the count of "250 covered, 58 partial, 73 missing" moves only by the rows above, and a row that goes down while the product improves says so in the summary (Phase 29).

## Appendix V. Worked vectors (recomputed for this document; the suites pin them)

**IMEI.** The body `49015420323751` has Luhn check digit 8, so `490154203237518` is valid; adding 1 to the **whole** number gives `490154203237519` (fails). Consecutive units step the **body** and recompute: `490154203237518`, `490154203237526`, `490154203237534`, `490154203237542`, `490154203237559` (the next, whose check digit is 9). `356938035643809` is valid. The 09 / 90 swap blind spot: bodies `49015420323709` and `49015420323790` have the same check digit (6), so a Luhn-valid IMEI can still be mistyped; scanning is the real defence.
**EAN-13 / GTIN (weights 3 and 1 from the right of the 12 data digits):** `890123456789` gives `8901234567890` (an invented number; `890123` is not an allotted prefix); `400638133393` gives `4006381333931`; the book `978938123456` gives `9789381234563`; the scale label body `210012301250` gives `2100123012503` (item 00123 at 1.250 kg under the profile `21` + 5 + 5 + check).
**Code 128, the IMEI `490154203237518`:** Set C for the first 14 digits, a switch to Set B for the last: values `105` (start C), `49`, `1`, `54`, `20`, `32`, `37`, `51`, `100` (to Set B), `24` (the digit 8), check `93`, stop `106` (the check is `(105 + 49x1 + 1x2 + 54x3 + 20x4 + 32x5 + 37x6 + 51x7 + 100x8 + 24x9) mod 103 = 2,153 mod 103 = 93`). 12 symbols, 134 modules; with quiet zones 154 modules, **38.5 mm at 2 dots on a 203 dpi printer, which does not fit a 38 mm label and fits a 50 mm one** [R-lab 1.7].
**Expiry.** `08/27` is `2027-08-31`; the batch is saleable **on** 2027-08-31 and expired from 2027-09-01; a voucher dated 2026-10-23 sees 312 days left and, with warning days `[90, 60, 30]`, no tier yet; one dated 2027-07-10 sees 52 days left and the 60-day tier.
**FEFO.** Cells at Main on 2026-10-23, need 30, `minLifeDays` 90, `expiredSale: block`: B-1990 (exp 2026-03-31, 6) **expired, excluded**; B-2102 (exp 2026-12-31, 10, 69 days left) **short-dated, last and not proposed**; B-2031 (exp 2027-08-31, 18) first; B-2044 (exp 2027-11-30, 40) second: picks B-2031 18 and B-2044 12, `short` 0. Need 70: B-2031 18, B-2044 40, then B-2102 10 (flagged short-dated) because the valid cells cannot cover it, `short` 2 (the negative-cell rule applies).
**Count spread.** B1 5 and B2 7 held at one godown (the item 12): parts B1 5 and B2 7 give an item delta 0 and cell deltas 0 and 0 (the item stays 12; the old one-line-per-batch count made it **7**); parts B1 5 alone give item delta -7, B1 0, B2 -7 (the item 5, B2 0); a blank whole-item count of 12 moves nothing. With keys `(batch, variant)` the same arithmetic over a longer key.
**Unit chain** (IMEI A; Main is a normal godown; Returns is a quarantine godown): PV1 on 5-Oct receives A: in stock at Main. SV7 on 9-Oct sells A to Raju: sold. **SV9 on 9-Oct (a later number) sells A to Meena: refused, naming SV7 (`soldTwice`).** CN2 returns A from **Meena**: refused (`notSoldToParty`: sold to Raju); from **Raju**, received at Returns: **Held (Returned)**; a Stock Journal from Returns to Main: in stock. Cancelling SV7 puts A back in stock at Main (the shell has no lines). Altering PV1 to remove A after SV7 exists: refused, listing SV7 as the dependant. A purchase of A dated **after** SV7: SV7 is flagged `sold before received` (R3's override case). A sale naming an unknown IMEI at a godown with 3 pending slots **adopts** one; with none it is refused.
**GS1.** `0108901234567890` `17270331` `10B2410A` (no separator needed: the batch is last) reads GTIN `08901234567890`, expiry `2027-03-31`, batch `B2410A`; adding a serial after the batch needs the separator: `10B2410A<GS>21SN001`; the same text without the separator is **refused** (14.4). An unknown AI is rejected with the raw text shown.
**MRP ceiling.** Rate 21.43 tax-exclusive at 5 % GST on a pack whose MRP is 22.50: `21.43 x 1.05 = 22.5015`, above the MRP by 0.0015, inside the 0.005 tolerance: no finding; rate 21.60 gives 22.68: a finding naming both amounts.

*The vectors below were added by the second review; each is pinned by the suite named in its heading, and each was recomputed for this pass (Luhn, sizes and dot arithmetic by script).*

**Rule W, same-day order across voucher types (suite `trackunit`; every case is also run with the vouchers presented in every order).** (a) Unit A is not held before 12-Oct; **PV 9** (in), **SV 14** (out to Raju) and **CN 1** (in, a return from Raju) are all dated 12-Oct. Number order would read CN 1, PV 9, SV 14 = `in, in, out`: `receivedTwice`, status "sold", and a resale refused. Rule W: from not-held the only valid first event is PV 9 (CN 1 is a return and no earlier `out` to Raju exists), then SV 14, then CN 1 (the last `out` was to Raju) = `in, out, in`: **A is in stock and a same-day resale SV 15 is accepted**. (b) **PV 20** (in) and **SV 3** (out) on one date, A not held before: number order gives `notInStock`; rule W gives PV 20, SV 3. (c) A held; **SV 7** (out, Raju), **CN 2** (in, a return from Raju), **SV 9** (out, Meena) on one date: arranged SV 7, CN 2, SV 9; **with CN 2 returning from Meena** the search tries SV 7 first, finds CN 2 cannot follow (the last `out` was to Raju), backtracks and arranges SV 9, CN 2, SV 7; **with CN 2 returning from Kavita, to whom A was never sold,** no arrangement is valid: number order (CN 2, SV 7, SV 9) is used and `notSoldToParty` is reported (with a `receivedTwice`, since A is held when the return arrives). (d) A held; SV 7 (Raju) and SV 9 (Meena) on one date: no valid arrangement, number order, `soldTwice` naming SV 7. (e) **A count is the close of its date:** an item holds 10 at the start of 31-Mar, SV 40, 41 and 42 sell one each the same day, and a closing Physical Stock numbered **3** counts 7. Counted-first (number order, no `eod`): delta 7 - 10 = -3, then the sales -3: **item 4**. With `eod`: the sales first (10 to 7), the count 7: delta 0: **item 7**.

**Pending identities, adoption and transfer (suite `trackunit`).** PV 5 on 5-Oct books 20 phones with 14 IMEIs: one slot of 6 dated 5-Oct. SV 12 on 9-Oct sells an unknown IMEI X: it adopts the slot, **effective pending 5**. A sale of another unknown IMEI Y dated **4-Oct**: no slot dated on or before it, `notInStock` (the R3 override "bill not yet entered"). The clerk alters PV 5 to list 6 more IMEIs, X among them: 20 serials, **pending 0**, the adoption **superseded**, the chain `in(PV 5), out(SV 12)`; had she listed only 3 others (17 serials) the slot is 3 less the one still-adopted unit = **2** truly unidentified. A Stock Journal moves 4 of the 6 pending from Main to Shop: Shop holds 4 slots dated 5-Oct, Main 2; a Shop sale dated 9-Oct adopts one: Shop pending 3.

**`Specific` costs (suite `trackcost`).** IMEI A bought on PV 5 at **10,450** (the row's cost) and sold on SV 12 at 14,000: value out 10,450, margin 3,550. **Credit Note 2 returns A from the same buyer: value in 10,450, not 14,000**; the item's value rises by 10,450 and a resale at 14,000 shows margin 3,550 again (the first text booked the return at 14,000: stock overstated by 3,550). **Opening:** `openingValue` 31,350 over 3 rows, two with explicit costs 10,000 and 10,500: the third row costs **10,850** (31,350 - 10,000 - 10,500); if all three state costs summing to 31,000 the 350 difference sits in the pool and costs plus pool equal 31,350. **Conversion:** IMEI B (cost 12,000) issued from item 64 GB and received on item 128 GB by IMEI: the first falls 12,000, the second rises 12,000. **A count finds** unit C with no history on an item worth 90,000 over 9 units: C enters at the pool average **10,000**. **Order independence:** PV 20 buying A at 10,450 and SV 3 selling A on one date give A an end-of-date value of 0 in either processing order.

**Dual-SIM (suites `trackentry`, `tracksync`).** The box carries `356938035643809` and `356938035643817`: both Luhn-valid (check digits 9 and 7), the same type code `35693803`, bodies `35693803564380` and `35693803564381` differing by **1**. Pair mode makes them one unit (20 boxes scanned twice each give 20, never 40); with pair mode off the second scan raises "IMEI 2 of row 1?". A sale whose box was scanned as `...817` stores `356938035643809`. **Server:** the purchase row writes events for both identifiers with `uid = I\u0001356938035643809`; a sale on device A by `...809` and on device B by `...817`, pushed in either order, resolve to **one `uid` with two `out`s: one `duplicate-serial` row**.

**Caps Lock (suite `scaninput`).** A scanner sends the batch code `B7K2Q9X` as Shift+B, 7, Shift+K, 2, Shift+Q, 9, Shift+X. Caps Lock off: `B7K2Q9X`. Caps Lock on: the browser delivers `b7k2q9x`; with the correction every letter is `shiftKey ? upper : lower` and the run reads `B7K2Q9X`; the scale label `W00319801500` is matched with its prefix case-insensitively.

**Legal Metrology Table-I (suite `labelrules`).** Panel **40 cm2**: numerals and letters at least 1.0 mm, so a font of at least 1.43 mm (at a cap-height ratio of 0.70). Panel **120 cm2**: at least **2.5 mm**, so a font of at least 3.57 mm; a 3.0 mm font draws 2.1 mm numerals: a warning. Exactly **100 cm2** reads as the 1.5 mm band and exactly 2,500 cm2 as the 4.0 mm band (a boundary value belongs to the LOWER band: Table-I's upper bounds are inclusive, read from the rendered pages; this sentence said the larger band until the review corrected it); a moulded 120 cm2 pack needs **4.0 mm**; a 60 x 40 mm label with the area left blank is checked against its own 24 cm2, band 1.0 mm, as a lower bound.

**Dots, GS1 and sizes (suites `labellayout`, `trackcell`, `tracksync`).** 40 mm at **300 dpi** is **472** dots (11.81 per mm), not 480; a 50 x 25 mm label at 203 dpi is 400 x 200 dots = 10,000 bytes. **2 dots at 203 dpi is 0.250 mm, below GS1's 0.264 mm minimum; 3 dots is 0.375 mm.** The cell key of godown `g1`, batch `b17` and variant `Red\u0001XL` is `2:g13:b17Red\u0001XL`: two lengths are read, the rest is the variant. Serial row sizes: **25, 62 and 134 bytes** (bare id, id with id2 and cost, a sale row with a warranty entry): 5,000 rows are 125, 310 and 670 KB, and 524,288 bytes hold 8,456 rows of the middle kind. A pull page of 500 vouchers of 400 KB is 200 MB; the 4 MB page cap gives about 10 per page.

**FSSAI and the Tally example (suites `labelrules`, `trackimport`).** A 6-month product with DD/MM/YY passes (the proviso) and so does MM/YYYY; a pack of exactly **100.0 cm2** is exempt from the batch number and ingredients ("not more than 100"); one of 29 cm2 also need not carry the date of manufacture; a 5-day shelf life needs only the use-by. The Tally batch name **`Feb-654321543214321`** extracts the 15-digit run `654321543214321` (it **fails Luhn**, so it is flagged), keeps `Feb-` in the unit's `note`, and a scan of the bare number finds the unit.

## 20. Review log (2026-10-23, the second pass)

**What this is.** The second review's 42 findings (lens A: inventory, accounting and sync, 23 findings; lens B: hardware, UX and India compliance, 19), each **re-checked against the code or the primary page before it was accepted**, then applied in place (the sections it touched were rewritten, not patched, so the document stays decision-complete). **Result: all 42 reproduced in substance and were applied; seven fixes were adapted from the reviewer's wording (1, 4, 10, 11, 32, 34, 41); three findings carried a sub-claim that did not reproduce or needed correcting (23, 26, 32); none was rejected outright.** "Reproduced" names what I read; **nothing was run** (no gate, build, suite, probe in a browser, or hardware; the one exception is arithmetic, sizes, Luhn and dot counts, which were computed by script). Where a fix rests on a primary page, it was fetched in this pass [W] (section 19).

| # | Sev | Finding (short) | Reproduced? | Disposition (where) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | blocker | the chain orders same-date events by voucher number, but numbers are per type | **Yes** [C `store.ts` `sortVouchers` sorts `(date, number)`; `nextVchNo` numbers by `typeId`; `Voucher` has no time of day in `types.ts`] | Accepted, **ADAPTED**: of the reviewer's two options, **(a) per-unit validity resolution** was built as **rule W** (4.3, T31), not (b) an immutable `Voucher.at` stamp: (b) needs a new synced voucher field with an older-client carry, does nothing for existing data, and would still make a back-dated entry's real time arbitrary; and **a count is the close of its date through an `eod` line flag** read by `stockMovements` like `countPart` (4.2, 4.7), because the item-level engine also sorts by number and the count double-decremented. Cell and `Specific` value rules are judged at date boundaries; one function serves entry, save, Data Check, post-pull, server and the cost ledger; vectors (a), (b), (c), (d), (e) in Appendix V |
| 2 | major | `Specific` leaves a re-entering unit's cost undefined | **Yes** [C `inventory.ts`: `inValue = m.line.amount \|\| round2(qty * m.line.rate)`; `valuation.ts` header: a credit note enters "at the note's own value"; spec 7.3 filled price, customer and warranty, never cost] | Accepted: `unitCostLedger` and six cost rules (4.5), R30, T31 (cost resolved by the chain, order-independent); vectors for return, opening, conversion, found unit |
| 3 | major | the Mobile preset defaults `Specific` against AS 2 para 15 | **Yes** [W: ICAI's EAC opinion quotes paras 14 and 15: specific identification for items not ordinarily interchangeable or segregated for projects; inappropriate for large numbers of interchangeable items] | Accepted: Mobile preset defaults Avg. Cost with per-unit cost as data; `Specific` opt-in with a `METHODS` caution and confirmation; jewellery may default it (T6, 4.5, 11.5, Q6) |
| 4 | major | a mixed-version fleet can sell tracked items with no unit identity | **Yes** [C: no mechanism existed; `sync_device` has no `caps` column, `007_sync_conflicts.sql`; an older build has no panel or funnel by construction] | Accepted, **ADAPTED**: the server holds no item rows, so "flag a voucher from a writer lacking `trk1` that names a tracked item" is **client-side** (the post-pull scan records `unit-unrecorded`); device capabilities on the server (`sync_device.caps`), the Company features list, the switch-on refusal (30 days), `serial-line-short`; **"an old build cannot be forced" stated** (10.1, 10.5 item 7, T33, Q23) |
| 5 | major | the server cannot compute the unit key or aliases; `id2` is unaddressed | **Yes** [spec 3.1 had no kind on `InventoryLine`; 10.2 derived events "from the voucher alone"; 4.3 emitted one event per unit keyed on `id`] | Accepted: `InventoryLine.serialKind` snapshot, lock on `serialKind`, `uid` canonical key and alias map, outward rows store the primary id, two events per `id2` row, alias re-pointing on the server (3.1, 3.3, 10.2, T11); two-device IMEI 1 / IMEI 2 vector |
| 6 | major | server write-path traps (bind parameters, round trips, payload source, three writers, no rebuild) | **Yes**, each: (a) the DDL had 13 columns, so a statement stops near 5,000 rows against a 10,000-unit cap; (b) "one indexed read per touched unit key" inside `lockTenantLog` [C `db.js`]; (c) [C `.wt-cf` `pushRun.js` inserts `ins.payload` and `ins.op`]; (d) [C three `INSERT INTO change_log` sites: `sync.js`, `rest.js` x2, `scripts/seed/to-sql.mjs`; `syncconflict.assert.ts` `allInserts` counts them]; (e) no path existed | Accepted: stored payload, chunked `unnest` inserts and one set query, 50,000-event budget, `indexUnits` from every writer plus the drift rule, `rebuild-unit-events.mjs` (10.2, T-9a) |
| 7 | major | adoption and pending are half defined | **Yes** [spec 4.3 text: `pending` less "units later adopted"; R4 required `serials.length = qty` for any outward line] | Accepted: dated slots, adoption on or before the sale, explicit `in` supersedes, transfers move slots (4.3, R4, 7.5); vectors |
| 8 | major | Upgrade does not keep today's looseness | **Yes** [spec 11.4 loosened only `expiredSale`, `pick`, `mrpAt`; R10, R25 and R27 were Block; the audit's probes show negative and phantom cells exist today] | Accepted: `strict: false` makes them Warn, Tighten, exemption of pre-Upgrade cells, Data Check re-balancing suggestion (2.3, 11.4, R10, R25, R27, T32) |
| 9 | major | opening by cell ignores the split lists; the trust rule drops serials | **Yes** [C `splitCompany.ts` ~533-552 writes `alloc.godowns` and `alloc.batches` independently; `openingStock.ts` reads them as two lists; spec 3.6 and 4.6 ignored them for a tracked item] | Accepted: `openingCellMap` reads cells, then the lists (north-west-corner pairing, flagged), then Main; Opening Quantity read-only while cells exist; a mismatch is never silent (3.6, 4.6) |
| 10 | major | Split Company loses unit chains, sale parties, warranties, genealogy | **Yes** in substance [the vouchers stay in the earlier company; the later company opens each unit with a bare `in`] | Accepted, **ADAPTED**: the reviewer's second option (document the limit and make R5 Warn) plus an Excel list of units sold with warranty running; **no per-unit stub carry in v1** (no safe synced home for tens of thousands of rows): 4.3 `unknownUnit`, 4.6, R5, Q24 |
| 11 | major | "one device: everything" is false (no cross-window coordination) | **Yes** [C grep of `src/`: no `BroadcastChannel` or `liveQuery`; only `storage` listeners for UI prefs; `writeGuard` is synchronous over the in-memory store] | Accepted, **ADAPTED**: guarantee stated per open window; **`ensureFresh()` compares the outbox rows (`++seq` [C `db.ts`]) written since the window last looked with the keys it wrote itself** and reloads on a foreign one, instead of re-reading unit tails from Dexie (there is no unit index on disk: a scan of the vouchers table) or building a `BroadcastChannel` mirror (5 caller 2, 10.1, T8); two-tab recipe |
| 12 | major | resolution (b) "credit note for the later sale" leaves the chain false | **Yes** [spec 10.7 text; a credit note makes the last event an `in`] | Accepted: removed; the person chooses which invoice is wrong; replace, edit, cancel, acknowledge, dismiss (10.7) |
| 13 | minor | size claims wrong for the default row; pull page is documents, not bytes | **Yes**, computed: bare row **25 B** (5,000 = 125 KB), id + id2 + cost **62 B**, a row with `w` **134 B** (5,000 = 670 KB, over `LIMITS.versionChars` = 524,288) [C `conflict.js`]; `PULL_PAGE = 500` documents [C `sync.js`] | Accepted: caps are 5,000 rows and 400,000 serial bytes per voucher, `w` holds exceptions only, byte-bounded pull page (3.1, 3.7, R21) |
| 14 | minor | the cell key is ambiguous (CF's variant key contains U+0001) | **Yes** [C `.wt-cf` `variantCore.ts`: `VARIANT_SEP = '\u0001'`, a two-field key is `a\u0001b`] | Accepted: length-prefixed keys; batch names stripped of controls (2.1, 3.4) |
| 15 | minor | `trackOverrides` carry written as an object spread | **Yes** (JavaScript semantics: spreading arrays into an object literal gives `{0: ...}`) | Accepted: keep or union by `(rule, line, unit, at)`, always an array (10.5) |
| 16 | minor | `duplicate-serial` shares the 2,000-row cap; lease rows violate `NOT NULL`; T18 depends on optional T-9b | **Yes** [C `conflict.js` `openRows: 2000`; `sync.js` `upsertConflicts` "capped"; spec DDL `seq NOT NULL`, `line_no NOT NULL`] | Accepted: own `unitRows` cap and an aggregated row; a separate `unit_leases` table; T18 and decisions reworded to attach the near-hard promise to T-9b (10.2, 10.3, T18) |

Cost, release note and the MCP flag (4.5, 15.5) |

One residual: the extracted text lost the inequality signs at the boundaries | Accepted: five bands as data with the citation, cap-height measurement, panel-area input, larger band at a boundary [unverified glyphs] (13.8, 13.3, 18 item 7, Appendix V) |

**Not taken from the review: nothing.** **Not verified in this pass (said, not hidden):** every behaviour that needs a run, a browser, Postgres, an Electron binary or hardware: rule W's search, the cost ledger's invariant, the `eod` ordering, the coalescing `ScanInput`, the backstop thresholds (300 ms, 6 characters), the pair mode's "within 3" step, the outbox freshness check and the chunked inserts are **designs to be proven by the suites and recipes they name**, not observations; the 66 ms per render and the Bluetooth stall are figures from the review lens and the research, **not re-run**; the Legal Metrology boundary glyphs, the TSC `BITMAP` polarity and status table, the FSSAI compendium's later versions and the CDSCO file's edition date were not settled; two of the review lens's own primary fetches (the TSC manual, NIC's schema page) were **not repeated** by me, the ones I repeated are listed in section 19. A second review of these changes has **not** been done: the lenses to apply next, in order, are rule W against every reader of a same-day order (any engine this document does not name), the cost ledger against `valuation.ts`'s eight methods, the outbox freshness check against Dexie's real transaction behaviour, and the label job and PowerShell helper against a real Windows spooler.
